# Garbage Collector Tuning Guide

Audience: developers tuning CratonVM heap behaviour for a specific workload
— Quarkus boot, JFR-recorded benchmarks, low-latency request handlers,
long-running daemons, or test fixtures with tight budget envelopes.

Source: [`gc/src/`](../gc/src/) — **61 files** (43 top-level, 17 under
`gc/src/zgc/`, 1 under `gc/src/old_gen/`), **~264 k LOC**.
Re-derive rather than trusting it: `find gc/src -name '*.rs' | wc -l` and
`… | xargs cat | wc -l`. The figure has been wrong on this page several
times, which is why the command is written out here rather than the figure
alone.
The collector
dispatches through the `VmHeap` enum
([`gc/src/vm_heap.rs`](../gc/src/vm_heap.rs)); each backend is a separate
module.

> **Current Status.** Every flag, default and constant on this page
> is verified against `types/src/flags.rs`, `vm/src/config.rs`, `gc/src/zgc.rs`,
> `gc/src/gen_heap.rs`, `gc/src/g1.rs` and `types/tests/doc_numeric_claims.rs`.
> Key collector characteristics:
> - `-Xms` is honoured on all three backends (see "Heap totals");
> - 8-byte object headers;
> - `Runtime.totalMemory` / `freeMemory` and the GC MXBeans match HotSpot;
> - `-XX:+UseG1GC` default region sizing, heap boundaries and survivor sizing match HotSpot 25;
> - ZGC parallel marking is default ON;
> - All GC switches are registered in `types/src/flag_groups.rs` under `CRATONVM_GC`.

## Choosing a backend

CratonVM ships three collector backends, selected via `VmConfig::gc_algorithm`
([`vm/src/config.rs`](../vm/src/config.rs)):

> **ZGC is the default collector, not Generational.** If you pin nothing,
> this is what you run. Two things follow.
>
> **(1) Budget some extra heap, but not for the reason this block used to
> give.** Until it opened *"A non-compacting collector needs more
> headroom than a compacting one"* — which contradicted the very next block on
> this page, and the FAQ row below it, both of which say compaction has been
> **on by default **. ZGC here compacts. What is true is
> narrower: **a cycle may decline to compact** (the cost gate, the per-cycle
> JIT coverage proof, or `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0`), and a run of declined
> cycles fragments like a non-moving collector until one is admitted. How much
> headroom that wants is a property of the workload's allocation shapes rather
> than a fixed multiple, and **every OOM shape found so far under ZGC has
> turned out to be a fixable allocator defect** — a trigger asking about live
> bytes when the binding constraint was allocatable space; a TLAB reservation
> sized flat regardless of thread count — rather than an inherent cost. If
> something throws `OutOfMemoryError` under ZGC, raise `-Xmx` to get moving,
> check `moved=` on `[GC] zgc-relocate:` to see whether the cycles were
> declining — and file it.
> **(2) The escape hatch is `-XX:+UseGenerationalGC`**, available in every
> build including `--no-default-features`. `-XX:-UseZGC` does the same thing.
>
> ZGC was promoted on measured suite behaviour: across every suite with a
> per-collector sweep, it is at parity with or ahead of Generational on PASS
> count, ties or leads on HANG, and is the only backend that has never
> crashed. Read that as "roughly comparable, slightly ahead," not as a wide
> margin — the gap narrows or closes depending on which suite and run you
> look at.

> The small-object end of the arena is **compacted** at the end of a
> collection by default; `CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-relocate` restores the non-moving
> sweep byte for byte, which makes it a usable bisect for anything that looks
> like a stale-reference or relocation defect.
>
> **Parallel marking is on by default.** Unset
> `CRATONVM_ZGC_PARMARK` means `auto`: HotSpot ZGC's pause-worker ergonomic
> (60 % of the CPUs rounded up, capped at 2 % of the heap in 2 MiB pages, so a
> small heap still marks serially), or `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=<n>` when given
> (`1` = serial). Measured on the host (z2b rows, `Zr2CcParmarkShapes` and
> `Zr1MkParmarkCost`): about **2.5x lower mark pause and 2x lower wall for
> +15-20 % CPU**. `CRATONVM_ZGC_PARMARK=0` (or `CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-parmark`)
> restores the serial loop. (It was off from on a +31 % / +153 %
> pause measurement of the engine of that time, before claim-on-push, the
> persistent pool, the inline driver and the idle spill.)
>
> **What to watch.** Compaction is the configuration in which a ZGC cycle
> returns a **non-empty pointer map**, so every consumer of one runs for this
> collector: JIT frame maps, monitor tables, external root providers, native
> side tables. Those consumers are collector-agnostic and already run for the
> generational moving-young path, but "runs for another collector" is not
> "has run for this one". **A crash, a stale-reference warning or a
> silently-wrong result that disappears under `CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-relocate` is
> that class of bug**, and the flag is the bisect.

### Is it a relocation defect? The Generational bisect

The Generational backend's equivalent of `CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-relocate` is
**`CRATONVM_GC=-moving-young`** (alias `CRATONVM_NO_MOVING_YOUNG=1`). It turns
the young generation back into an in-place mark-sweep, and because the old-gen
collection that can compact (`GenerationalHeap::major_gc`) is called only from
the moving young path (`collect_garbage_inner`), it stops the old generation
compacting too. On a default run nothing in the heap then relocates. (On the
moving path the old generation compacts in two cases: always under
`CRATONVM_OLDGEN_COMPACT=1` / `CRATONVM_GC=oldgen-compact`, off by default
; and, **default ON**, for the one collection
after an old-gen allocation was refused for want of a contiguous block,
`CRATONVM_GC_OLD_OOM_COMPACT`, `=0` off. The non-moving path compacts only
under the opt-in `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_PINNED_COMPACT`, so leave that unset while
bisecting.) A crash, a stale-reference warning or a silently-wrong result
that disappears under it is a relocation defect; one that survives is not.

> **`-moving-young` is NOT a clean no-relocation lever on ZGC, and the name does
> not say so.** ZGC reads the same `gc_quiescence::moving_young_enabled` as
> one term of its relocation refusal — deliberately, because the whole
> rewritable-root contract its refusal chain leans on is maintained only while
> moving-young is on. But that term, like three of its five neighbours, is
> applied *only on a cycle with a live compiled frame*. So with
> `CRATONVM_NO_MOVING_YOUNG=1` a ZGC run **still compacts on every JIT-cold
> cycle** and refuses on every JIT-warm one, which is not "nothing in the heap
> relocates" and is not a bisect. Measured: `--nojit` plus
> `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_UNDER_PROVEN_JIT=0` gives `compaction_cycles=1` with
> the term firing and stopping nothing.
>
> On ZGC use **`CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-relocate`** (alias `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0`),
> which has no such gate. `[GC] zgc-relocation-refusal-term:` counts the
> verdicts and `[GC] zgc-relocation-skip-reason:` counts the ones that stopped a
> slide, so the gap between them is the population this warning is about. See
> the "Which collector a token binds" section of `docs/flag-tokens.md`.

```
CRATONVM_NO_MOVING_YOUNG=1 cratonvm -XX:+UseGenerationalGC -Xmx256m \
 --verbose:gc -cp <classes> <Main>
```

**Confirm the lever engaged before you believe either answer.** Every line must
read `kind=non-moving`, and the shutdown census must read
`[GC] decision histogram: moving=0 non_moving=N skipped=0`. (`skipped=` is the
count of cycles that refused to run at all — neither collector — and it is a
third term, not a slice of `non_moving`; a non-zero one is a separate finding,
not a diversion.) Do **not** look for
`[GC] moving_young:` — that line is printed only on Generational and only when
moving-young is enabled (`VmHeap::print_gc_summary`), so it is absent by design
on exactly this run.

> **If you ran this bisect , re-run it.** The flag did not work
> on a process with no live compiled frame: the term that diverts the cycle
> required a live JIT frame *as well as* the gate being off, so a short repro,
> a unit-test-shaped reproduction — anything that had not tiered up — moved on
> every collection with the flag set. Measured at `d6d5cd262`: 1512 of 1512
> cycles `kind=moving`, `reason=moving-no-jit-frames-live`, on a run whose own
> decision record said `moving_young_requested=false`. **Any "not a relocation
> defect" conclusion drawn from that lever on a JIT-cold workload is void.**
> 

Two things it is *not*. It is not a throughput setting — giving up compaction
in both generations costs fragmentation headroom, which is the whole
`[moving-young] fallback` trade. And it is not the lever for "why is my young
generation not compacting?": that question is answered by the `divert_non_moving`
table in [`docs/GC.md`](GC.md), and on a JIT-warm workload the dominant term is
`unrewritable_conservative_jit_roots`, whose only lever is
`CRATONVM_GC=-peer-pin-divert`.

### What is actually shipping, in one place

If another page contradicts this block, that page is stale — the reason this
block exists is that a default collector documented as an opt-in experiment
leaves an operator unable to tell what they are running.

| Question | Answer | Where it is decided |
|---|---|---|
| Which collector runs if I set nothing? | **ZGC** | `VmConfig::default`, `vm/src/config.rs` |
| Is the `zgc` Cargo feature on? | **Yes, by default** — it gates the `GcAlgorithm::Zgc` variant, so the default could not be `Zgc` without it | `gc/Cargo.toml`, `vm/Cargo.toml`, `vm-cli/Cargo.toml` (`^default = `) |
| How do I get a build with no ZGC? | `--no-default-features` (name `mimalloc` back if you still want it on non-Windows targets). Generational becomes the default there and `-XX:+UseZGC` warns and falls back | `vm-cli/Cargo.toml` |
| How do I switch collector at runtime? | `-XX:+UseGenerationalGC` (or `-XX:-UseZGC`); `-XX:+UseG1GC` for G1. Available in every build | `parse_gc_algorithm`, `vm/src/config.rs` |
| Does ZGC move objects? | **Yes, by default — and each cycle decides again.** After the sweep, a stop-the-world slide compacts the small-object end of the arena and returns a **non-empty** `PointerMap`; `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0` (`off`/`false`/`no`) restores the non-moving behaviour byte for byte, so an A/B is a re-run and not a rebuild. Whole-heap and stop-the-world remain the defaults, and it is non-generational unless `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`. **"Default-on" is not "it happened":** `relocate_stw_admitted` re-decides per collection and may decline — on the per-cycle JIT coverage proof (`CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_UNDER_PROVEN_JIT=0` restores the older blanket refusal: compact only when no compiled frame is live) or on the cost gate, which declines a low slide whose selected garbage is under 1/32 of the low region's live bytes (`CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_COST_GATE=0`; default-on). Check `moved=` on `[GC] zgc-relocate:` before concluding a cycle compacted; `live=0 moved=0` is a refusal. | `ZgcRealHeap::relocation_requested_by_default` and `::relocate_stw_admitted`, `gc/src/zgc.rs` |
| Does ZGC have TLABs? | **Yes, both (default-on).** The VM-thread buffer (`CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_TLAB`, `=0` turns it off) makes `VmHeap::refill_tlab` hand the VM thread's own buffer a chunk, so the interpreter's `new` and the JIT's inline bump both hit (bintrees ~6x faster than the helper path; see `ZGC_VM_TLAB_DEFAULT_ON`); the backend keeps its own buffer under that for the helper and native paths (`CRATONVM_ZGC_TLAB=0`, which switches both off — they carve from one source). Engagement: `vm_tlab_refills` on the `[GC] zgc-features:` line. **Chunk sizing counts the VM-thread buffer ** (`CRATONVM_ZGC_VM_TLAB_SHARE=0` restores the old arithmetic): the one sizing entry point divides a sixteenth of the arena by the buffers claiming one, and that divisor used to count only the backend's own cells — so once the VM buffer became default-on and carried nearly all allocation without registering a cell, **every thread was sized as if it were alone**. That is the Tomcat `TestNonBlockingAPI` defect verbatim: ~4 000 threads × 512 KiB is the whole heap, 1.99 GB of it on the free list in pieces, and a 2 MB `char[]` with nowhere to go. Below ~256 buffers the 512 KiB clamp decides and the divisor is inert, so on a small thread count the switch changes nothing measurable | `gc/src/zgc/vm_tlab.rs`, `ZArenaTlabRegistry::chunk_bytes_now`, `ZgcRealHeap::alloc_raw_tlab` |
| Is it concurrent, generational or compacting? | **Concurrent: OPT-IN (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=60`).** The strong closure is traced by a worker pool while every mutator runs; the pause replays the SATB ingress, re-scans the roots and sweeps. Measured per-cycle pause **-38% to -58%**, wall clock **+37% to +55%**, and roughly **twice as many cycles** (floating garbage) -- so it is off unless asked for. **Compacting: YES, on by default** (`CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0` is the kill switch). **Generational: OPT-IN (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`).** Every collection between two whole-heap ones is a young cycle: the old generation is pre-marked and never traced, and the remembered set supplies the old-to-young roots. The split is by **object** age, not page age | [the concurrent+generational plan](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md) |
| How do I tell whether a collection marked concurrently? | `--verbose:gc`'s **`mark=`** field on each `[GC] zgc-real:` line — `concurrent`, `stw-parallel` or `stw-serial`. At shutdown, `[GC] zgc-concurrent:` gives `cycles_started` / `cycles_completed` / `black_allocations` / `satb_replayed` / `concurrent_phase_ms`. **`cycles_started=0` means the run says nothing about concurrent marking**, which is a different fact from concurrent marking not helping | `ZgcRealHeap::collect_garbage`, `VmHeap::print_gc_summary` |
| When does a concurrent cycle open? | When allocation crosses `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`% of the collection threshold (default `0`, i.e. never). `=60` is the value the measurement was taken at and the one to opt in with. **`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=auto`** is a third setting, not a boolean: it seeds the window at 60 and then lets `refresh_adaptive_conc_start` move it from each cycle's own measurements, which is the answer to "a fixed percentage is why concurrent marking loses on *total* pause while winning 38-58% per cycle". **By default a `System.gc`-driven workload never opens one** — a forced collection collects now — so a benchmark built out of `System.gc` calls measures the stop-the-world path. Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_SYSTEM_GC_CONCURRENT=1` (on in the concurrent profile, `CRATONVM_GC=zgc-concurrent` / `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`) a `System.gc`, `jcmd GC.run`, JVMTI `ForceGarbageCollection` or class-metadata request opens a whole concurrent cycle of its own and waits for it, as HotSpot 25's ZGC does (; the last two doors since) | `ZgcRealHeap::should_start_concurrent_mark`, `conc_start_percent_setting` |
| Do young cycles actually happen? | `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_NURSERY_PERCENT` (default 10, `0` disables it) adds a nursery-size clause to `needs_gc`, so a young cycle can fire on its own rather than only when a whole-heap collection would have. `[GC] zgc-nursery-trigger:`'s **`fired=`** is the engagement counter — a **separate line** from `[GC] zgc-nursery:`, which carries `sweep_skipped` / `floor` / `old_live_bytes` and no trigger count at all: **zero on a generational run means every collection still came from the whole-heap predicate**. The clause bypasses `gc_rearm` deliberately (that floor is a quarter of remaining headroom and would make it unreachable); it cannot storm because the watermark resets on every collection | `ZgcRealHeap::needs_gc` |
| Is the young cycle's SWEEP actually bounded? | `--verbose:gc`'s `gen=young/N **swept=A/B**` field: `A` is the registered objects the sweep walked, `B` the whole registry. `A == B` means the nursery floor never moved and the sweep is O(registry) — the state the first Phase G measurement was in. At shutdown, `[GC] zgc-nursery:` gives `sweep_skipped` / `floor` / `old_live_bytes`; **`sweep_skipped=0` with `young_cycles>0` is the inert state**. The floor is the arena cursor the last WHOLE-HEAP collection ended on, so a young cycle sweeps only what the bump cursor served since then — an object the free list placed below the floor waits for a major (over-retention, never unsoundness) | `ZgcRealHeap::gen_young_floor` |
| How do I tell whether a collection was a young one? | `--verbose:gc`'s **`gen=`** field on each `[GC] zgc-real:` line: `young/N` (a minor, where `N` is the objects it retained **without tracing** — the work it did not do), `major/+N` (whole-heap, `N` objects promoted) or `off`. At shutdown, `[GC] zgc-generational:` gives `enabled` / `young_cycles` / `minors_since_major` / `old_retained` / `remembered_roots` / `promotions` / `recards_after_relocation`, printed unconditionally so a run that never engaged says so rather than printing nothing. **`young_cycles>0` with `old_retained=0` means the run did full-heap work under a generational name** — which is the vacuous green a "generational is on" claim would otherwise rest on | `ZgcRealHeap::collect_garbage`, `VmHeap::print_gc_summary` |
| When is a collection forced to be whole-heap? | **Six cases**, and each is a refusal rather than a policy: the mark set came from a **concurrent** cycle (it *is* the whole-heap closure and cannot be scoped after the fact); the collection was driven by **hard allocation failure** (`hard_alloc_failure` — a young cycle retains the whole old generation unexamined, so it is the wrong tool for "the heap is full"; **`headroom_low` is deliberately *not* one of these**, and the code says so in as many words); a previous young cycle **reclaimed nothing** (`gen_force_major_next` — an escalation latch, and the reason a generational run can show a major it did not ask for); the `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MINORS_PER_MAJOR` ceiling (default 8) has been reached; **nothing has been promoted yet** (`has_old_objects`), in which case a young cycle would be a full one anyway; or the **young page set is incomplete** (`young_pages_are_complete` — an allocation that landed off the page grid, which fails closed, because an unrecorded page is retained until a major and therefore forever if majors are rare). Check `young_cycles` before concluding anything. **This row named four cases and listed `headroom_low` among them **, which sent an operator chasing `young_cycles=0` on a small heap to a knob that has nothing to do with it | `ZgcRealHeap::collect_garbage`, the `force_major` / `young_cycle` pair in PHASE G |
| What does a sweep do per dead object? | It zeroes the object's first `HEADER_SIZE` (16) bytes only (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_HEADER_ZERO=0` restores a full-body memset, which is redundant with the memset `alloc_raw` already does on every handout) and hands over one free-list span per **run** of adjacent dead objects rather than one span per object (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_DEAD_RUNS=0` restores the per-object calls, each of which then needs sorting in `coalesce_free_list`). **Both apply to every cycle**, and the saving is on the whole-heap one: a young cycle's sweep is 6.3 ms of a 112 ms pause where a whole-heap cycle's is 170 ms of 265 ms, because it walks 13.0M dead objects against a young cycle's 137k. They were scoped to young cycles when they landed — a mid-gauntlet caution that has since expired — and the restriction pointed the change away from the cost. Engagement on `[GC] zgc-sweep-cost:`: **`zero_bytes_skipped=0` with collections having run means the first is on and inert, and `dead_runs == dead_objects` means the second is**. The flags were `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_*` before the scoping was removed; **the old names are dead and set silently to nothing** | `ZgcRealHeap::collect_garbage`'s `ZSweepCfg`, `zgc_sweep_header_zero` / `zgc_sweep_dead_runs` |
| Is it safe to stop zeroing a dead object's body? | Yes, and the reason is that the header is the whole of the property. The sweep zeroed to stop "a later scan seeing a stale header". Since the 8-byte header every object starts with one header word (class id and mark word) and is at least `MIN_OBJECT_SIZE` (16) bytes; arrays and legacy instances carry a second shape/aux word, a compact instance has its first field there. Zeroing the first 16 bytes therefore clears the whole header of every object, and the result -- class id 0, a mark word with no `COMPACT` flag, shape 0 -- is the same `class_id=0, num_slots=0` corpse a reader of a vacated span always saw. `ARRAY_DATA_OFFSET == HEADER_SIZE`, so an array's length is still in the shape word, not a body prefix. The body is only reachable **through** that header: field reads size the object from its shape or its class's compact layout, extent walks from `alloc_size(header)`, membership from the registry the sweep just removed the base from. And reuse was never the reason -- `alloc_raw` and `tlab_refill` memset unconditionally | `ObjectHeader`, `ZgcRealHeap::alloc_raw` |
| Should I turn generational on? | **No, not for CPU ** On `Zr1GnGenShape 64 400 200000 5 8 64` at `-Xmx1g` generational cost **12.6 s** CPU against the default's **6.3 s** (10.8 s with the young slide refused); HotSpot's generational ZGC ran 0 minors and 3 majors there. By the allocation volume (an estimate pending the z2 host rows) the mode runs ~36 cycles where the default runs ~5-6, and its minor-count ceiling still schedules as many whole-heap cycles as the default -- see `zgc-zr1gn-proposal-generational-direction.md` (internal proposals). Defaults INSIDE the mode since z2/g2: old-start bitmap ON (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_OLD_BITMAP=0` restores the header walk; since z3/rv3 a young cycle the bitmap cannot serve runs whole-heap instead of falling back, `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_BITMAP_FALLBACK_MAJOR=0` restores the fallback), young slide OFF (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_RELOCATE=1`), young complement sweep ON since(`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_COMPLEMENT=0`), nursery census OFF (`CRATONVM_ZGC_NURSERY_CENSUS=1`), JIT inline start-bit store open (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_INLINE_ANNOUNCE=0`); opt-in: `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MAJOR_PROMOTED_PERCENT=<p>`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_ELEMENT_CARDS=1`. `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:` (with `--verbose:gc`) splits the collector time by cycle kind. The earlier measurement follows. On the shape it is for — 800k retained objects, 48M allocated, an old-to-young store per round — it is **neutral at the default promotion age (+2.5% to +4% total pause) and clearly worse at age 1 (+46% to +55%, reclaim 75.7% → 63.7%)**, with wall clock flat. The split works (4.8M objects skipped per young cycle, every old-to-young edge intact) and does not pay, because `sweep` is 182 ms of a 309 ms pause and walks every registered object whatever the split says. A real young space, reclaimed by resetting a cursor, is what would change that. If you try it anyway: `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_PROMOTION_AGE` (default 3, clamped to `1..=15` because the header field is 4 bits) decides how many collections an object must survive; a lower value promotes sooner, so young cycles skip more and old garbage accumulates faster. Measure with `gen=` and `old_retained`, and note that **a lower promotion age is not a stronger version of the same knob** — age 1 promoted 11.7M objects, filling old with garbage no young cycle examines | the plan's §3 |
| Should I turn concurrent marking on? | If your workload has **many mutator threads, a large live set, and a pause budget you are missing**. It trades throughput for pause and the trade is not small; measure your own workload with `--verbose:gc` and compare `mark=concurrent` cycles against `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=0`. `-XX:ConcGCThreads=<n>` (/mt; wins over the env knob) or `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_WORKERS` (an explicit value is clamped to `1..=32`) is the second knob; the default is HotSpot ZGC's ergonomic, 25 % of the CPUs rounded up (12.5 % with `-XX:-UseDynamicNumberOfGCThreads`), capped at 2 % of the heap in 2 MiB pages (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GC_THREAD_FLAGS=0` restores the old `cores/4`, floor 1, cap 4) — 1 worker measured best on a single-threaded probe, 2 on an 8-thread one. Deliberately a different knob from the stop-the-world mark pool: that one wants every core because nothing else is running, this one is stealing cycles from the mutators beside it. `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=<n>`  sizes ZGC's stop-the-world pools that are on (the parallel mark when `CRATONVM_ZGC_PARMARK` selects it, the compaction passes, the parallel sweep when `CRATONVM_ZGC_PARSWEEP` turns it on); `1` makes them all serial | the plan's §2b |
| Why did a collection run? | `[GC] zgc-trigger:` carries four reason tallies plus `hard_alloc_refusals` — `stress` (`CRATONVM_DBG_GC_STRESS`), `live_bytes_threshold` (75% of `-Xmx`), `headroom_low` (the arena cannot serve a `zgc_headroom_margin` request) and `alloc_budget` (`CRATONVM_ZGC_ALLOC_TRIGGER`, percent of capacity allocated since the last cycle, **default 0 = off** — the implicit floor a pause target used to supply was withdrawn , see the flag notes below). They are not exclusive: a cycle can be charged to the threshold *and* to headroom, which is the whole question when two arms differ in collection **count**. They count collections (not raw polls of `needs_gc`) — so comparisons reflect completed collection cycles. | `ZgcRealHeap::needs_gc`, `ZgcCounters::trigger_seen` |
| Can I measure the reference-slot mix? | `CRATONVM_ZGC_CENSUS=1` turns on the sweep-time walk that answers "what fraction of live reference slots are legacy 16-byte cells?", and `CRATONVM_ZGC_CENSUS_ACCESS=1` adds the per-access arm (and implies the first). The summary prints when the heap is dropped, and only if the census was enabled. **It was unreachable ** — `ZSlotCensus::enable` had no caller outside its own tests, so `is_enabled` was `false` for the life of every process and a 2 900-line instrument had never run. Read `legacy_share` off the **`last_walk`** block, not the cumulative `walk` one: the cumulative figure is lifetime-weighted and reads high on legacy. A `VERDICT=no_data` row is the instrument saying so, and is a different fact from "no legacy slots" | `ZSlotCensus::enable_from_env`, `gc/src/zgc/census.rs` |
| What does it cost me? | Headroom, and less of it than this row used to claim. It said "**not** compacting means free memory can be plentiful and still too broken up to serve one large array" — written when the slide was off. The slide is on, so the steady state is defragmented; what remains is that **a cycle may decline to compact** (the cost gate, the JIT coverage proof, `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0`), and that a declined cycle leaves exactly the fragmentation the sentence describes, with `headroom_low` as the symptom and an aborting `alloc_object` as the endgame. The large-object end is compacted separately (`CRATONVM_ZGC_HIGH_COMPACTION=0` is its kill switch) and reserves `capacity / 8` | the sizing notes below; `[GC] zgc-relocate:`'s `moved=` |

| Backend | Module | Status | Best for |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ZGC** (default) | [`gc/src/zgc.rs`](../gc/src/zgc.rs) | Default, and still **not a real ZGC** | Most workloads, on the suite evidence above. Compacting by default, concurrent marking and generational mode opt-in, one arena. Still not OpenJDK ZGC: it marks under a **pre-write** barrier (snapshot-at-the-beginning), not under ZGC's load barrier, so it is conservative about objects that die mid-cycle, and relocation is stop-the-world. Fewest hangs and zero crashes across the Tomcat suite. See [the maturity assessment](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md) for what is and is not built, and the plan to close it. |
| **Generational** | [`gc/src/gen_heap.rs`](../gc/src/gen_heap.rs) | Production; the escape hatch (`-XX:+UseGenerationalGC`) | Tight heap budgets, and anything that regressed on the flip. Young copying + old free-list + write barriers + card table. Carries the `[moving-young] fallback` throughput problem the flip exists to escape — and note that on a JIT-warm workload the young half is in practice **not** a copying collector at all, because a live compiled frame diverts the cycle to the non-moving sweep; see the `divert_non_moving` table in [`docs/GC.md`](GC.md). Both the young mark and the young evacuation are parallel when they do run. |
| **G1** (Garbage-First) | [`gc/src/g1.rs`](../gc/src/g1.rs) | Production, opt-in (`-XX:+UseG1GC`) | Workloads that need COMPACTION more than they need short pauses: a live set small relative to the heap, fragmenting allocation shapes, and a large old generation. Region-based, evacuating, mixed young/old collections, a real SATB concurrent-mark cycle, and a **parallel evacuator that is on by default** (`CRATONVM_G1_PARALLEL_EVAC=0` is the bisect). Evacuation is stop-the-world; marking is not. Its structural limit is that the per-pause reference fix-up walk is O(live heap) rather than O(collection set) — see [`docs/GC.md`](GC.md) for the measured phase split. |

Trade-offs at a glance:

- **Generational** has the simplest configuration surface and the tightest
 minor-GC pause envelope. On a VM-built heap each young semi-space is
 **`-Xmx / 4`** (`GenerationalHeap::with_capacity` in
 [`gc/src/gen_heap.rs`](../gc/src/gen_heap.rs)), so 64 MiB at the 256 MiB
 library default but 1 GiB under the launcher's ergonomic 4 GiB heap;
 `-Xmn` / `-XX:NewRatio` resize it (see "Young generation size" below).
 `DEFAULT_YOUNG_SEMI_SIZE` (64 MiB) is used only by the argument-less
 `GenerationalHeap::new`, not by the VM.
- **G1** breaks the heap into regions and selects collection sets per pause
 target. It pays a per-store remembered-set cost (~10 ns) but amortises
 full-heap compaction. Use it when the old generation is large and
 reclamation latency matters more than minor-GC throughput. The region size
 is an **ergonomic, not a constant**: `g1_ergonomic_region_size`
 (`gc/src/vm_heap.rs`) targets ~2048 regions at any heap size, so it is 1 MiB
 up to a 2 GiB heap and then 2/4/8/16/32 MiB, clamped to
 `[256 KiB, 32 MiB]`. That bound matters because almost every per-pause cost
 in the collector is linear in the REGION COUNT and the remembered set's
 worst case is quadratic in it. `-XX:G1HeapRegionSize=<bytes>` overrides it
 (rounded up to a power of two, then clamped to the same range).
- **ZGC** is **not** stub-only. `ZgcRealHeap` (`gc/src/zgc.rs`) is a real
 memory-backed collector — `Arena` storage, real `ObjectHeader`s, real
 reference processing — and `-XX:+UseZGC` really selects it
 (`GcAlgorithm::Zgc` → `GcBackend::Zgc` → `VmHeap::Zgc`). What it is *not* is
 ZGC: it is stop-the-world, whole-heap by default and non-generational unless
 opted in. It **is** compacting by default — see the row above; "not a real
 ZGC" is a statement about the pause and the barrier, never about the motion.
 It *does* have TLABs — thread-private chunks carved from the
 arena, default-on, kill switch `CRATONVM_ZGC_TLAB=0` or
 `CRATONVM_GC=-zgc-tlab`. The chunk is **not** a fixed size: it is a
 sixteenth of the arena divided by the buffers actually claiming one — the
 VM-thread buffer included, — and clamped up to 512 KiB,
 because a fixed chunk times a large thread count would be the whole heap. The
 colored-pointer / `ZPage` code above it in the same file is **mostly, but no
 longer entirely**, a metadata-only simulation: `forwarding::ZRelocationSet::select`
 is a real production consumer — it ranks the slide's pages by descending
 garbage ratio — while the load-barrier layer is still unreached, no
 `ZGoodMask` is ever constructed by a running heap, and `ZgcRealHeap` stores
 plain machine pointers whose liveness lives in the header's `GC_FLAG_MARKED`
 bit rather than in a pointer's metadata bits. Do not guess at this:
 `scripts/zgc-submodule-adoption.sh` prints the current production reference
 count per submodule and `types/tests/zgc-submodule-adoption.txt` is the
 recorded answer a test holds it to, precisely because a hand-written version
 of this sentence has been wrong twice. Across every suite with a
 per-collector sweep, ZGC's PASS/HANG/CRASH counts sit at parity with or
 ahead of Generational's — see the "What is actually shipping" table above
 for the honest current comparison rather than any one suite's headline
 count. The path to a real ZGC is
 [`docs/feature-designs/zgc-production-implementation-plan.md`](feature-designs/zgc-production-implementation-plan.md);
 see [the maturity assessment](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md)
 for what is and is not built.
- **ZGC's arena has two ends, and the split is operator-visible.** Small
 objects and TLAB chunks bump upward from the bottom; anything too big for a
 TLAB to serve (>= 64 KiB, i.e. `ZGC_TLAB_MAX_CHUNK / 8`) bumps *downward*
 from the top, with its own free list and a floor of `capacity / 8` reserved
 for it. The reason is the regime the split was designed for and still has to
 survive: **a cycle that does not compact** (the cost gate declines it, the
 JIT coverage proof refuses it, or `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0`). There, the
 largest request the arena can serve is the largest gap between two survivors —
 and one long-lived object inside a thread's private chunk caps every hole in
 the heap at one chunk. The split is what keeps a large array's supply
 independent of that. (This paragraph read "this collector does not compact by
 default" , **five weeks** after the default moved — compaction
 has been on by default since ****
 (`ZgcRealHeap::relocation_requested_by_default` in `gc/src/zgc.rs`), pinned by
 `compaction_is_on_by_default_and_zero_is_the_kill_switch`. It said "three
 months" , which was itself a drifted number in a sentence
 about drift.) The split's *reason* survives the flip, its premise does not. The large end has its own
 compactor, `CRATONVM_ZGC_HIGH_COMPACTION` (default on, `=0` is the kill
 switch), distinct from the low slide's `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE`. The practical consequence for sizing is that a
 workload dominated by large buffers is served from a region bounded below by
 `-Xmx / 8`, and one that allocates no large objects at all gives that region
 up again (the floor is a preference the small-object end may overrun rather
 than fail).
- The `zgc` feature is **on by default**, because the default `GcAlgorithm` is
 `Zgc` and that variant is `#[cfg(feature = "zgc")]`. A
 `--no-default-features` build has no ZGC at all and falls back to
 Generational; `-XX:+UseZGC` there warns and falls back too.

### ZGC switches

A flag that exists and is undocumented is the same defect class as a documented
flag that does nothing. The full generated
inventory is [`docs/config/flag-inventory.md`](config/flag-inventory.md) and the
token spellings are in [`docs/flag-tokens.md`](flag-tokens.md); what follows is
only what moved, with the reason, because a default that changes how often the
default collector runs is not a detail.

| Flag | Default | What it does, and why |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_VM_TLAB_SHARE` | **ON** | Counts the VM-thread buffer against the TLAB reservation share, so a chunk shrinks with the number of threads actually claiming one. `=0` restores the pre- arithmetic, which counted only the backend's own cells and therefore sized every thread as if it were alone once the VM buffer became default-on. The kill switch exists so the claim is falsifiable on a built binary rather than argued; see the TLAB row above for the Tomcat measurement it re-closes. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_HEADROOM_BYPASSES_REARM` | **OFF** | Lets the `headroom_low` clause of `needs_gc` ask for a collection without clearing the `gc_rearm` floor — but only while the last cycle actually reclaimed something. `gc_rearm` is about **live bytes** and `headroom_low` is about **allocatable space**, and on a heap that may decline to compact those two come apart without bound: a 2 GiB heap holding 10 MiB live sets the floor near 507 MiB, the bump cursor then runs to capacity over a fragmented free list, and the clause answers "no" until an allocation actually fails — which for the infallible `alloc_object` is `abort`, not an `OutOfMemoryError`. Off by default because removing the floor on a heap genuinely full of **live** data is a collection per allocation, which is the storm the floor exists to prevent; the tree has shipped that argument before and reverted it. internal records names the measurement that would earn the flip. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CENSUS` | **OFF** | Turns on the sweep-time reference-slot walk. See the census row above. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CENSUS_ACCESS` | **OFF** | Adds the per-access arm, and implies `CRATONVM_ZGC_CENSUS` — asking for the dynamic composition and silently getting nothing because a second variable was unset is the same "enabled and inert" trap the gate exists to make visible. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PAUSE_TARGET_INCLUDES_RELOCATE` | **OFF** | Feeds the pause-target controller the pause **including** the relocation slide. `refresh_pause_target_budget` sizes `alloc_trigger_bytes`, which is a clause of `needs_gc` — so it decides how often the collector runs — and it was fed a reading taken before the compaction block, which on `ZgcTlabStress 8 200` was measured at about **twice** the logged pause. On the default configuration the loop was modelling roughly a third of its own cost. Set to `1`, the reading equals the `pause_with_relocate_us` field `[GC] zgc-relocate:` already prints. Staged behind a flag for one wave rather than flipped, because it tightens every budget on every workload. `[GC] zgc-pause:`'s `total_us` is unaffected in both arms; it is documented as the pre-slide figure. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METRICS` | **OFF** | Also print `metrics::ZgcMetrics`'s OpenJDK-shaped per-cycle line and its whole-run summary at heap teardown. **Recording is not behind this flag** — `ZgcMetrics` is fed on every collection now; only the two renderings are opt-in, because the format differs from `[GC] zgc-real:` and every harness in this tree keys on that one. Additive: the existing lines are unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TRIGGER_SHADOW` | **OFF** | Print one `[GC] zgc-trigger-shadow:` line per collection showing what an allocation-rate-driven trigger *would* have decided — `rate_bps`, `free_bytes`, `largest_bytes`, `cycle_us`, `ttl_ms`, `ratio`, `affordable_span`, and `fired=` naming which legacy `needs_gc` clauses actually asked (or `none` for a `System.gc` or a hard refusal that bypassed the poll). Pure observation; costs one arena lock per collection. **Read `ratio`'s stability between adjacent cycles, not its value** — a projection that swings by an order of magnitude between neighbours refutes the direction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER` | **OFF** | HotSpot ZGC's director rules decide when to collect (`gc/src/zgc/director.rs`): **allocation rate** (worst-case rate = 2 x the 1 s window mean + 3.29 sd; collect when free space under the soft max no longer covers it for one cycle -- decaying mean + 3.29 sd of measured cycles -- plus 100 ms), **proactive** (warm, grown 10% of the soft max or 5 min idle, and past the 1%-throughput interval) and **high usage** (free <= 5% of the soft max). The 75%-of-`-Xmx` live-bytes clause stands down; headroom, the pause-target budget, stress, nursery, warm-up and the hard allocation refusal are unchanged. Evaluated as a byte threshold, so a poll costs two relaxed loads and a compare. Collections it admits are tallied as `director` and named `Allocation Rate` / `Proactive` / `High Usage` on `-Xlog:gc` and notifications. Flip criteria: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_HOTSPOT_GC_CAUSE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | An allocation-driven ZGC collection is reported under HotSpot 25 ZGC's `GCCause` on `-Xlog:gc` (`Major Collection (<cause>)`), `GarbageCollectionNotificationInfo.getGcCause` and JFR `jdk.GarbageCollection.cause`: `Warmup`, `Allocation Rate` (every other `needs_gc` clause), `Proactive`, `High Usage`, and `Allocation Stall` for an allocation that failed outright. HotSpot's ZGC never says `Allocation Failure`; this VM's ZGC did on every allocation-driven collection. `=0` restores `Allocation Failure`. `System.gc` / `Metadata GC Threshold` are unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in; a zr z8 default flip was reverted after the host batch: F1 CPU 1.11x, pause 0.88x vs STW -- the slide, not the mark, dominates the pause) | HotSpot ZGC's model: every non-urgent trigger (warm-up, allocation budget, live-bytes threshold, headroom, the `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER` rules) first opens a CONCURRENT mark when the heap has the headroom to finish one (free space above 5% of the capacity -- at least the headroom margin -- plus 1.5x the expected allocation during the mark), and the collection is taken when the mark reaches its fixed point. Stop-the-world only on allocation failure, at the urgent reserve, without headroom, or when a requested start is refused (the take-over cover) or not served within half the reserve. Configures concurrent marking by itself; inert in generational mode. Counted on `[GC] zgc-conc-trigger:` (`ct_opened`, `ct_finished`, `ct_abandoned_stw`, `ct_urgent`, `ct_lapsed`, `ct_start_refusals`, `ct_no_headroom`). Flip table: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_EVERY_TRIGGER_WINDOW_INERT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Makes "inert in generational mode" true for the every-trigger mode and for the concurrent profile. Before this switch, on a `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` heap, the every-trigger construction seed (60 % of the threshold) fell through to the occupancy-window mode. That opened concurrent cycles nobody configured, and every one of them closed as a major. Now such a heap opens no window cycle. An explicit `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START` answers to `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_CONC_START_WINDOW_INERT` (next row). `=0` restores the seed's window. Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_CONC_START_WINDOW_INERT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The explicit half of the row above. On a `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` heap an explicit `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START` (`<p>` or `auto`) opens no concurrent window either: here a concurrently marked cycle is always whole-heap, so every window cycle closed as a major, while HotSpot 25 keeps counting `ZGC Minor Cycles` under any trigger. A refused window prints `gen_inert=1` on `[GC] zgc-conc-miss:`. `=0` restores the window (and its majors). Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_RATE_FROM_ALLOCATION` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The adaptive concurrent-start window samples the allocation since the last collection before the sweep resets the counter (it used to read the live-set growth, ~0 on a steady live set, so the window stuck at the bootstrap or the ceiling). Only acts in window mode (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=auto`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_WINDOW_EXTRAPOLATE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A concurrent mark cut short by its collection feeds `elapsed x total scanned / scanned concurrently` (capped at 4x) back as the mark-duration estimate instead of its short elapsed time, so windows stop shrinking cycle over cycle. Window mode only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SATB_FILTER_MARKED` | **2** (filter at buffer flush; `=1` per-store; `=0` kill) | SATB records whose old value is already marked are dropped (HotSpot's `SATBMarkQueueSet::filter` rule). Mode 2 filters a 256-entry thread buffer when it flushes, so the per-store barrier is unchanged and an all-marked batch skips the ingress and the hand-off; mode 1 filtered each store (an extra mark-bitmap read per store). Only acts while a concurrent cycle is open. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_IGNORE_MAX_GC_PAUSE_MILLIS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `-XX:MaxGCPauseMillis` does not size ZGC's pause-target budget (HotSpot's ZGC does not read it); `CRATONVM_ZGC_PAUSE_TARGET_MS` still does. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_STALL_CAUSE_FROM_TRIGGER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection taken through the allocation-FAILURE door is reported `Allocation Stall` only when no trigger rule asked for it; otherwise it carries the rule `needs_gc` charged (`Warmup`, `Allocation Rate`, ...). The compiled refill trigger (`tlab_alloc_shaped_inner`, census site `tlab-alloc-shaped`) takes that door on an occupancy verdict, so every collection a JIT-compiled allocation loop caused printed `Major Collection (Allocation Stall)` although no allocation failed. `=0` restores the z1 reading. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_YOUNG_POOL_SINCE_CYCLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `ZGC Young Generation` reports the bytes allocated since the last collection and `ZGC Old Generation` the post-collection live figure. Old `committed == used`, young committed = heap committed minus old committed (never below young used), so the pools sum to `getHeapMemoryUsage` (HotSpot 25 ZGC's pool shape, host `Zr3T2HeapVsPools`). `=0`: everything in the old pool with the heap's commit, young always 0. `MemoryMXBean.getHeapMemoryUsage` and `Runtime` are unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_HOTSPOT_BEAN_ORDER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC heap pools list their managers as HotSpot 25 does (`ZGC Minor Cycles, ZGC Major Cycles, ZGC Minor Pauses, ZGC Major Pauses`), and a collection's `end of GC pause` notification is queued before its `end of GC cycle` notification. `=0`: bean order on both. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_HOTSPOT_PHASE_SET` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection writes no `jdk.GCPhasePauseLevel1` rows, on either JFR backend: HotSpot 25 ZGC writes none (its pauses have no sub-phases; `Zr4JfJfrEvents sysgc` on HotSpot: `present=false`). This collector's laps run inside its one pause, so they are not written as concurrent rows either. `=0` writes them as level-1 rows again (the z4/jf shape). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_EXIT_HEAP_SUMMARY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `-Xlog:gc+exit` (and `gc*`) prints HotSpot ZGC's exit lines on ZGC: `Stopping ZGC`, `Heap`, ` ZHeap used NM, capacity NM, max capacity NM` (allocated, committed, `-Xmx`, truncated to MiB) and the `Metaspace` line. HotSpot's two page-cache lines (`Cache`, `size classes`) and `class space` describe structures this heap does not have and are not printed. |
| `CRATONVM_VERSION_XLOG_BOOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `-version` / `--version` together with an `-Xlog` option boots the VM before printing the banner (HotSpot creates the VM for `-version`), so `-Xlog:gc -version` prints `[gc] Using The Z Garbage Collector`, `-Xlog:gc+init -version` the `gc,init` block and `-Xlog:gc+exit -version` the exit summary. Without `-Xlog`, or with no class library, the banner keeps its no-VM fast path. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PAUSE_GCINFO_NO_USAGE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A `ZGC Major Pauses` / `ZGC Minor Pauses` `GcInfo` (`getLastGcInfo`, the `end of GC pause` notification) carries all-zero pool usages before and after, as HotSpot 25's pause tracer records none (`Zs2ObBeanSurface`: `gcinfo_usage_before=zero`). The cycle beans keep the real figures. `=0`: the pause records carry the collection's real usage, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_POOL_PEAK_AT_GC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `ZGC Young Generation` / `ZGC Old Generation` `getPeakUsage` also takes the pools' usage at both edges of every collection, as HotSpot's ZGC cycle tracer records it (a query-time sample alone missed the pre-collection peak). `resetPeakUsage` resets it. `=0`: query-time peaks only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_HOTSPOT_POOL_INIT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `ZGC Young Generation`'s `MemoryUsage.getInit` is `MinHeapSize` (8M by default, `-Xms` when given) and `ZGC Old Generation`'s 0, as HotSpot 25's `ZServiceability` builds them. `=0`: young 0, old the startup commit. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_HEAP_ALIGN_HOTSPOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The launcher aligns ZGC's `-Xmx`, initial and minimum heap up to 2 MiB, HotSpot's ZGC granule: `-Xmx9m` is a 10M heap (`Runtime.maxMemory` 10485760, `Max Capacity: 10M`), `-Xms5000k` a 6M minimum. `-XX:SoftMaxHeapSize` is not aligned (nor is it on HotSpot). Launcher only; the embedding entry points keep the typed sizes. `=0`: the typed sizes. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REFUSE_ZERO_GC_THREADS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | An explicit `-XX:+UseZGC` with `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=0` (checked first) or `-XX:ConcGCThreads=0` exits 1 with HotSpot's two lines on stdout, `Error occurred during initialization of VM` / `The flag -XX:+UseZGC can not be combined with -XX:<name>=0`, also in place of a `-version` banner. ZGC as the default (no `-XX:+UseZGC`) is not refused: a zero `ConcGCThreads` is dropped silently , a zero `ParallelGCThreads` keeps the warning (HotSpot's default G1 refuses it with a G1-named text). `=0`: warn and continue. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_HOTSPOT_CONFIGURATION` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC the real JFR backend's `jdk.GCHeapConfiguration` states `MinHeapSize` / `InitialHeapSize` / `MaxHeapSize` and narrow-oop mode `32-bit` (it wrote `Runtime.totalMemory` as both the minimum and the initial size), and `jdk.GCConfiguration` the explicit `-XX:ParallelGCThreads` / `-XX:ConcGCThreads`, `UseDynamicNumberOfGCThreads`, `DisableExplicitGC` and `ExplicitGCInvokesConcurrent`. Ergonomic thread counts keep the generic figures. `=0`: the generic figures. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_HOTSPOT_PAUSES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection's `jdk.GCPhasePause` rows are HotSpot 25's pause names over the pause's own phase split: `Pause Mark Start (Major)` (`Pause Mark Start` for a minor; absent when the mark start was its own pause), `Pause Mark End`, `Pause Relocate Start`; `GarbageCollection.longestPause` is the longest. Both JFR writers. `=0`: one `GC Pause` row. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_STALL_REQUEST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `jdk.ZAllocationStall` `type` / `size` = the page HotSpot 25 ZGC would allocate for the request the arena refused before the stall (Small 2 MiB; Medium `MaxHeapSize/32` in [2M,32M]; Large rounded to 2 MiB). `=0`: no type, size 0. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_REF_AND_YOUNG_EVENTS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `jdk.GCReferenceStatistics` (four rows per major collection, none per minor, as HotSpot 25) and, for a generational-mode `ZGC Minor`, `jdk.ZYoungGarbageCollection` (threshold = promotion age - 1); a major's young event also needs `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_MAJOR_YOUNG`. `=0`: neither. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_SYSTEM_GC_ON_CALLER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC `jdk.SystemGC` is written by `Runtime.gc` on the calling thread with its stack, spanning the call (including the door's finalizer / cleaner / notification drains); the door then walks no stack for a row it will not write. `=0`: the `System.gc` door's per-request row , also on the calling thread and with the request's stack, spanning the collection only -- the dev merge retired the collection's `VM Thread` row this switch used to fall back to. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_DRIVER_THREADS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection's own JFR events (real backend: `GarbageCollection`, `GCPhasePause`, `GCPhaseConcurrent`, `ZOld`/`ZYoungGarbageCollection`, `ZRelocationSet*`, `GCReferenceStatistics`...) are committed on `ZDriverMajor` (whole-heap collection, its mark start and concurrent mark) or `ZDriverMinor` (young), as HotSpot 25 names them; safepoint rows stay on `VM Thread`. `=0`: all on `VM Thread`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_STALL_STACK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The real backend's `jdk.ZAllocationStall` is written from the stalling thread's context: its own Java thread entry and its Java stack, as HotSpot's. `=0`: no stack, thread found by name. |
| `CRATONVM_JFR_ENUM_KEYS_FROM_ONE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The real JFR backend numbers its enum-like constant pools (`GCName`, `GCCause`, `GCWhen`, `ZPageTypeType`, `ReferenceType`, ...) from 1, so a "no value" reference (id 0) reads back as `null`; numbered from 0 it read as the pool's first entry. All collectors (the numbering is internal; only a null reference reads differently). `=0`: from 0. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_SYSTEM_GC_NATIVE_FRAME` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The caller-side `jdk.SystemGC` stack starts with the native `java.lang.Runtime.gc` frame, as HotSpot 25's (its `JVM_GC` commits under that frame whatever the Java caller); the native's own frame is not in this VM's capture. Both modes. `=0`: the Java caller (`System.gc`) on top. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JVMTI_FORCE_GC_DOOR` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC a JVMTI `ForceGarbageCollection` is HotSpot 25's `_jvmti_force_gc` collection: cause `JvmtiEnv ForceGarbageCollection` (JFR, `-Xlog:gc`, GC notifications), no `jdk.SystemGC` on either JFR writer (HotSpot commits it in `JVM_GC` only), and not refused by `-XX:+DisableExplicitGC`. `=0`: the `System.gc` door. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_METASPACE_CHUNK_FREE_LISTS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The real JFR backend writes HotSpot 25's `jdk.MetaspaceChunkFreeListSummary` per ZGC collection: four rows (Before / After GC x `Metadata` / `Class`), every count and size 0 -- HotSpot's elastic metaspace has kept no chunk free lists since JDK 16 and writes zeros; this VM has none either. Generational and G1 write none (out of the ZGC round's scope). `=0`: none. |

GCHeapSummary` (both writers) is the heap as the mark-start pause found it, at the cycle's start, as HotSpot traces it; the metaspace summaries keep the closing collection's edges. A mark start whose cycle never closes is still written, on its own (: as `Pause Mark Start (Major)` with its `ZMarkStartYoungAndOld` safepoint under `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_PAUSE_VM_OPERATIONS`). | `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_STANDALONE_MARK_START_JOINS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The mark-start pause of a ZGC concurrent cycle that its closing collection did not close concurrently (a `System.gc` abandoned it and marked afresh) carries the id that collection takes; it joins the collection's pause rows and `jdk.GarbageCollection` span instead of sitting before it under the same `gcId` (local row `jf2_t2_sysgc_conc`: `pause ... inside_gc=false`, HotSpot `true`). | `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_PAUSE_PHASES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection prints, between its `gc` start and end lines, HotSpot 25's `gc,phases` pause lines -- `GC(n) Y: Pause Mark Start (Major) 0.011ms`, `GC(n) O: Pause Mark End ...`, `GC(n) O: Pause Relocate Start ...` (`y:` and the plain names for a generational-mode minor; a concurrent cycle's mark start first when a recording joined it) -- and its `gc,metaspace` line `GC(n) O: Metaspace: NM used, NM committed, NM reserved`, the shapes of a local HotSpot 25.0.3 `-Xlog:gc*` capture. HotSpot's other per-collection `gc*` lines are recorded as intended differences in `docs/GC.md`. the `gc,init` block's min and max, the soft max the collection ran under; percent of the max). `=0`: none of these. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JFR_CODE_HEAP_ROWS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC the real JFR backend's `jdk.CodeCacheStatistics` is one row per HotSpot code heap (`CodeHeap 'non-nmethods'`, `'profiled nmethods'`, `'non-profiled nmethods'`, as `jdk.types.CodeBlobType` keys), the JIT's code in the non-profiled one with `unallocatedCapacity` = code-cache cap - used, and `jdk.CodeCacheConfiguration` states the cap as `reservedSize` / `nonProfiledSize`: the figures the `CodeHeap` pool beans report. | `CRATONVM_JFR_NATIVE_THREAD_NULL_JAVA_NAME` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The real JFR backend writes a VM-internal thread (no Java thread id and no Java name: `VM Thread`, `ZDriverMajor` / `ZDriverMinor`, `ZUncommitter`) with a null `javaName`, as HotSpot 25 writes its non-Java threads (`RecordedThread.getJavaName` is `null`); it wrote `""`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_REFUSE_MALFORMED_GC_THREADS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC (explicit or default) a malformed `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=` / `-XX:ConcGCThreads=` value (`abc`, `-1`, `+3`, `1.5`, empty, `0x`, above `u32`) ends the launch as HotSpot 25 does: `Improperly specified VM option '<name>=<value>'`, `Error: Could not create the Java Virtual Machine.`, `Error: A fatal exception has occurred. Both flags take HotSpot's value syntax (`0x` hex, `k`/`m`/`g`/`t` multiplier). `=0`: warn and continue, decimal only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_IMPLICIT_ZERO_GC_THREADS_SILENT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A default (implicit) ZGC drops `-XX:ConcGCThreads=0` without a stderr line, as HotSpot's default G1 accepts it silently. `=0`: the `Warning: ignoring ...` line. |

The edges sample only once a program has been handed the pool beans, and walk the class store only when the loaded-class census moved (never inside the pause). this switch keeps the `GcInfo` edge samples, and lists its own `Metaspace`-only pool (`committed` = `used`) only under that row's kill. `=0`: no edge samples (a cycle `GcInfo`'s `Metaspace` entry is the pool's usage at delivery). |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CODE_HEAP_INIT_HOTSPOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With the row above on, ZGC's three `CodeHeap '...'` pools each report HotSpot 25's `init` of 2555904 (`InitialCodeCacheSize`, 2496K, committed per heap at start), so `MemoryMXBean.getNonHeapMemoryUsage.getInit` is HotSpot's 7667712 instead of 2 MiB; the non-heap threshold samples carry the same `init`. G1 / Generational keep's figures. `=0`: 2 MiB on the non-profiled heap, 0 on the other two. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GCINFO_CODE_HEAP_EDGES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_JMX_HOTSPOT_NON_HEAP_POOLS` on, a ZGC cycle `GcInfo` (`getLastGcInfo`, the cycle notifications) carries the three `CodeHeap '...'` entries sampled at the collection's two edges (the JIT's mapped code, one relaxed load per edge), as HotSpot 25 samples every pool at `gc_begin` / `gc_end`; they were rendered from the usage at delivery, so before == after and code installed after the collection was counted. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_FIRST_CYCLES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC cycle `GcInfo` recorded before the `Metaspace` pool was first handed out keeps the class-store size at its edges (one length read, no walk) and gains the measured `Metaspace` key -- the class metadata of exactly the classes defined by then, one walk of that prefix of the class store -- when a `GcInfoBuilder` first names the pool (`MemoryImpl.getMemoryPools0`, or the first notification drain). `=0`: such a record keeps no key and reads the JMM's undefined usage (`init = max = -1`); HotSpot always has the figure. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_THRESHOLD` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A `Metaspace` usage threshold is checked when it is set and at the end of every collection (HotSpot 25: the same two points, not on class loading), so its sensor fires `MEMORY_THRESHOLD_EXCEEDED` and counts. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_THRESHOLD_SET_DRAIN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On ZGC a usage-threshold crossing that `setUsageThreshold` finds (any pool) raises the drain hint and  asks the delivery thread at once, so the notification is delivered without waiting for a collection or for the setting thread to allocate (: also for a non-heap pool such as `Metaspace`, whose set takes's arm since the dev merge). `=0`: it waits for a drain door (the next collection, or the 16 KiB occupancy poll of an allocating thread). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_BACKFILL_HOLD_MEMO` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_BUSY_LOCK_BACKFILL` on: once a drain's busy-lock retries (16 yields + `try_read`s) failed, later drain points keep the hold after their one non-blocking try until the class-definition epoch or the collection count moves, instead of paying the retries again while one thread holds the class lock for a long define. Delivery order and content unchanged. `=0`: the retries at every drain point. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_EDGE_TALLY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * ZGC: the `GcInfo` `Metaspace` / `Compressed Class Space` edge samples (`zgc_metaspace_edge_sample`) and the perf-data sampler's metadata pass take HotSpot's model figures from the running arena tally the `-XX:MaxMetaspaceSize` check keeps (`hs_tally_catch_up`: the classes defined since the last look; a full re-sum after an unload) instead of a class-store walk. Deciding rows: `t11-pl3-report.md` (`mt-*`). .. notified=true metaspace_keys=true`, CPU T/D 0.912 over 10 pairs, no `differs` line without the skew control). `=0`: the class-store walk at every sample, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_BUSY_LOCK_BACKFILL` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The `Metaspace` back-fill (`CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_FIRST_CYCLES`) survives a busy class lock. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_IMPLICIT_ZERO_PARALLEL_GC_THREADS_REFUSE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With no collector flag (the default ZGC), `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=0` ends the launch as HotSpot 25's default G1 does: `Error occurred during initialization of VM` / `The flag -XX:+UseG1GC can not be combined with -XX:ParallelGCThreads=0` on stdout, exit 1, also before a `-version` banner. `=0`: the `Warning: ignoring ...` line and the run. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_METASPACE_PEAK_AT_GC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The `Metaspace` pool's collection-edge samples are its peaks (`getPeakUsage` keeps the figure a collection saw after the classes are unloaded), and `resetPeakUsage` resets that peak. The t12 rows compared the line with HotSpot 25.0.3 under `-Xlog:gc`: `[info][gc] Allocation Stall (main) 51.223ms` against `[info][gc] Allocation Stall (main) 1.308ms` (same tags, level and shape); the stall count is judged as stall COLLECTIONS (`Zt12StStallRate`), since HotSpot also logs one line per other thread that waits on the same cycle. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_OVERHEAD_BACKSTOP` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | The GC-overhead crawl backstop on ZGC: eight consecutive forced collections on a heap under 2 % free, each of whose windows the live set grew by at least 1/16 of (`zgc_window_is_crawl`), and the next refusal throws after the soft-reference rung and the bounded grace. Ends the near-OOM one-collection-per-refusal crawl (`Zr2CpCapacity grow`: 3 302 collections with `=0`). A program working in a steady near-full heap (live flat) never counts. Not a HotSpot rule (HotSpot's overhead limit is Parallel-only). |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_ROOM_SKIPS_SOFT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The native factory door (no attempt between its rungs) skips the clear-soft rung when its forced collection was the only one since the arena refused the request and a single span now serves it: HotSpot's young, try, full, try. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_ROOM_SKIP_REPAY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | That skip is tracked per thread (one per-VM slot, taken only when free); a caller whose retry still fails (another thread took the span, or the native's own first allocation did) pays the skipped clear-soft rung (`last_ditch_reclaim` + second major) and retries once more before `OutOfMemoryError`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_OOM_REPLAY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: when a native's one-attempt allocation door refuses (the `NativeAllocOom` unwind) and the native's body is listed as restartable (`native-builtins/src/oom_restartable.rs`: `Class.getDeclaredFields0` / `getDeclaredMethods0` / `getDeclaredConstructors0` / `getMethods` / `getInterfaces0` / `getDeclaredClasses0` / `getName`, `Thread.getStackTrace0` / `dumpThreads` / `getThreads`, the `--compatible` `Integer.toString` / `Long.toString` / `Double.toString` / `Integer.toHexString`, `Object.getClass`, and sincethe `--compatible` `AbstractStringBuilder.getValue`; the list is frozen by `native-builtins/tests/oom_restartable_ratchet.rs`), the native funnel walks the native ladder's rungs and re-runs the native on its re-read (pinned) arguments after each, before `OutOfMemoryError`. Before, the rewrite skipped it ("rewrite target is not walkable ... SKIPPED -- any reference they hold to a moved object is now dangling"), a reference array's elements were omitted from the slot walk (dangling after a slide), the slide stopped at it (compaction starved) and its bytes were not counted live. `=0` restores all of that (positive control: the guard lines come back on `Zs1DgUnwalkableSurvivor prim`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_ONE_FREES_OVERSIZE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the per-object sweep (`sweep_one`: young cycles under `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`, and the fallback arm when the complement sweep refuses, e.g. `CRATONVM_ZGC_BITMAP_SWEEP=0` or `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_DEAD_RUNS=0`) frees a DEAD array that `alloc_size` refuses, in either region, once `oversize_array_extent`'s checks pass against the cycle's immutable snapshot (race-free under `CRATONVM_ZGC_PARSWEEP`) and the true low bump cursor. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_SPILL_HIGH_SPANS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A spilled dead base in the large-object end always pushes its free span; the "already reclaimed by the complement" test compared it against `Arena::used` (low cursor plus large-object bytes) and dropped the span while removing the registry bit, so the bytes were lost for good. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_OVERSIZE_SCREEN_MEMO` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The live over-long array screen (`oversize_live_array_extent`, a `size / 8`-bit registry scan) memoises its answer per `(base, length)` for one stop-the-world collection; a memoised positive re-checks the registry bit on every hit. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_ELEMENT_CARD_REFUSED_SCAN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1 CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_ELEMENT_CARDS=1`: a young cycle's element card on a reference array past 1 << 28 elements that the live screen refuses (its switch off, or a claimed extent that swallows a neighbour or runs past the arena) is scanned conservatively (the granule's own slots; only registered young targets are rooted) and kept. Before, the card was dropped without rooting its young targets. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_FULL_REQUEST_MAJOR` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: a full request (`System.gc`, `jcmd GC.run`) makes the collection whole-heap (`Major Collection (System.gc)`), as HotSpot 25's generational ZGC does. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_SOFT_CLEAR_MAJOR` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: a collection that clears every soft reference (the last-ditch rung before `OutOfMemoryError`, a per-VM clear-all request, the near-full soft clear `CRATONVM_ZGC_NEAR_FULL_SOFT_CLEAR_PERMILLE`) is made whole-heap through the collecting thread's full request (needs `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_FULL_REQUEST_MAJOR`, default on). A young cycle pre-marks the old generation, so it consumed the request and kept every old soft referent. Engagement: `majors_by_request=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_SKIP_REFERENCES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | default ON since zt t8: rows yr/yf (`Zt3GnYoungRefs`, `Zt7CcYoungFinalize` 8192 10) answer HotSpot 25.0.3's young window `kept=true` / `finalized=false` only with it on (`=0`: `kept=false`).* Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`. A collection the heap predicts young (`ZgcRealHeap::arm_young_refs_strong`: the inputs of `collect_garbage`'s young-cycle test, read before the VM's pre-collection reference pass) treats references as strong, as HotSpot 25's minor does: the pass nulls and condemns nothing, so every marker traces the referents, a young referent survives and nothing is cleared or enqueued; a dead young finalizable object is kept (with its closure) for the next major instead of being resurrected for `finalize`. Never armed while a clear-all-soft rule is pending. A wrong prediction is sound either way (an armed cycle that runs whole-heap clears its referents one major later). Engagement on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`: `young_refs_strong_cycles=`, `young_refs_mispredicted=`, `young_fin_deferred=`. Page `zgc-zt3gn-young-cycles-process-references-and-finalizers-FIXED.md`; row `Zt3GnYoungRefs` / `Zt7CcYoungFinalize`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_PREDICT_EXACT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` and the opt-in major rule `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MAJOR_PROMOTED_PERCENT` (the headroom rule it also covered was retired in); changes nothing without it. The young-cycle prediction behind `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_SKIP_REFERENCES` evaluates that rule exactly (the function `collect_garbage` calls, without its counters) instead of predicting a major whenever it is set, so their young cycles keep young referents and defer young finalizers as HotSpot 25's minor does. `=0` restores t7/cc's fail-closed prediction (every collection under such a rule unarmed: `young_refs_strong_cycles=0`). Row `Zt3GnYoungRefs 8192 10` with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MAJOR_PROMOTED_PERCENT` set (its t9 row named the headroom rule, retired in). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_ARMED_MAJOR_SOFT_CLOCK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`, on a collection `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_SKIP_REFERENCES` armed as young that `collect_garbage` ran whole-heap (`young_refs_mispredicted=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`). The young arm of the pre-collection pass skips the soft LRU condemnation, which is where a ZGC collection records the round's "now", so that major's reference round left `SoftReference.clock` at the previous major's value; HotSpot's major advances it. The round now notes the wall clock (`ReferenceProcessor::note_round_clock_now`, G1's remark device) and commits it. A minor is unchanged: it advances nothing, as HotSpot 25's minor (`Zt9GnSoftClock minor`, t9b SAME). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MAJOR_RULE_CAUSES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`, on a heap with an old generation: a collection whose HotSpot cause is `Warmup` or `Proactive` (HotSpot 25's major-only director rules) runs whole-heap. Before, the cycle kind ignored the cause, so a warm-up step after a `System.gc` (which tenures every survivor) ran young and `-Xlog:gc` printed `Minor Collection (Warmup)`, a line HotSpot does not print. The young-cycle prediction (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_SKIP_REFERENCES`) sees the same rule. Engagement: `majors_by_rule_cause=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_HOLDS_SOFT_FREE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`, on a young cycle that keeps its referents: the reference round leaves the soft LRU policy's free-at-last-gc figure (`SoftRefLRUPolicyMSPerMB x free MiB`) where the last major stored it, so a major after a minor judges idle soft references with the free heap after the last major. Decided by `Zt12Gn4SoftFree minor` against HotSpot 25 (replaced `Zt10Gn2SoftFree`, whose uncounted fill-time majors made the switch look inert): HotSpot `kept=true`; t16 rows `gn5_SF4_1h` / `_1hc` SAME `kept=true`, the off arm `gn5_SF4_1` / `_1c` `kept=false`. `=0`: a young round stores its own free figure, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_JNI_WEAK_STRONG` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`, on a young cycle that keeps its referents: every uncleared JNI weak global's referent is a root of the pause, so the post-pause weak-global sweep keeps the handle as the young cycle keeps a `java.lang.ref` referent, as HotSpot 25's minor does (`Zt10Gn2YoungJniWeak minor`: `cleared=false` on HotSpot and with the switch; `=0` clears a weak global whose young referent nothing else holds). |

Shards the young-aged sweep (`sweep_young_aged`, and the aged pass (1) of `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_COMPLEMENT=1`) over `n` threads at bitmap-word boundaries of the covered words; the generational survivor writes (age, old-start bit, promotion card) are per-object atomics the mutator barrier already runs concurrently. GN-17 of internal records. Engagement: `young_sweep_sharded=` / `young_sweep_shards=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`; the decision is `minor_sweep_us` and process CPU against `=1`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_COMPLEMENT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: a young cycle sweeps its nursery as the complement of its live set (a start bit with no mark bit is garbage: an address and a header store, never a header read) instead of one object at a time. t12 rows on `Zr1GnGenShape 64 400 200000 5 8 64`: CPU median 0.965x of `=0` over five interleaved pairs (max 0.976x), `zr1gn-check:` SAME as HotSpot, sweep per minor 2.5x / 7.8x / 3.5x lower. Engagement: `complement_sweeps=` (= `minor_cycles`), `complement_refusals=` and `complement_last_refusal=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_HIGH_YOUNG_LIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: a whole-heap cycle leaves its YOUNG survivors in the large-object region (allocations of 64 KiB or more) out of the old live figure, because the young cycles after it visit and count them (they lie above every young floor). They were counted twice, then a third time once promoted, until the next major: a heap of large arrays read up to twice its live set, so the live-bytes trigger fired early and the near-full soft clear saw a full heap. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_FLOOR_ARM_HIGH_OLD_LIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` and young cycles on the floor-bounded sweep (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_OLD_BITMAP=0` or `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_AGED_SWEEP=0`): a whole-heap cycle leaves its OLD survivors in the large-object region out of the old live figure as well, because the floor arm's young cycles sweep every base above the floor and count them themselves. They were counted twice until the next major (GN-18 of internal records). No effect on the default generational arms (bitmap and aged sweep on). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_SYSTEM_GC_TENURES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: the whole-heap cycle a `System.gc` door runs (with `CRATONVM_GC_SYSTEM_GC_TENURES_ALL`, default on, read on the collecting thread) ages every survivor straight to the promotion age (card + old-start bit, as an ordinary promotion), as HotSpot's full collection tenures live young data; the survivors used to stay young and were traced by up to `promotion_age - 1` more young cycles (GN-5). Engagement: `system_gc_tenures=` on `[GC] zgc-gen-cost:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_JIT_PROMOTION_FLOOR` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: the JIT reference-store plan publishes `promotion_age << 4` as its young floor, so a compiled reference store into any young receiver (age below the promotion age) skips the barrier helper, exactly the helper's own age test; it published age 0, so receivers of age `1..promotion_age-1` paid a call per store once the heap had an old generation (GN-7). Lowered before a lower promotion age, raised after a higher one. Deciding row: E-put of the generational proposal (`Zr1GnGenShape S1`, JIT on, CPU and `SP_REF_STORE_*` helper counts). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_POOL_LIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` and an old generation: the `ZGC Young Generation` pool counts the young survivors of the last collection (the live figure minus the last major's old live set and the promotions since) plus what was allocated since; the old pool the rest. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_EMPTY_MINOR_NEEDS_PROMOTION` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: a young cycle that freed nothing forces the next collection to a major (zr's escalation) only when something was promoted since the last major beyond that major's own promotions, when the cycle examined no live young object, or on the floor-bounded sweep arm. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_TIMER_NOT_WARMUP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: a cycle the director tick's `Timer` rule asked for (`-XX:ZCollectionInterval`) clears the warm-up bit an allocation poll may have raised before the tick's request landed, so it is neither a warm-up step (`warmup_cycles_done`) nor director warmth; HotSpot counts only `Warmup`-cause cycles and the cycle reports `Timer`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_TICK_WITHDRAW_OWN_BIT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: a director tick request whose pause lost to another thread's withdraws its cause bit only while the bit is its own (the request charged it and no collection completed since). `Proactive` is also the bit an allocation poll's director charges, so the t7 withdrawal could clear a poll's `Proactive` for the NEXT collection (`zgc-zt8x5-*` item 8). They warned and ignored the malformed ones. The Min / Max free-ratio cross-check stays in `run` (HotSpot checks it after the parse). Off ZGC unchanged. `=0`: those arms as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TICK_NOTIFICATIONS_OFF_THREAD` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: a director tick collection hands its GC notifications to the delivery thread as the metadata door does, on `--compatible` too (whose rule hands off only a lock-holding door's), so no `NotificationListener` runs on the attached `ZDirector` thread (and no `java.lang.Thread` is built for it). HotSpot 25 runs them on its `Notification Thread` (local 25.0.3, `Zt11Tg4TickNotificationThread`). `--jdk-only` already handed them off; `CRATONVM_FINALIZER_THREAD=0` still delivers inline. `=0`: the rule of every other door. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_UNDELIVERABLE_NOTIFICATIONS_OFF_THREAD` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC, `--compatible`: a door that cannot deliver its queued GC notifications now -- the allocation-failure door (never), `maybe_gc` or the ref-work drain inside a JIT borrow (the drain defers) -- hands them to the delivery thread, lock held or not, as the metadata door does. Before, a lock-free door left them for "the next drain point", which a compiled allocation loop followed by a `Thread.sleep` wait never reaches: `Zt11Gn3GenParity` with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` printed `causes=[]` (no notification on the `ZGC Minor Cycles` listener in 5 s) where `--jdk-only` and HotSpot 25 deliver `[Allocation Rate]` on their notification thread.
