| `CRATONVM_ZGC_NOTIFICATION_MISSED_REDRAIN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: one drainer at a time delivers the queued GC notifications, and a drain point that finds another drain in progress returns, trusting that drain's loop. A notification queued after that loop's last pop waited for the next drain point -- for a `--compatible` program that sleeps after a lock-free door collected while the delivery thread was finishing a batch (t12/gn4's hand-offs made that a frequent second drainer), or after `System.gc`'s forced drain lost that race, never; HotSpot's Notification Thread delivers every one. Now a refused drain point leaves a mark (`GcNotificationQueue::begin_drain_noting_miss`, a `SeqCst` store and fence on each side, so the refused side's retry wins the drain or the drainer sees the mark) and the drainer, having let go, drains again when the mark is set and anything is pending, at most 4 chained re-drains per drain point. A refused build (a short heap) still ends the drain for good. G1 and Generational keep the plain door. Rows `x10_N1*` of internal records. `=0`: the next drain point, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TICK_STOP_WAITS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: the launcher's main-thread exit arms (main returned, the `-version` boot) stop the director tick and then wait, inside the main thread's blocked region and for at most 10 s, for a tick pause that was already past its last stop check (`wait_for_zgc_tick_pause`), so its `GC(n)` lines print before the `gc,exit` summary, as HotSpot's director stop waits for its running cycle (`t11-x8-report.md` C12). The `System.exit` arm stops without waiting. `=0`: no wait. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TIMER_SKIPS_STALL_CYCLES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: an allocation stall's collection (the collecting thread marked by its forced site, `gc_quiescence::with_allocation_stall_door`) does not restart the `Timer` rule's clock (`ZTimer::note_cycle_end_as`). HotSpot 25 runs a stall's collection as a minor, and its major timer reads the old generation's last cycle, so with `-XX:ZCollectionInterval=1` a churn that stalls several times a second still gets a `Timer` cycle each second (local 25.0.3, `-XX:+ZCollectionIntervalOnly`: causes `[Allocation Stall, Timer]`); here every stall restarted the clock and no `Timer` cycle came (row TG3-O4). Only reachable with `-XX:ZCollectionInterval`. `=0`: every whole-heap cycle restarts the clock. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TICK_EXIT_NATIVE_WAITS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: the exit natives (`System.exit`, `Runtime.exit`, `Shutdown.halt0`, the bottom of `System.exit` under `--jdk-only`) stop the director tick and wait, in a VM-wait region on the exiting thread and for at most 10 s, for a tick pause that was already past its last stop check (`NativeContext::zgc_stop_director_tick_before_exit`), before the pre-exit hook prints the `gc,exit` summary; `CRATONVM_ZGC_TICK_STOP_WAITS=0` also turns this wait off. HotSpot's `before_exit` stops its director (aborting the running cycle) on every exit path before the summary. `=0`: the exit arm only stops, as in t11. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_MISSING_VALUE_REFUSALS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector (the launcher). A launcher option given without its value ends the launch with the JDK 25 launcher's line on stderr and exit 1, before every VM option's refusal: `Error: --add-modules requires modules to be specified` (also `--add-exports`, `--add-opens`, `--add-reads`, `--patch-module`, `--limit-modules`, `--enable-native-access`, and the empty `=` forms `--add-modules=`, `--add-exports=`, `--add-reads=`, `--patch-module=`, `--limit-modules=`, `--enable-native-access=`, `--module-path=`, `--upgrade-module-path=`), `Error: --module-path requires module path specification` (`-p`, `--upgrade-module-path`), `Error: -cp requires class path specification` (`-classpath`, `--class-path`), `Error: -m requires module name` (`--module`, `--module=`). As on HotSpot a separate value starting with `-` is a missing value, and `-version` / `--help` before the option ends the scan. Also reads `--class-path <path>` / `--class-path=<path>` as the class path (clap refused both). Sincean empty separate value (`--add-opens ""`, `-p ""`, `-m ""`) is a missing value too, as libjli's `has_arg`; only the class path takes one (`-cp ""` boots). `=0`: clap's `error: a value is required ...`, exit 2, where clap parses (`--add-modules --jdk-only Hello`); a `-version` launch prints its banner, rc 0 (the version path runs before clap). |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector (the launcher). `JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS` is read as the JDK 9+ `java` launcher reads it (libjli `JLI_AddArgsFromEnvVar`): `NOTE: Picked up JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS: <value>` on stderr first, then its tokens -- split at white space, a `"`/`'` run kept whole without its quotes, `@argfiles` expanded as on the command line -- go in after `argv[0]`, before the command line's own arguments, so every launcher stage (the missing-value scan, the module launch, `getInputArguments`) sees them. Refused as HotSpot refuses them, exit 1: an unclosed quote (`Error: unmatched quote in environment variable JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS`), `-jar`, `-m`, `--module[=]`, `--dry-run`, the help, `-X` and version options (`Error: Option <opt> is not allowed in environment variable JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS`, or `... <opt> in @<file> is not allowed ...` from an argfile), a main class (`Error: Cannot specify main class in environment variable JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS`). Before, the variable was ignored. The switch is read from the process environment directly (the launcher needs it before the flag snapshot exists), so only `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS=0` itself kills it. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_BOOT_LAYER_OPTION_REFUSALS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector (the launcher). A `-jar` with nothing after it prints `Error: -jar requires jar file specification` and the full usage (`java.launcher.opt.header` and `footer`; libjli's `ARG_CHECK`), before any VM option is checked. `=0`: this launcher's own `No class name or --jar specified` error, and clap's refusal of the bare `-jar` (exit 2). |

An obsolete HotSpot flag -- `ZGenerational` (obsoleted in 24, JEP 490), in any spelling (`-XX:+`, `-XX:-`, `=value`, bare) -- prints HotSpot 25's `OpenJDK 64-Bit Server VM warning: Ignoring option ZGenerational; support was removed in 24.0` on stderr, in command-line order and only for the options before the first refused one (HotSpot stops its parse there), ahead of every banner and startup line. The table is `cratonvm_vm::config::HOTSPOT_OBSOLETE_FLAGS`. `=0`: the flag is dropped silently, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_LIMIT_FLAG_SYNTAX` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Under `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_FLAG_HOTSPOT_PARSE`, on ZGC (and on every collector under `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_FLAGS_ANY_COLLECTOR`): `-XX:ZFragmentationLimit` and `-XX:ZYoungCompactionLimit` take HotSpot 25's `double` syntax in the ordered first-refusal scan -- a value outside it (`=abc`, `=`) or the bare name is `Improperly specified VM option '<option>'` and the two `Error:` lines, exit 1, as `ZAllocationSpikeTolerance`. The values themselves are still ignored (recorded in `docs/GC.md`, ZGC paragraph G); HotSpot's `0 ... 100` range is `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_LIMIT_FLAG_RANGE` . `=0`: both dropped silently, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_JVMTI_ONLOAD_CLASS_UNLOADING` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 28e).* Every collector, both modes, per VM. JVMTI's `com.sun.hotspot.functions.IsClassUnloadingEnabled` asked by a startup agent in `Agent_OnLoad`, before the VM exists, answers the VM's effective `-XX:±ClassUnloading` / `-Xnoclassgc` (the same bit `CRATONVM_CLASS_UNLOADING_OFF_KEEPS_CLASSES` installs, noted in the OnLoad scope before the agents load), as HotSpot reads the parsed `ClassUnloading` flag in any phase. It answered `JNI_TRUE` there under `-Xnoclassgc`. Probe `Zt23X19OnLoadUnloading` (needs its C shim as `-agentpath`). `=0`: `JNI_TRUE` without a VM, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_LIMIT_FLAG_RANGE` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts with `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_LIMIT_FLAG_SYNTAX`) | * `-XX:ZFragmentationLimit` / `-XX:ZYoungCompactionLimit` outside HotSpot 25's `range(0, 100)` print the `double` range line (`double ZFragmentationLimit=200.000000 is outside the allowed range [ 0.000000 ... 100.000000 ]`), `Improperly specified VM option 'ZFragmentationLimit=200'` and the two `Error:` lines, exit 1, in the ordered first-refusal scan. The text is recalled (`FlagAccessImpl_double::range_error`); row `h7_L3` decides. `=0`: an out-of-range value boots, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_THP_FLAG_LINUX_ONLY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Off Linux, `-XX:±UseTransparentHugePages` (any spelling, `=<value>` kept in the name) is `Unrecognized VM option 'UseTransparentHugePages'` and the two `Error:` lines, exit 1, in the ordered first-refusal scan, as on HotSpot 25, which declares the flag in `globals_linux.hpp` only; `-XX:+IgnoreUnrecognizedVMOptions` anywhere in the options lets it pass. Every collector. `=0`: dropped silently, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_LARGE_PAGE_FLAG_SIGN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * `-XX:UseLargePages` and, on Linux, `-XX:UseTransparentHugePages` without a sign (bare or `=<value>`) are HotSpot 25's `Missing +/- setting for VM option '<option>'`, and a signed spelling with a value (`-XX:+UseLargePages=true`) is `Improperly specified VM option 'UseLargePages=true'`, each with the two `Error:` lines, exit 1, in the ordered first-refusal scan (host row `h7_W1e`, 25.0.4). Every collector. `=0`: dropped silently, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_HELP_JVM_CFG_VMS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * The full usage (`-help` / `-h` / `-?`, `--help`, a bare `-jar`) lists, between `java.launcher.opt.header` and the footer, the VMs of the class library's `lib/jvm.cfg` past the first (default) one, as libjli's `PrintUsage`: each `KNOWN` VM's ` -<vm>\t to select the "<vm>" VM` line, then each `ALIASED_TO` VM's `is a synonym for` line. JDK 25's own file (`-server KNOWN`, `-client IGNORE`) gives none; Ubuntu's `openjdk-25` adds `-zero` and `-dcevm`, which its HotSpot prints (host rows `tg8_h4`, `tg8_h15`, `tg10_h1`, `tg10_h3`). The JDK is the default search's (`JAVA_HOME`, `PATH`). Every collector. `=0`: no such line, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_TOO_SMALL_INITIAL_HEAP_REFUSED` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * HotSpot 25's `GCArguments::initialize_heap_flags_and_sizes` floors, after `Too small maximum heap`: an initial heap given below 1 MiB (`-Xms`, `-XX:InitialHeapSize`) is `Error occurred during initialization of VM` / `Too small initial heap`, then a minimum heap below 1 MiB (`-Xms`, `-XX:MinHeapSize`) `Too small minimum heap`, on stdout, exit 1, `-version` included (host row `tg8_i4`: HotSpot refused `-Xms512k -version`). 0 is not set (ergonomics). Every collector. `=0`: such a value boots, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_HEAP_STATISTICS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_PAUSE_PHASES` on, under `-Xlog:gc+heap`: a ZGC collection prints HotSpot 25.0.3's per-generation `gc,heap` block -- the `Min / Max / Soft Max Capacity` triple and the `Heap Statistics:` table (`Capacity`, `Free`, `Used` at `Mark Start`, `Mark End`, `Relocate Start`, `Relocate End`, `High`, `Low`; HotSpot's column layout byte for byte) -- under `Y:` then `O:` on a major, `y:` on a minor. The stop-the-world collection's six points: the occupancy before the pause for the first three, after it for `Relocate End`; `Free` is `max - used` with the free space's own high / low (as `ZStatHeap`). The per-generation statistics tables are `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GENERATION_STATISTICS`'s. `=0`: t6/ob4's single `O:` / `y:` triple. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GENERATION_STATISTICS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_HEAP_STATISTICS`: each ZGC `gc,heap` generation block ends with HotSpot 25.0.3's `Young Generation Statistics:` (9 lines: `Used`, `Live`, `Garbage`, `Allocated`, `Reclaimed`, `Promoted`, `Compacted` over `Mark Start` .. `Relocate End`, `-` where HotSpot has no figure) or `Old Generation Statistics:` (8 lines, no `Promoted`), layout byte for byte from a local `-Xlog:gc*` capture. A major's young block promotes every survivor (`Used` before -> 0, `Promoted` = after), its `O:` block reads the occupancy after the young half (heap table and `Used` = after, `Live` = after, no garbage); a major whose cause HotSpot's `should_preclean_young` lists (`System.gc`, `Diagnostic Command`, `Allocation Stall`, heap dump / inspection, JVMTI, WhiteBox, `Metadata GC Clear Soft References`) prints two young blocks (HotSpot's `Promote All` and `Collect Roots`), the second empty: 50 `gc,heap` lines for a `System.gc` major, 33 for another, 17 for a minor, as HotSpot. `Allocated` and `Compacted` print 0 (the stop-the-world pause allocates nothing; the slide's copy volume does not reach the logger). `=0`: t7/x4b's one `Y:` and one `O:` block over the whole-heap figures. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GEN_YOUNG_FIGURES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GENERATION_STATISTICS`, only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1` (no minor is printed otherwise): a minor's `y:` `Young Generation Statistics:` table counts the young generation only, as HotSpot 25's does -- `Used` is the heap before the minor less the old generation before it (the last major's old live set plus the promotions since), relocate-end `Used` the survivors kept young, `Live` those plus the bytes this minor promoted, `Garbage` / `Reclaimed` the rest, `Promoted` this minor's promotions. The `Heap Statistics:` table above it stays whole-heap. Sincethe old figure leaves out the young survivors below the promotion floor (still counted in the old live set), and the split is offered only after a minor on the young-aged sweep (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_AGED_SWEEP`); after any other minor the table prints the whole-heap figures, as under `=0`. `=0`: t9/x6's whole-heap figures (`Used` before -> after, `Promoted` 0). Item 8 of internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GENERATION_ORDER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_PAUSE_PHASES` on: a ZGC major prints its `gc,phases`, `gc,metaspace` and `gc,heap` lines generation by generation, as HotSpot 25.0.3's `-Xlog:gc*` -- the `Y:` pause line, a `Y: Metaspace:` line before each young `gc,heap` block, then the `O:` pause lines, the `O: Metaspace:` line and the `O:` block. A minor is unchanged. `=0`: every pause line, one `O:` metaspace line, then every block (the t7 order). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_PHASE_FRAMES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC collection's `gc,phases` block is HotSpot 25.0.4's: each generation collection between its frame lines (`Y: Young Generation` ... `Y: Young Generation <before>M(<pct>%)-><after>M(<pct>%) <s>s`, `O: Old Generation` ..., `y:` for a minor), with every phase line of HotSpot's cycle -- `Pause Mark Start (Major)`, `Concurrent Mark`, `Pause Mark End`, `Concurrent Mark Free`, `Concurrent Process Non-Strong`, `Concurrent Reset Relocation Set`, `Concurrent Select Relocation Set`, `Concurrent Remap Roots`, `Pause Relocate Start`, `Concurrent Relocate` -- and a pre-cleaning major (`System.gc`, JVMTI, heap dump / inspection, `Allocation Stall`, ...) runs two young collections, `(Promote All)` then `(Collect Roots)`. Each line carries the time the phase took here: the pauses from the pause split, `O: Concurrent Mark` the closed concurrent cycle's phase (failed Pause Mark End attempts apart), `0.000ms` for a phase this collector runs inside a pause or not at all. Each generation's `gc,metaspace` / `gc,heap` lines print before its frame end, as HotSpot's `-Xlog:gc*` does. `=0`: the pause lines alone, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_INIT_CAPACITY_MIB` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC's `-Xlog:gc+init` `Min Capacity`, `Initial Capacity`, `Max Capacity` and `Soft Max Capacity` lines print whole MiB, as HotSpot 25.0.3's `%zuM` does (`-Xmx1g`: `Max Capacity: 1024M`; `-XX:SoftMaxHeapSize=100k`: `Soft Max Capacity: 0M`). They printed the exact size (`1G`, `100K`). `=0`: the exact size. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DIRECTOR_REARM_FLOOR` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | Acts only under `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER=1`. A director rule's yes waits for the same `gc_rearm` anti-storm floor as the occupancy clauses. Without it, the high-usage rule (live set above 95% of the soft max) and the rate rule (free space below its reserve) fire again right after every cycle, with no new allocation. Engagement: `director_deferred=` on `[GC] zgc-trigger:`, beside the director's own tally `director=`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DIRECTOR_STW_MODEL` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | The director's rules translated to a stop-the-world, byte-polled cycle. The rate rule reserves only the poll latency (one sample step + 4 MiB), with no cycle-duration term and no `max_rate x 0.1 s` term. The proactive rule assumes a full throughput drop while a cycle runs. Acts under `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER=1` / `CRATONVM_ZGC_TRIGGER_SHADOW=1`, and only without concurrent marking. Proposal `zgc-zs1tp-proposal-director-stw-model`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_EXCLUDES_PAUSE` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | The ZGC metrics' allocation rate (`rate_bps` / `ttl_ms` / `ratio` on `[GC] zgc-trigger-shadow:`, the `[GC-SUMMARY] zgc` rate, the TSV) is measured over mutator time: each cycle's stop-the-world pause is subtracted from the inter-cycle interval (every lap of the pause, the mark-end lap of a concurrently marked cycle included --; the concurrent phase itself is in no lap and stays in the interval). No collection trigger reads this rate. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DIRECTOR_HOTSPOT_WARMTH` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | Acts only where the director samples (`CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER=1` / `CRATONVM_ZGC_TRIGGER_SHADOW=1`). Warmth is counted in warm-up cycles, as HotSpot's `ZStatCycle` does: the allocation-rate rule waits for the first warm-up cycle (`is_time_trustable`), the proactive rule for the third (`is_warm`). A `System.gc` or an allocation stall is not a warm-up cycle. Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_WARMUP=0` every cycle counts. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SOFT_MAX_PACES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `-XX:SoftMaxHeapSize` below `-Xmx`, the live-bytes trigger also fires at 75% of the soft maximum, not only at 75% of `-Xmx`, and the re-arm floor is a quarter of the soft headroom . HotSpot's ZGC reads the soft maximum in every director rule; the `=0` arm reads it only in the warm-up clause. Inert without `-XX:SoftMaxHeapSize` (or with it at or above `-Xmx`). Flip evidence (t16 verdict pr2 T10): `Zt1TpSoftMaxPaces` `peak=near` SAME in both modes (the `=0` arm prints HotSpot-divergent `peak=far`); engaged `gc_count` 84 vs HotSpot 98. Proposal `zgc-zt1tp-proposal-soft-max-paces-the-default-trigger`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_WARMUP_HOTSPOT_STEPS` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | The warm-up clause counts its 10/20/30% steps in warm-up cycles rather than collections, so a `System.gc` is not a step, as on HotSpot. It also waits for at most half a step of new allocation instead of the `gc_rearm` floor, which had pushed the second warm-up from 20% to ~26%. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START_RECHECK` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | A concurrent mark start re-tests that it is still due before requesting its pause, so the loser of the open race does not stop the world for a cycle that is already open. Counted as `ct_lost_races=` / `ct_start_rechecks=` on `[GC] zgc-conc-trigger:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_LAUNCHER_OOME_THROWABLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: an `OutOfMemoryError` while the launcher builds an instance `main`'s receiver is thrown as `main`'s uncaught `java.lang.OutOfMemoryError` (vm-cli prints `Exception in thread "main" ...`, rc 1, as HotSpot) instead of `[cratonvm] main-vm run returned Err: ...`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_FFM_MINT_COLLECT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only (with `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_DOORS_COLLECT` on): the FFM segment minted for a downcall's `ADDRESS` return after the C call (memo, JIT direct-stub and general paths) and for an upcall's address argument takes the fallible door and the reclaim ladder, then raises `OutOfMemoryError`; the one-attempt door it replaces aborted the process when a JIT helper made the downcall. on ZGC this key (and `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_DOORS_COLLECT`) is its kill again (`panama::memo_address_return_collects`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PROXY_INSTANCE_COLLECT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only (with `CRATONVM_ZGC_NATIVE_DOORS_COLLECT` on): the `Proxy.newProxyInstance` native (`reflect_annotations::native_proxy_new_instance`) mints the proxy instance through the fallible door and the reclaim ladder, then raises `OutOfMemoryError` (HotSpot allocates it in Java, `cons.newInstance`, which collects first); the one-attempt door raised it without a collection. Generational and G1 keep the one-attempt door. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_ADDCLASS_PRESIZE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the `--compatible` `ClassLoader.defineClass` backend (`classloader::define_class_via_full`) makes room in the loader's `classes` list (`ArrayList.add(null)` under the list's monitor, then `size` put back) BEFORE the mirror reservation and the define, when the list is full, so the `ClassLoader.addClass` it runs after the define allocates nothing. A refused growth is the define's `OutOfMemoryError` with nothing defined, as on HotSpot (`define_instance_class` calls `addClass` before the dictionary update); before, the class stayed defined and a retry met `LinkageError: duplicate class definition`. Generational and G1 unchanged. defineClass` reaches in either mode; the presize now also runs at the real-JDK doors that make the upcall (`CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS`), and only while that switch is on. |
| `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector (moves both modes to HotSpot; G1 and Generational take it alike): the real-JDK define doors (`lang_system`'s `ClassLoader.defineClass0/1/2`, which serve `ClassLoader.defineClass*`, `Lookup.defineClass` and JNI `DefineClass` in both `--jdk-only` and `--compatible`; `Unsafe.defineClass0`; `--compatible`'s own `Lookup.defineClass` door) call `ClassLoader.addClass(Class)` for every non-hidden class a loader object defines, after the define, as HotSpot's `SystemDictionary::define_instance_class` does. Before, the loader's private `classes` list stayed empty in both modes. The upcall's failure (its list growth's `OutOfMemoryError`) is the define's. On ZGC `CRATONVM_ZGC_ADDCLASS_PRESIZE` now runs at these doors too, so the growth happens before the define. A loader object whose `classes` is null (a VM-made loader) is skipped. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CLASS_NAME_UNPOOLED` | on (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the mirror `name` String of a class that a loader object defined (one with a defining-loader row: the classes that can unload) is not added to the interned-string pool, a strong root on ZGC, so it goes with the class when the loader unloads. Not Java-visible: the slot holds this VM's internal-name overlay (`pkg/Foo`), which HotSpot does not have, and `Class.getName` interns its own answer, so `getName == "Foo"` is unchanged. Read once per process. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_UNLOAD_CLASS_NAME_MEMO` | on (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the class-unload transaction (`unload_dead_class_metadata`) drops the `Class.getName` memo rows (`CRATONVM_CLASS_GET_NAME_MEMO`) of the classes it removed and releases their JNI global roots. Each row is an `Object[2]` holding the mirror's name String and the interned binary name, and the JDK's `postDefineClass` -> `getPackageName` -> `getName` fills one for every class `defineClass` defines, so every unloaded class used to leave 1 `Object[]` + 2 Strings live for good (`Zt7ClClassNames`: 143 B/define, HotSpot 0). Not Java-visible (an unloaded class id is never named again). Read per unload transaction that removed classes. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On a ZGC heap that collects stop-the-world only (no concurrent mark configured: `zgc_weak_string_table_sound`; generational mode too sinceunless `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_WEAK_STRING_TABLE=0`), the interned-String pool is a weak root swept after each collection; elsewhere it stays strong. Rows (local, against HotSpot 25.0.3): `Zr1RfInternProbe weakclear|churn`, `GcgW2InternProbe reclaim|identity|literal|unload` SAME in both modes; 20 runs each of `Zr1RfInternProbe churn` (-Xmx64m) and `MtChurnProbe 8 100 96` clean; interleaved CPU 0.28-0.89x of the strong pool; `Zt7ClClassNames 40 1000 3` growth 56 -> 0 B/define. `=0`: the strong pool (`churn` at -Xmx64m throws OutOfMemoryError). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE_STRONG_LATCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * The ZGC arm of the weak interned-String table asks `VmHeap::zgc_weak_string_table_sound` at every root scan and every `ldc` record decision. With the latch its first "strong" answer holds for the VM's life (`StringTableState::zgc_sound_now`): moving from weak to strong is sound, the other way is not (a literal resolved while the pool was strong has no `ldc` record, so a `WeakReference` to it could clear while its class lives), and an embedder calling `ZgcRealHeap::set_generational_enabled(false)` on a running heap made that move. A launcher run never moves (every input of the soundness test is fixed for it), so no row can show a difference; unit test `zt13x10_the_zgc_arm_latches_its_first_strong_answer`. `=0`: the arm follows the heap both ways, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_ATTACH_SELF` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector (the counters are HotSpot's on each): `jdk.internal.perf.Perf.attach0(0)` (or this process's id) answers a direct buffer over this VM's own jvmstat perf-data view, as HotSpot's `PerfMemory::attach` does, so `VMManagementImpl.getInternalCounters` (the `sun.management` `HotspotRuntime` / `HotspotThread` / `HotspotMemory` / `HotspotClassLoading` internal counters) lists what `jstat` reads, now including `sun.rt.safepoints` / `safepointTime` / `safepointSyncTime` / `applicationTime` (the GC barrier's safepoint accounting; 0 under `CRATONVM_JMX_SAFEPOINT_COUNTERS=0`), `sun.threads.vmOperationTime` (stopped minus time-to-safepoint) and `sun.threads.total_gc_cpu_time` / `sun.threads.cpu_time.vm` (0, as HotSpot 25.0.3 ZGC on Windows). Values refresh every 100 ms (the perf-data sampler), and at once after an explicit collection (`CRATONVM_PERF_EVENT_REFRESH`,). Once handed out the view stays mapped until the process ends. Nothing without perf data (`-XX:-UsePerfData`, `CRATONVM_PERF_DATA_FILE=0`); under `-XX:+PerfDisableSharedMem` the image is kept in memory (`CRATONVM_PERF_DATA_IN_MEMORY`,). `=0`: the zero-capacity buffer (`noPerfData`, an empty list for every pattern). Probe `Zt9PlPerfCounters`. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_ATTACH_FOREIGN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector: `jdk.internal.perf.Perf.attach0(<pid>)` for ANOTHER VM's id maps that VM's published perf data read-only (Unix: `<tmp>/hsperfdata_<user>/<pid>`, a regular file; Windows: the named mapping `hsperfdata_<user>_<pid>`, sized by its backing file), as HotSpot's `PerfMemory::attach` does for a foreign id; jvmstat's local monitoring (`MonitoredHost.getMonitoredVm`, and with it `VirtualMachine.list`'s display names) reads other VMs through it. The mapping is recorded per VM and unmapped by `Perf.detach` (the cleaner `Perf.attach` registers); this VM's own view is never unmapped. Only this user's VMs. No such VM: the zero-capacity buffer, as before (HotSpot throws there; that difference is `zgc-zt10pl2-*`). `=0`: the zero-capacity buffer for every foreign id. Probe `Zt10Pl2ForeignAttach`. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_DATA_IN_MEMORY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector: `-XX:+PerfDisableSharedMem` with `UsePerfData` on (the last of each `+`/`-` pair in the launcher's options) keeps the jvmstat image in process memory, as HotSpot keeps its perf memory in the C heap under that flag: no file and no named mapping (so `jps` / `VirtualMachine.list` do not list the VM), but the sampler keeps it live and `Perf.attach(0)` hands it out, so the in-process `sun.management` internal counters answer. An embedder sets no launcher options and gets no image. `=0`: no perf data under the flag, as before. Probe `Zt9PlPerfCounters` with `-XX:+PerfDisableSharedMem`. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_EVENT_REFRESH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector: when an explicit collection returns (`System.gc`, `Runtime.gc`, `MemoryMXBean.gc`: the `force_gc` door), the calling thread writes `sun.rt.safepoints` / `safepointTime` / `safepointSyncTime` / `applicationTime`, `sun.threads.vmOperationTime` and `sun.gc.collector.<n>.invocations` / `.time` into the perf-data image (the collector figures as the `GarbageCollectorMXBean` natives read them), as HotSpot updates them at the pause, instead of at the sampler's next pass (up to 100 ms later). These event-refreshed counters never go back while the switch is on (a stale sampler pass keeps the larger value;: only they, so `sun.cls.loadedBytes` follows unloads again). Collections the VM starts itself reach the image at the sampler's pass, except on ZGC (at the pause: `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_PAUSE_REFRESH`,). `=0`: the sampler alone, every counter stored as written. Probe `Zt9PlPerfCounters` (reads right after `System.gc`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_PAUSE_REFRESH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: every collection pause (`run_collection_pause`, after `complete_gc` and the bean update, before the GC-notification wake-up) writes the same counters `CRATONVM_PERF_EVENT_REFRESH` writes after an explicit collection, so a `GarbageCollectorMXBean` notification listener reading the in-process counters sees the collection that notified it, as on HotSpot -- when the delivery follows this pause's own wake-up (a delivery thread an earlier hint already woke can run first: `zgc-zt12x9-wave-t11-review-residuals-SUPERSEDED.md`). Off with `CRATONVM_PERF_EVENT_REFRESH=0` too. Other collectors unchanged. `=0`: allocation-, trigger- and timer-started collections reach the image at the sampler's next pass (up to 100 ms). Probe `Zt12Pl4PausePoll` (: the allocating thread reads the pause beans and the counters itself; the `=0` kill must print `perf_in_step=false`). `Zt11Pl3PerfCounters`' `pause_refresh` line cannot show the kill: its listener runs after the delivery thread built the notification, by when the sampler has usually written the counters (t11 rows `pl3_R0-*`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_REFRESH_BEFORE_ENQUEUE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * ZGC: `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_PAUSE_REFRESH`'s refresh runs in `run_collection_pause` after `gc_event_finish_by` queued the collection's GC notifications, so a delivery thread an earlier hint had woken could run a listener that read the in-process collector counters one collection behind. With this switch `gc_events::record_gc_notification` runs the same refresh after the beans' final update and before the first record is queued, and only when a record is about to be queued for a listener (a VM without GC listeners pays nothing more); the late refresh stays. Row `x10_P1*` (`Zt11Pl3PerfCounters`' listener-read `pause_refresh` line, 20 loaded repeats) of internal records. `=0`: the late refresh alone, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_COLLECTOR_SLOTS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: `sun.gc.collector.0` is the `ZGC Minor Pauses` bean and `sun.gc.collector.2` the `ZGC Major Pauses` bean (count and time), as HotSpot 25's ZGC perf collectors are its pause counters (`ZGC minor collection pauses` / `ZGC major collection pauses`, measured), so `jstat -gc` `YGC` / `CGC` count pauses; `sun.gc.collector.1` stays 0 (: left out of the image, `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_IMAGE_SHAPE`). Other collectors unchanged. `=0`: the first three beans in order (`ZGC Minor Cycles`, `ZGC Minor Pauses`, `ZGC Major Cycles`). Probe `Zt11Pl3PerfCounters` (`slots` line). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_IMAGE_SHAPE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC: the perf-data image (the `hsperfdata` file, the in-memory image, `Perf.attach(0)`) has HotSpot 25's ZGC counter set (measured, Windows 25.0.3 `jcmd PerfCounter.print`): no `sun.gc.collector.1.*` (HotSpot's ZGC has collectors 0 and 2 only, so `jstat -gc` `FGC`/`FGCT` print `-`), one space per generation (no `sun.gc.generation.0.space.1/2.*`: `S0C`/`S1C`/`S0U`/`S1U` print `-`), no `sun.os.hrt.ticks`, and the names `sun.gc.collector.0.name` / `.2.name` (`ZGC minor/major collection pauses`), `sun.gc.generation.0/1.name` (`young`/`old`), `.spaces` (1) and `.space.0.name` (`space`). Probe `Zt10Pl2CounterCensus` (rows `census-*` in `t12-pl4-report.md`). |

| `CRATONVM_PERF_DATA_IN_MEMORY_FALLBACK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | default ON since zt t31 (rows `31nofile*`).* Every collector: when the perf-data file cannot be published (an unsafe or unwritable `hsperfdata` directory), keep the image in process memory (`create_in_memory_for_vm`) so `Perf.attach(0)`, the in-process `sun.management` counters and the sampler still work, as HotSpot 25's `PerfMemory::create_memory_region` reverts to non-shared memory (`create_standard_memory`) on POSIX and Windows. A name another VM of this process already published (a CratonVM-only case: a process hosting many VMs) gets no image unless `=1`. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_HOTSPOT_COUNTERS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Every collector: right after the perf-data image is made (`perf_data_file::add_hotspot_counters`), the HotSpot 25 counters it lacked that have a real source here are appended to it, as HotSpot adds counters to a live perf memory (entry bytes, then `used`, then the entry count): `sun.gc.metaspace.minCapacity` / `sun.gc.compressedclassspace.minCapacity` (constant 0) and their `maxCapacity` (the reserved figures, sampled), `sun.rt.createVmEndTime` / `vmInitDoneTime` (written when the boot ends: init level 4 or `mark_boot_finished`), `sun.rt.jvmVersion` (HotSpot's byte layout of `java.vm.version`), `sun.rt.internalVersion` (`<vm name> (<release>) for <os>-<cpu> JRE (<runtime version>)`, no build stamp), `java.property.{java.class.path, java.home, java.library.path, java.version, jdk.debug}` and `sun.property.sun.boot.library.path` (the property values at boot). On a ZGC-shaped image (`CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_IMAGE_SHAPE`) also `sun.gc.generation.<g>.capacity` (sampled, = the space's), `minCapacity` / `maxCapacity` and `space.0.initCapacity` / `maxCapacity` (`[MinHeapSize, InitialHeapSize, MaxHeapSize]`: young min / init, old 0, both max), `sun.gc.collector.0/2.lastEntryTime` / `lastExitTime` (the pause beans' last collection, ticks since VM start) and `sun.gc.cause` (`No GC`: a collection runs with the world stopped) / `sun.gc.lastCause` (the cause of the cycle that ended last). threads.started` / `live` / `livePeak` / `daemon` (`ThreadMXBean`'s four figures, from the thread registry's JMM roster: `ThreadRegistry::jmm_perf_thread_counts`, sampled) and `sun.cls.initializedClasses` / `linkedClasses` / `verifiedClasses` (the `<clinit>` step and link attempts the VM counts; with no shared classes a link verifies) and `sun.cls.sharedClassLoadTime` (0: nothing is shared), and, with the JIT on, `java.ci.totalTime` (the compile time `CompilationMXBean` answers, in ticks), `sun.ci.totalCompiles` / `osrCompiles` / `standardCompiles` (the JIT's published bodies), `sun.ci.lastMethod` / `lastType` (the last of them, `"<class> <method>"`, 1 normal / 2 OSR) and the constant `sun.ci.threads` (the compile workers). gc.tlab.*` counters (`allocThreads`, `fills`, `maxFills`, `alloc`, `gcWaste`, `maxGcWaste`, `refillWaste`, `maxRefillWaste`, `slowAlloc`, `maxSlowAlloc`: HotSpot's `ThreadLocalAllocStats::publish` figures for the last collection with a refilling thread, the allocation and the wastes in heap words; computed with the world stopped at each collection's start, `perf_data_file::publish_tlab_stats`, from the per-thread TLAB cycle counters and the VM's refill-byte total) and `sun.cls.defineAppClasses` / `appClassBytes` / `unsafeDefineClassCalls` (counted by the `defineClass1` / `defineClass2` / `Unsafe.defineClass0` natives once they hold the bytes, as `jvm_define_class_common` / `Unsafe_DefineClass_impl` count). ci.standardTime` / `osrTime` (the published method-entry / OSR compiles' wall time, in nanosecond ticks; HotSpot adds a successful compile's time to its kind only), `sun.ci.totalBailouts` (compiles that ran and published nothing: a backend failure, a refused install; not a decline or the code-cache-cap deferral; an in-task IR bail that published a single-pass body is a compile) and `sun.ci.lastFailedType` / `lastFailedMethod` (the last of them); `java.ci.totalTime` is now every attempt's nanoseconds, and `CompilationMXBean.getTotalCompilationTime` its whole milliseconds. Units and variability are HotSpot's. `=0`: the 49-name image of. Probes `Zt10Pl2CounterCensus` (79 names; 94 with t15/pl6, JIT on; 107 with t16/pl7), `Zt14Pl5PerfValues`, `Zt15Pl6PerfValues`, `Zt16Pl7PerfValues` (rows in `t14-pl5-rows.txt`, `t15-pl6-rows.txt`, `t16-pl7-rows.txt`). |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_CLASS_TIMERS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | default ON since: HotSpot 25 publishes these counters by default; t16 `pl7_values_T` SAME (`present=true ... time_covers_selves=true`), t20 `fl1_ct_{D,T}` CPU T/D median 1.011 over 6 ABBA pairs (D-first 0.958, T-first 1.016), stdout equal.* Every collector, with `CRATONVM_PERF_HOTSPOT_COUNTERS` on: the image also gets HotSpot's class-trace timers `sun.cls.classLinkedTime`, `classVerifyTime`, `classInitTime`, `defineAppClassTime` and each one's `.self` (ticks, monotonic), and `sun.cls.time` becomes their self times' sum, with `PerfClassTraceTime`'s accounting (`cratonvm_types::perf_class_trace`): the inclusive time of the outermost event of a kind, the self time without nested events of other kinds. The events: a link (`link_claimed_class`, `link_class_without_java`), its link-time verification, a `<clinit>` call, a `defineClass1` / `defineClass2` / `Unsafe.defineClass0` native. Cost: two clock reads per event. `=0`: the names are absent and `sun.cls.time` stays 0. This VM's split is not HotSpot's: bytecode verification (Pass 3) runs at define time here, inside the define's event, so `classVerifyTime` holds the link-time checks only. |

Rows `pl8_boot_*` (`Zt18Pl8PerfValues boot` under `CRATONVM_DBG_PERF_SAMPLE_INTERVAL_MS=60000`). `=0`: the first pass one interval in. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_ATTACH_MISSING_THROWS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | rows `pl4_fa_missing` / `_missingC`).* Every collector: `jdk.internal.perf.Perf.attach0(<id>)` for an id with no perf data at all (no secure `hsperfdata_<user>` directory of this user, or no regular non-empty `<id>` file in it) throws HotSpot's `IllegalArgumentException` (`Could not map vmid to user name` on Windows, `... user Name` elsewhere, as `PerfMemory::attach`) instead of answering a zero-capacity buffer. Data that exists but cannot be mapped still answers the zero-capacity buffer. Needs `CRATONVM_PERF_ATTACH_FOREIGN`. `=0`: the zero-capacity buffer, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_METASPACE_MAX_BEFORE_GC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Every collector: `sun.gc.metaspace.maxCapacity` (`jstat -gcmetacapacity` `MCMX`) is the class space's reservation alone until the first collection, then the class plus non-class reservation (`perf_data_file::metaspace_max_values`). Rows `pl9_ms_*` (`Zt20Pl9PerfValues metaspace`), `pl5_values_*`. `=0`: the combined reservation from the first sample, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_DEFINE_COUNTS_CLASS_PATH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Every collector, both modes: `sun.cls.defineAppClasses` / `appClassBytes` also count the classes this VM's application loader defines from the class path (`ClassManager::load_class`, counted before the parse), which HotSpot's application loader defines through `defineClass1` / `defineClass2`. The platform loader's defines are not counted (HotSpot counts only its classes outside the CDS archive). Rows `pl9_def_*` (`Zt20Pl9PerfValues defines`), `pl7_values_*` (`define app_loader_counted`). `=0`: the define natives' counts alone, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_METASPACE_AT_GC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * ZGC images (other collectors unchanged: G1 also updates them at a humongous allocation, out of this round's scope): `sun.gc.metaspace.capacity` / `used`, `sun.gc.compressedclassspace.capacity` / `used` (`jstat -gc` `MC` / `MU` / `CCSC` / `CCSU`) and both `maxCapacity` read 0 (the reservations `CRATONVM_PERF_METASPACE_MAX_BEFORE_GC` gives) before the first collection, then hold the figures the sampler's first pass after each collection takes (`perf_data_file::metaspace_counter_values`). HotSpot 25's `MetaspacePerfCounters` are written at `universe_init`, before any class is loaded, and then at each collection pause only (`ZServiceabilityCounters::update_sizes`), so they stand still between collections. Residual: HotSpot writes them at the pause, here up to one sampler period later. Rows `pl10_ms_*` (`Zt21Pl10PerfValues metaspacehold`). `=0`: the current figures at every sampler pass, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_HRT_QPC_FREQUENCY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Windows only, every collector, both modes: `sun.os.hrt.frequency` and `jdk.internal.perf.Perf.highResFrequency` are the performance counter's frequency (`QueryPerformanceFrequency`, HotSpot's `os::elapsed_frequency` there: 10000000), and every tick counter (`sun.os.hrt.ticks`, `sun.gc.collector.<n>.time` / `lastEntryTime` / `lastExitTime`, `sun.rt.safepointTime` / `safepointSyncTime` / `applicationTime`, `sun.threads.vmOperationTime`, `sun.cls.time` and the class-trace timers, `java.ci.totalTime`, `sun.ci.standardTime` / `osrTime`) and `Perf.highResCounter` are scaled from the VM's nanoseconds to it (`cratonvm_types::perf_ticks`; exact at 10 MHz). Elsewhere the unit stays nanoseconds, HotSpot's there. Rows `pl10_hrt_*` (`Zt21Pl10PerfValues hrt`). `=0`: 1000000000 on every OS, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_PERF_SAMPLE_INTERVAL_MS` | 100 (diagnostic) | The perf-data sampler's period in milliseconds (a positive number). A row that must tell an event-time write (`CRATONVM_PERF_EVENT_REFRESH`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_PERF_PAUSE_REFRESH`) from a sampler pass sets it beyond the run (`60000`): no pass then lands between a collection and the read (the t12 `pc-ER0` kill printed `true` when one did).: the sampler holds the whole period (`park_for_interval`); before, an early return of `park_timeout` with no unpark ran an unasked pass, which wrote the image in the t16 kill rows `pl7_values_SIR0` and `pl5_pc_SIER0-3`. |
| `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_EXACT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector, as `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS`: that switch's upcall calls exactly `java.lang.ClassLoader.addClass(Class)` (invokespecial semantics), as HotSpot's `JavaCalls::call` on `Universe::loader_addClass_method` does. The method is package-private in `java.lang`, so no user loader overrides it; t7's virtual call resolves by name from the receiver's class, which can select a user loader's own `addClass(Class)` (its side effects would run, its exception would be the define's, the JDK list would stay empty; unverified on this VM until the row runs) (probe `Zt8X5AddClassShadow`; HotSpot 25.0.3: `user addClass calls=0`, `jdk classes size=3 same=true`). `=0`: the virtual call. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: a define door publishes the class before its `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall, so a failed upcall (its list growth's `OutOfMemoryError`: the presize was skipped, or another thread's `addClass` took the presized slot) leaves the class defined behind a define that threw, and the program's retry met `LinkageError: duplicate class definition`; HotSpot makes the upcall before the dictionary update and its retry defines the class. With the switch on the failed upcall records the class as an orphan (class id, digest of the bytes), and a retry of the SAME bytes by the same loader through `ClassLoader.defineClass0/1/2` adopts it: the upcall runs again and the define answers the class. Other bytes keep the `LinkageError`. Rows: `Zt6Nd4AddClassBoundary` with `CRATONVM_ZGC_ADDCLASS_PRESIZE=0` (`linkage=0`; `=0` here: `linkage>0`); `CRATONVM_DBG_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY=1` names each orphan. Generational and G1 unchanged. The deciding row is `Zt10Df2OrphanRetry`, whose list growth is megabytes (the list is filled with nulls and trimmed) while the freed room holds the mirror, so the upcall itself fails after the publish. `Unsafe.defineClass` notes and adopts too, and the `Proxy` bridges adopt a cached `$ProxyN` whose upcall was refused (by class id). .)`, `Unsafe.defineClass(null, ..)`) adopts too: the class is named by the bytes' `this_class` (`classloader::adopt_nameless_add_class_orphan`); before, the `defineClass1/2` doors classified a duplicate by the caller's name only and a nameless retry met the `LinkageError`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_MORE_DOORS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall at the definers `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS` left out: `--compatible`'s `Proxy.newProxyInstance` / `getProxyClass` bridges (a `$ProxyN` this call defined, before it is initialized), `--compatible`'s `Lookup.defineClass` door from a lookup class of the application or platform loader (the platform one only while `CRATONVM_LOOKUP_DEFINE_PLATFORM_LOADER` names it), and `Unsafe.defineClass` into the application loader. HotSpot records each in its loader's `classes` list (probe `Zt8X5AddClassDefiners`: `proxy ... in=true grew=1`, `lookup-app ... in=true grew=1`). Also off with `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS=0`. Generational and G1 unchanged (nothing in it depends on the collector; the zt round's scope rule). newProxyInstance` / `Lookup.defineClass` bodies run (concrete bytecode beats a `Bridge`) and define through `defineClass1` / `defineClass0`, which `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS` serves (t9a: `in=true` with this switch `=0`; the t8b base, whose bridges made no upcall, printed `in=true` too). That row's kill control is `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS=0`. This switch decides `Unsafe.defineClass` into the application loader (`Zt9DfDefinePath` `unsafe-app`) and the bridges where they still serve (the forced `Proxy.getProxyClass` under `--compatible`: `Zt10Df2AddClassDoors`; the synthetic JDK). `CRATONVM_DBG_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_DOOR=1` names the door of each upcall. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_INIT_AFTER_ADD_CLASS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: `ClassLoader.defineClass0` with `initialize` and no class data (a non-hidden class into a loader object) runs `<clinit>` after its `addClass` upcall, as HotSpot's `JVM_LookupDefineClass` initializes after `define_instance_class`; it ran inside the define, before the upcall (only an initializer that reads its own loader's `classes` list can tell). A failing initializer raises the VM's own `ExceptionInInitializerError` instead of one rebuilt from the define's message. Also off with `CRATONVM_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS=0`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_FORMAT_ERROR_FIRST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: the `addClass` presize and the mirror reservation run before the define parses the class file, so malformed bytes on a spent heap were an `OutOfMemoryError` where HotSpot (which parses first) throws `ClassFormatError`. With the switch on, an `OutOfMemoryError` refusal of either step is checked against a parse of the bytes (only then: a served define never parses twice); bytes that do not parse skip the step and the define raises its `ClassFormatError`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ORPHAN_HIDDEN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only, with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY` on: `ClassLoader.findLoadedClass` (all three doors, through `classloader_real::hide_package_refused`) answers `null` for an orphan, a class whose define published it and then failed its `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall, until a retry of the same bytes adopts it. HotSpot makes the upcall before the dictionary update, so its failed define left the name undefined (row R1-visible: `Zt10Df2OrphanRetry 4 64 400000 visible` with `CRATONVM_ZGC_ADDCLASS_PRESIZE=0`: HotSpot `visible=0`; before this switch CratonVM printed `visible=4`). The retry is adopted as before (the adoption looks the class up by its defining-loader row, not through `findLoadedClass`). Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_FFM_ILLEGAL_NATIVE_ACCESS_TAKEN_AT_BOOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | HotSpot's `initPhase2` runs `ModuleBootstrap.boot`, whose class initialiser reads AND REMOVES `jdk.module.illegal.native.access` before `main`; this VM initialised `ModuleBootstrap` only at the first restricted call, so `System.getProperty("jdk.module.illegal.native.access")` answered `deny` / `allow` (and `System.getProperties` listed it) where HotSpot answers `null`. Now the VM's `initPhase2` slot (`Vm::boot_module_system`) calls `ModuleBootstrap.illegalNativeAccess`, so the JDK latches the mode while the property is there and removes it. Only when the property's value is one the JDK accepts (`deny`, `allow`, `warn`): HotSpot aborts the boot on any other, which this VM still leaves to the first restricted call. The same initialiser also removes `jdk.module.patch.*` / `jdk.module.enable.native.access.*`, as on HotSpot. Probe `Zt13X10IllegalNativeAccess` (`prop=null`). `=0`: the property stays visible until the first restricted call. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PROXY_UPCALL_SERIALIZED` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector, with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_MORE_DOORS` on: the `Proxy` bridges (the synthetic JDK; the real-JDK paths of both modes run the JDK's `ProxyBuilder`, whose memoizer already waits) mark a freshly defined `$ProxyN`'s `addClass` upcall in flight before the class reaches the proxy cache, and a cache hit on another thread waits (`Thread.yield` rounds, so it passes safepoints) until that upcall ends, then adopts the orphan a refused upcall left or answers the class. Without it a racer could answer a `$ProxyN` that is not (yet) in its loader's `classes` list. `CRATONVM_DBG_PROXY_UPCALL_STALL_MS=<ms>` holds the first define between its cache insert and its upcall (the race row `31d8_*`). |

It kept the first, failed define's domain. `Unsafe.defineClass` installs no domain object and is unchanged. Probe `Zt14Df5OrphanRetryDomain`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ORPHAN_ADOPT_RECORDS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only, with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY` on: the adoption of an orphan (a define whose `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall failed after the publish) makes its `addClass` upcall although the failed upcall had claimed the class's one built-in loader record (`CRATONVM_BUILTIN_LOADER_ADD_CLASS`), so the adopted class is in the application loader's `classes` list, as on HotSpot (whose retry is the class's only define). It was missing. Probe `Zt12Df4UnsafeAppOrphan`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_BIND_AFTER_ADD_CLASS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: `ClassLoader.defineClass0/1/2` into a USER-DEFINED loader binds the class's name only after its `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall returned, as HotSpot's `SystemDictionary::define_instance_class` makes the upcall before `update_dictionary`. While the upcall runs the class is stored and its mirror minted, but no by-name lookup answers it (`findLoadedClass`, resolution, the class store's name index, the loader's defining index); an upcall that throws WITHDRAWS the name, so the define leaves no class and a retry with the same or OTHER bytes defines a fresh one (the orphan adoption of `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY` is then never needed for these doors; it stays the fallback of the others). A second define of the name meanwhile waits (`Thread.yield` rounds, safepoint-passing) for the first to settle, as HotSpot's placeholder makes it wait. Built-in loaders, hidden classes, initializing defines, `Unsafe.defineClass` and the `Proxy` / `Lookup` bridges bind at the define as before. `CRATONVM_DBG_DEFINE_ADD_CLASS_RETRY=1` prints `unbound define bound / withdrawn / waited` lines. Probe `Zt16Df6RetryOtherBytes`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_FORMAT_CHECKS_FIRST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only, with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_FORMAT_ERROR_FIRST` on: an `OutOfMemoryError` refusal of a define's `addClass` presize or mirror reservation yields to the define's `ClassFormatError` also for bytes that parse structurally but fail one of the class manager's pre-publish `ClassFileParser` checks (`cratonvm_classloading::define_refused_by_format`: the attribute decode, the constant pool's references and names, class and member modifiers such as `ACC_INTERFACE` without `ACC_ABSTRACT`, duplicate interfaces, the class attributes), as HotSpot makes them all before it allocates; they were an `OutOfMemoryError` on a spent heap. The checks use the user-defined loader's scope (the boot and platform loaders relax some). `=0` keeps the structural parse alone (t9). Probe `Zt16Df6FormatFirst`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_WITHDRAW_PINS_CONSTRAINT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only, with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_BIND_AFTER_ADD_CLASS` on: a define whose `ClassLoader.addClass` upcall threw (its name withdrawn) keeps the JVMS §5.3.4 loader-constraint pin a pending constraint on (name, loader) takes from it, as HotSpot's `check_constraints` (`LoaderConstraintTable::check_or_update`) set it before the upcall and the failed upcall leaves it. A later define of the name by a loader of the constraint, the retry included, is then the `LinkageError` HotSpot throws, in its text for a pin to a class that never became `loaded`: `loader constraint violation: loader <L> wants to load class <N>. (<N> is in unnamed module of loader <L>...)`, without `A different class ... previously loaded by`; an initiating load answered with another class reads the same way. `=0`: nothing pinned, the retry defines (pre-t21). Only reachable with a constraint recorded on the name (member resolution across loaders) and a failed upcall (with `CRATONVM_ZGC_ADDCLASS_PRESIZE` on, a spent heap refuses the define before the class exists: not emulated, page §9). Probe `Zt21Df9ConstraintAfterFailedUpcall`. Generational and G1 unchanged. |

With both copies defined, the application loader's `findLoadedClass` answers its own one before the platform loader's (`BuiltinClassLoader.loadClassOrNull` asks it first). Hidden classes keep the application namespace. `MethodHandles.Lookup.defineClass`'s own door (reached only with the `JdkInvoke` alignment row off, `CRATONVM_COMPAT_ALIGN=-jdk-invoke`; the default path is `defineClass0`) follows this switch for a platform-loader lookup class (; its separate key was retired by). | `CRATONVM_FIND_LOADED_BUILTIN_INITIATED_ONLY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The application and platform loaders' `findLoadedClass` (`ClassLoader.findLoadedClass`, `findLoadedClass0`) answers a class one of their ANCESTORS defined only when the loader initiated it, as HotSpot's `JVM_FindLoadedClass` reads the asking loader's own dictionary: a class one of its classes resolved (the resolution door now files that row for the two loaders), `Class.forName` with it, a reflective listing's or the launcher's signature type, a direct supertype of a class it defined. A Java `loadClass` delegation records nothing, so after `Class.forName("java.sql.Timestamp", false, platformLoader)` alone the application loader's `findLoadedClass` is null (it answered the class). The VM's own lookups keep the ancestor answer; a withheld answer makes `BuiltinClassLoader.loadClassOrNull` delegate in Java, as on HotSpot. `CRATONVM_DBG=access` prints the `BUILTIN-INITIATED census` exit line. | `CRATONVM_G1_SELF_FORWARD_SCAN_SKIP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *r23 w6 G1 evacuation copy throughput.* A pause that self-forwarded nothing (no evacuation failure) answers its identity scans of the forwarding map (kept regions, retire-kept-only, the retry's seeds) without walking the map. |
| `CRATONVM_G1_RESERVE_CREDIT_FINE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *G1 near-full proxy-define page.* A fallible small allocation at the G1 TLAB reserve may claim a Free region once collections gave back a sixteenth of a region per claim since the last refusal (was half a region); the last Free region is never taken and a heap filling with live data earns nothing. |
| `CRATONVM_G1_FULL_COMPACTION_IN_PLACE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *G1 near-full proxy-define page.* An armed G1 full compaction (the allocation-failure ladder, `System.gc`) whose copying pass left the Free pool under an eighth of the heap's regions then slides the movable Old regions in place, as HotSpot's full collection does: no Free to-space is needed. `[GC] g1 in-place-compaction:` is its census. |
| `CRATONVM_G1_FULL_COMPACTION_IN_PLACE_PARALLEL` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *G1 near-full proxy-define page.* The in-place full compaction runs on the evacuation pool; `=0` runs it on the driver alone. |
| `CRATONVM_G1_EVAC_ONE_STORE_MARK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *r23 G1 evacuation page.* A G1 evacuation copy's destination mark word is one plain store with the old-generation stamp or the incremented age folded in, not a store plus a locked read-modify-write on the line the copy just wrote. |
| `CRATONVM_G1_EVAC_COPY_END_OWNER_STORE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *r23 G1 evacuation page.* A parallel G1 copy inside the worker's own whole-region TLAB publishes the region's copy end with a store (one writer), not `fetch_max`'s locked loop. |
| `CRATONVM_LAYER_MODULE_LOADER_OWNED_EVERY_GC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | *r23 G1 near-full proxy-define page.* The row above on G1 and Generational too: a user loader's `java.lang.Module` is held weakly and pinned to its loader instead of rooted for good, so `Proxy.getProxyClass(new ClassLoader {}, ..)` no longer keeps each loader, its `jdk.proxyN` module and its classes alive (3.9 KB a define on G1, HotSpot 0; `Zs3Nd3UpcallMints proxy -Xmx64m` filled the heap). `=0` restores the strong root off ZGC. |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_MIRROR_NAME_FROM_BYTES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only: a nameless define (`defineClass(null, bytes, ..)`, `Unsafe.defineClass(null, ..)`) parks the class file's own `this_class` name in the reserved mirror, so nothing is allocated for the mirror after the define. |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_MIRROR_RESERVED_EVERY_DEFINER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC only (with `CRATONVM_ZGC_DEFINE_MIRROR_RESERVED` on): the other definers reserve the mirror before the define too -- `ClassLoader.defineClass0` (`Lookup.defineClass` / `defineHiddenClass`), the `--compatible` `ClassLoader.defineClass` / `defineClassN` backend / `Unsafe.defineClass` / `defineAnonymousClass` / `URLClassLoader` local define / `Lookup.defineClass` / `defineHiddenClass[WithClassData]` bodies, and the service loader's embedded-jar define; the protection domain is re-read across the reservation. It was the ZGC heap only (and `1` for every collector) until the zs / dev merge joined it with dev's launcher reporting (`vm-cli/src/main.rs` `premain_exception_describe`); text fixed by. HotSpot's ZGC stall fails after a collection that freed no 2 MiB page; without the verdict this VM collects once per sliver of free space down to the last object (~30 whole-heap collections on the ProxyBoxing / `Zr2CpCapacity grow` shape). Moves the OOME point earlier by at most the floor, toward HotSpot's.: the compiled allocation doors (`jit-helpers`: `new`, `newarray`, `anewarray`, the box door) have no clear-soft rung, so there the verdict is armed only while no soft reference is active (armed over one, the compiled allocation threw with soft references uncleared).: one verdict per fill. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_NEAR_FULL_SOFT_CLEAR_PERMILLE` | **on** = 60 (`=0` kill; `<permille>` sets the floor) | default ON since zt : on the local HotSpot 25.0.3 box `Zs6NoSoftNearFull 95` turns SAME (3/3, both modes), 90 / 80 and the regression set stay SAME, interleaved A/B 0.88-1.01x CPU.* An allocation-driven ZGC collection (not `System.gc`) whose previous cycle left under the given permille of the heap free asks every active soft reference to be cleared. This stands in for HotSpot ZGC's stalled majors near full, which clear all soft references: on 25.0.3, `Zs6NoSoftNearFull` keeps the cache up to 91 % fill and clears it from 94 %. This VM does not stall there, so a stall rule ('s `CRATONVM_ZGC_STALL_CLEARS_SOFT`, deleted by) never fired. `near_full_soft_clears=` on `[GC] zgc-refusal:`. |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_ROOT_FILTER_CONC` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The extra-root filter stays armed during the concurrent mark (it was disarmed, so each concurrently marked object took three global `RwLock` reads and a shared counter bump); the mark-end late-owner repair ( round) covers rows removed mid-phase. Concurrent cycles only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_REF_SKIP` | **on** since dev `43e50d605c` (; `=0` kill) | A ZGC concurrent mark cycle hides the referent slot of every ACTIVE Weak / Phantom `Reference` of the VM's reference processor (a mark-start snapshot, refreshed at the mark end), as G1's initial mark does, so those referents can clear at the cycle's end. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_REF_SKIP_SOFT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_REF_SKIP`, the ACTIVE Soft rows are hidden too, for the concurrent PHASE only; the mark end re-roots the referents the LRU policy kept (`kept_soft_referent_roots`). `=0` hides weak / phantom only. Engagement: `[GC] zgc-conc-abandon: cycle= cause=full-request abandons=` under `--verbose:gc`, `ZgcRealHeap::full_request_conc_abandons`. Concurrent cycles only; one TLS read per collection otherwise. gc` door withdraws it only when it did not collect, and nothing else on ZGC consumed it), so a won `System.gc` no longer leaves a flag that abandoned every later cycle that thread collected. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_FULL_REQUEST_CLOSES_OPEN_CYCLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A full request (`System.gc`, JVMTI, the VM-internal full doors; `force_full_collection`) that finds a concurrent cycle open first lets that cycle finish as its OWN collection (one pause on the allocation door, closing it with its mark set as an allocation-triggered collection does), then runs the requested collection, which has no cycle left to close and marks from scratch. HotSpot 25 queues the request behind the running major cycle (`ZDriverPort::send_sync`), so `GarbageCollectorMXBean.getCollectionCount`, `-Xlog:gc` and `jdk.GarbageCollection` move by 2 there; with only `CRATONVM_ZGC_FULL_REQUEST_FRESH_MARK` they moved by 1 (x1 finding X1-9). | `CRATONVM_ZGC_ROOT_FILTER_REPAIR_OWN_ARENA` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With the extra-root filter armed across a concurrent ZGC mark (`CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_ROOT_FILTER_CONC`), the mark-end repair declines to certify the mark set (a stop-the-world re-mark, `parallel_mark_fallbacks`) when a pin-table row present at mark start is gone. The tables are process-wide; with this on, only a row whose owner lies in this heap's arena counts, so another VM in the same process collecting or tearing down during the phase no longer forces the re-mark. `=0` counts every row in the process. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_ARENA_NO_THP` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | Advise the ZGC arena reservation `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE` at heap construction (Linux; a no-op elsewhere; skipped when `CRATONVM_HEAP_THP` asks for huge pages). | `CRATONVM_ZGC_SIDE_TABLE_NO_THP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The ZGC object-start, mark and old-start bitmaps (1/64 of `-Xmx` each, written sparsely: a bit per object start or mark) of at least 1 MiB (a 64 MiB heap) go on private anonymous mappings advised `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE` (`gc::reservation::map_side_table`) instead of `alloc_zeroed` blocks in the process allocator. The mapping is zero-filled 4 KiB at a time; the bitmap's words, geometry and every reader are unchanged, and a refused mapping falls back to `alloc_zeroed`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_RELOCATE` | **on as the cycle gate `=2`** since zs s2 (`=0` kill; `=1` selects the z9 per-page arm; knobs `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_RELOCATE_HOLE_KIB`, default 64, and `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_RELOCATE_FRAG_PCT`, default 25) | The low slide already evacuates only sparse pages (live under 25 %, widened by `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_PRESSURE_SELECT`), but on this collector a sparse page's garbage is already on the free list after the sweep, so moving its survivors buys only contiguity -- and every slide that moves anything rewrites the reference slots of the WHOLE live set. With the switch on, a page with survivors stays in place unless at least `FRAG_PCT` % of its extent is free in holes shorter than `HOLE_KIB` (holes the TLAB carve and large requests cannot use); the cost gate prices the slide by that fragmented garbage, and a selection left with no survivor declines the slide outright. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_RELOCATE=2` (or `cycle`) | **default** (unset = `=2` since zs s2) | default ON zs s2: host A/B on `cvm-s1b`, 7 interleaved rounds: `Zr3MtMarkShapes hubs` -9 % CPU, `Zr7PcCollectionCpu prim` -2 %, `MtChurnProbe` +0.7 %, `Zr2CpCapacity grow` +1.3 %, `Zr3JaRefArrayAlloc` equal; every stdout equal; `=0` kills.* The cycle gate: one keep/evacuate decision per CYCLE instead of per page. The slide runs the default selector unfiltered when the candidate pages hold fragmented garbage of at least 1/32 of the low live bytes (the cost gate's ratio), or when the low bump headroom is under a quarter of capacity (the allocator is already living on holes); otherwise the slide is declined outright (no copy, no rewrite). Rationale: the rewrite is paid once per slide, so once a slide runs, leaving sparse pages in place only gives up their contiguity; and pages kept in place were what made the z9 per-page arm (`=1`, unchanged) +12 % CPU on `MtChurnProbe`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_SLIDE_PIN_BY_BASE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The low slide's pin screen also drops the page of the OBJECT a pin lies in (an interior pin on page k+1 into an object based on page k used to let the object move), and the high slide honours a pin one past an object's end, the rule `resolve_interior_for_pin` already applies.
