An idle Windows process then keeps its memory until its next collection. Ignored on Linux and macOS. `un_running=0` on `[GC] zgc-uncommitter:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_POINTER_MAP_SORTED` | **on** (`=0` kill; zt t14) | default ON since zt t14 on the rl2 rows (SAME both modes, map peak halved on `Zt8Rl2InternSlide`, CPU <= 0.98x over 5 interleaved pairs).* The low slide hands its `from -> to` pairs over as the cycle's `PointerMap` in a SORTED form (one vector, 16 bytes a move) instead of hashing every move into 16 shards (~19-38 bytes a move plus one insert each, inside the pause). A lookup becomes a binary search; a consumer that mutates the map converts it back. Same answers. Deciding rows: `un_slide_map_peak` on `[GC] zgc-uncommitter:` against maxrss, and user+sys CPU, on `-Xmx256m MtChurnProbe 4 60 48` and `Zr3FpRss steady`, 6 interleaved pairs. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATIONS_CEILING_SCALED` | **on** (`=0` kill) | 025x over 5 interleaved pairs).* The ceiling at which the forwarding table (`counters.relocations`) is cleared follows the heap: one entry per 256 bytes of capacity, at least 256 Ki entries, never above the absolute 4 M (1 M at `-Xmx256m`; 4 M from 1 GiB up). It can only lower the bound. A clear degrades the barrier for older moves to the stamped-word path. Deciding row: `fp_relocations_peak` and maxrss on `-Xmx256m Zr3FpRss steady`, with no new `CRATONVM_DBG_VACATED_FRAMES` stale reads. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATIONS_TRIM_HYSTERESIS` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The forwarding table's footprint trim (`CRATONVM_ZGC_FOOTPRINT_TRIM`) is sized by the table's DEMAND -- the larger of what survived the prune and the most one slide published since the last prune -- and taken only after 8 consecutive over-provisioned prunes, walked or skipped (a trim keeps every entry). Without it a walk that leaves a fraction trims at once, and on a steady compacting run the next slide regrows the table: a rehash and a fresh allocation per cycle inside the pause. `fp_trim_regrows=` on `[GC] zgc-footprint:` counts those regrows on every arm. Deciding rows: the `fp*` rows of internal records (CPU and peak working set against the unset arm, `fp_trim_regrows` > 0 there). |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_QUARANTINE_DEAD_CHECK` | off (debug `=1`) | With `CRATONVM_ZGC_EVAC_QUARANTINE=1`, each release of the quarantine first walks every registered object's reference slots and counts the words that still name an address inside a held span: a slot the slide's rewrite missed, which stage 3 (remap in the next mark) would have to heal. A census, not a gate. `qdead=` on `[GC] zgc-relocate:` (releases checked / objects walked / stale words / screened words:a hit that is not 8-aligned cannot be an object base, so it is a primitive in a slot declared as a reference and is counted apart). O(registered slots) per release. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_REFUSES_RAW_JNI_LOCALS` | **follows raw locals** (unset: on iff `CRATONVM_JNI_INDIRECT_LOCALS=0`; `=1` forces, `=0` kills; zt t14) | zt t14 on rows `rl6s_*`: with raw locals and the switch unset the upcall lost every object (`upcall ok 0/40`, HotSpot 40/40), with it SAME both modes, cost 1.042x on the raw-locals cost probe; indirect locals (the default) need nothing, so unset leaves them alone.* ZGC refuses a slide while a thread of its VM holds RAW JNI local references (`CRATONVM_JNI_INDIRECT_LOCALS=0`, or a thread's raw escape), whose C copies a slide cannot rewrite; the JNI dispatch keeps that count for it. Unconditional like the retained-TLAB refusal (no compiled frame needed), and not licensed by `CRATONVM_ZGC_ASSUME_REWRITABLE`. `[GC] zgc-relocation-skip-reason: raw-jni-locals-open=`. Default arm: locals are indirect, so no holder exists outside a raw escape. |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_EVAC_BLOCK_SPANS` | off (debug `=1`) | Every span the evacuating slide would free is treated as screen-blocked, so each moved survivor's old extent is freed on its own. It is the positive control of that net (`evac=` fifth field, `evac_forced_blocks=`). Reclaims less, never more. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_BYPASS_ON_LATCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_SELECTIVE_RELOCATE` on. The selective arm's near-OOM fallback (the full selector after an allocation failure) reads the cycle's allocation-failure latch itself. z9 read the pressure tier, which is `None` under `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_PRESSURE_SELECT=0`, so with that kill set the failure collection still kept the long-hole pages and the large request failed again. `=0` restores the z9 read. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_RATE_EXCLUDE_PAUSE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=auto` and `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_RATE_FROM_ALLOCATION` on. The adaptive window's allocation-rate sample ends its time span at the collection's entry, where its byte count is read, instead of after the pause (a bias of `pause / interval` toward a low rate, which opens the window later). `=0` restores the post-pause span. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_POOL_WEAK_HEAP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Only when a concurrent mark runs (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`). The concurrent mark pool is spawned over an inert context before the mark-start pause arms anything, and binds the heap WEAKLY for the cycle. Before, the pool held the heap's own `Arc` while the cycle was open, so a heap dropped mid-cycle (an embedder dropping its VM) was never freed and its workers stayed parked; and a failed spawn panicked inside the pause, where it now declines the cycle (stays stop-the-world). `=0` restores the z3 pool. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START_ZERO_KILLS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER=1` an unset `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START` is seeded to 60 ("concurrent marking configured"); an EXPLICIT `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=0` now stays the kill switch the round's docs name, where z3 seeded it to 60 as well. `=0` restores the z3 seed. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SPAWN_BACKOFF` | **on** (`=0` kill) | After a parallel-mark cycle on which neither the persistent nor the per-cycle pool could be spawned, the next 16 parallel-mark cycles mark serially instead of spawning and joining up to N-1 threads inside each pause (and logging a warning per cycle). `=0` retries every cycle as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_SKIP_DEAD_HEADERS` | **on** /zb (`=0` kill) | The ZGC bitmap sweep no longer zeroes the 16-byte header of each dead low-region object (one read-for-ownership miss per dead object; HotSpot writes no dead object either). The free spans are computed from survivors only, every reader of arena bytes is registry or free-list gated, and every hand-out zeroes what it hands out, so no answer changes. With the store gone the dead side of the pass is a popcount per bitmap word, and the region step (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_LIVE_PROPORTIONAL`) skips 32 KiB blocks with no survivor. z5a: -20 % on `Zr3JaRefArrayAlloc 1 10 64 prim 400`. Set `=0` on a run hunting a stale reference: the zeroed header is what the "Stale pointer detected" fallbacks and `reclaim_guard`'s `ClassId(0)` gate read. Engagement: `dead_headers_skipped=` on `[GC] zgc-alloc-sweep:`; set explicitly, `--verbose:gc` also prints `[GC] zgc-sweep-skip-dead-headers:` per cycle. |
| `CRATONVM_JMOD_LAZY_INDEX` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch; listed here with the round's boot-footprint rows.* A JMOD on the boot class path is indexed from its ZIP central directory alone, and an entry's local header is read only when that entry is served (`classloading/src/jmod_index.rs`). `zip::ZipArchive::new` (zip 2.4.2) reads every entry's local header at open, which faults in about one page per entry of a mapped JMOD: about 47 MB of a Hello world's working set on a JDK that ships `jmods/` (70 files). Answers are the same as the `ZipArchive` path's: names, class index, and the bytes of every entry (a unit test compares both arms on every entry of every JDK 25 JMOD). A JMOD the index does not model (ZIP64, encryption, a method other than stored/deflated, a non-ASCII name, a ZIP64/AES/Unicode-path extra field, a prefixed archive) keeps the `ZipArchive` path. Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_JAR_LAZY_INDEX` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch; listed with the boot-footprint rows.* A plain class-path jar is opened from its ZIP central directory alone, with the same index as `CRATONVM_JMOD_LAZY_INDEX` (`classloading/src/jmod_index.rs`), and an entry's local header is read only when that entry is served. `zip::ZipArchive::new` reads every entry's local header at open, one page per entry of a mapped jar: a class path of 30 jars (363 MB, 90 000 entries) had 523 MB of working set after a one-resource read from each, 143 MB with the index (Windows, `JarProbe`, output SAME, CPU equal or lower). Class and resource bytes, existence, listing and the multi-release version set answer from the index; signed-jar verification and the nested-archive rows build the `ZipArchive` on first use. A fat jar, and a jar the index does not model (ZIP64, encryption, a non-ASCII name, a prefixed archive), keep the old open. |
| `CRATONVM_CLASS_CODE_COMPACT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch; listed with the round's per-class footprint rows.* At define, every byte view a decoded member attribute holds into the class file (each method's `Code` bytecode, the `StackMapTable` and unknown attributes nested in it, unknown member attributes) is copied into one compact per-class buffer (`compact_member_attribute_views`, `classloading/src/class_manager.rs`), so the class's metadata stops pinning the whole class file: ~0.56 KB kept instead of ~4.1 KB for a java.base class. The bytes are the same; only the buffer behind each view changes. Skipped for a class file backed by an external mapping (a stored JAR entry). The saving shows when the class-bytes cache does not hold the file either (`CRATONVM_BOOT_CLASS_BYTES_REFETCH`, or past the cache's 16 MiB FIFO cap). Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_BOOT_CLASS_BYTES_REFETCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch.* A class the boot loader defines from its own class path (the runtime image, a JMOD, `-Xbootclasspath/a`) keeps no copy of its class file in the class-bytes cache; its define-time fingerprint stays. Every reader of a defined class's file (`retransformClasses` / JFR retransform bases, the redefine `ClassFileLoadHook`'s old bytes, `Class.getAnnotatedSuperclass`, a defined class's own `.class` resource, the CDS dump, the debugger's `SourceDebugExtension`) now asks `ClassManager::defined_class_bytes`: the cache, else a re-read through the delegation chain that answers only when the bytes match the fingerprint. HotSpot keeps no class file for these classes either (`InstanceKlass::_cached_class_file` is for a retransformable agent's classes). With `CRATONVM_CLASS_CODE_COMPACT`, ~3.5 KB per boot class; positive control: `ClassManager::class_bytes_refetch_released`. Page: as above. |
| `CRATONVM_LAZY_ZERO_TABLES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch; every collector.* The fixed-capacity side tables sized to a class-id bound (the `field_layout` replace counters, the VM statics index, the `getClass` ref-array owners, the lambda-proxy hit bits, the `java/util` and class-init bits, the published-supers arena chunks, the JIT callee negative cache, the lock-slot tables) are allocated zeroed (`alloc_zeroed`: only the pages a slot write reaches become resident) instead of written element by element. Same values. `=0`: every table written in full at its first use, as before. Read once per process. |
| `CRATONVM_JIMAGE_PACKAGE_INDEX` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch; every collector.* A `lib/modules` jimage on the class path is indexed by package (~900 entries, a module number each) instead of by class (~27 000 `String` pairs, ~5 MB written at every boot); a class is confirmed by the image's own perfect-hash lookup. An image with a package in two modules keeps the per-class map. `=0`: the per-class map for every image, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_JIMAGE_DIRECT_MAP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Not a GC switch.* `jdk.internal.jimage.NativeImageBuffer.getNativeMap` answers a direct `ByteBuffer` (`DirectByteBuffer(long, long)`, the JNI constructor) over a read-only mapping of `lib/modules`, made once per path for the life of the process, as HotSpot answers its libjimage mapping. The `=0` arm copies the whole ~145 MiB image into a Java `byte[]` held for the life of the VM: a live huge array on the Java heap, and a `jrt:/` mount under `-Xmx` below ~160 MB threw `OutOfMemoryError` where HotSpot runs. A mapping that fails falls back to the copy. Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_ARENA_MAX_OBJECT_KB` | **64** (default ON; `=0` / `off` keeps mimalloc's own 2 GiB cap; any other number is the cap in KiB) | Linux launcher only. A pre-`main` hook (`.init_array.00100`) installs a default for mimalloc's `arena_max_object_size`, so only its 64 KiB small pages are carved from the eager, huge-page-advised arena; the 512 KiB medium pages, 4 MiB large pages and singleton blocks get their own OS mapping, which costs only the 4 KiB pages they touch and goes back on free. Those pages are per thread and per size class and mostly empty at boot, so on the arena each one held whole 2 MiB huge pages. Host rows (8 interleaved rounds): Hello -87 MB, 3000 classes -91, JIT -78, churn -75, GC-heavy -71 against the old default, at +5..7k faults (lazy commit: +18k) and CPU within -8..+3 %; `=2048` (medium pages on the arena too) saves 55-73 MB. An explicit `MIMALLOC_ARENA_MAX_OBJECT_SIZE` wins (mimalloc reads it in BYTES unless it carries a unit). `CRATONVM_GC_STATS` prints `arena_max_object=<default\|env\|off> arena_max_object_kb=` on the `[cratonvm] mimalloc:` line. Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_LAZY_ARENA_COMMIT` | off (opt-in `=1`, or `force`) | Five interleaved host rounds put its CPU at 0.95 s against 0.79 s eager on `Zr7RsBoot classes 3000` (+20 %) and 0.53 against 0.51 on boot. `MIMALLOC_ALLOW_THP=0` on the eager arm cost the same, so both the CPU (about 35k extra 4 KiB first-touch faults) and the ~90 MB RSS saving are the huge pages'. That is over the 1.05x bar for a default; the RSS side is now `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_SPARSE_BLOCK_MMAP`'s job. With the switch set, the `cratonvm` launcher on Linux/macOS (the only binary that installs mimalloc) defaults mimalloc's `arena_eager_commit` to `0` from a pre-`main` constructor. With mimalloc's own default (`2`, eager on an overcommit OS) the first 1 GiB arena is committed up front and so `madvise(MADV_HUGEPAGE)`d by mimalloc, and on a THP=`madvise` host every touched 2 MiB of the allocator became a huge page: 141 MB of a 209 MB Hello world. An explicit `MIMALLOC_ARENA_EAGER_COMMIT` still wins, under any spelling mimalloc accepts (it matches case-insensitively and takes the legacy `MIMALLOC_EAGER_REGION_COMMIT`; /rv8 the hook no longer forces one it did not spell itself, `=force` restores that); environment only (it is read before `main`). The `CRATONVM_GC_STATS` summary prints `[cratonvm] mimalloc: lazy_arena_commit=<default|env|late|off|not-run> arena_eager_commit=<n>`. Page: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_SPARSE_BLOCK_MMAP` | `boot` since FP-SPARSE-BOOT-FLIP (`=0` kills; wider opt-ins `=1` / `zeroed`, or `=all` for plain allocs too; threshold `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_SPARSE_BLOCK_MIN_KB`, default 1024, floor 64) | Linux launcher only: the global allocator is mimalloc behind a thin wrapper. With the switch, every `alloc_zeroed` of at least the threshold (alignment up to a page) is served from its own anonymous `mmap`, advised `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE`, instead of mimalloc's arena, which the eager default commits and `madvise(MADV_HUGEPAGE)`s (a big zeroed block written sparsely then faults in every 2 MiB it touches, and a reused arena block is memset in full). The mapping is zero-filled lazily in 4 KiB pages, `realloc` is `mremap` (no copy), a shrink below the threshold moves back into mimalloc, and `dealloc` is `munmap`; a block's owner is decided by mimalloc's own page map (`mi_is_in_heap_region`), never by the mode. The wider modes stay opt-in because they reach every collector's and every crate's big zeroed blocks; the ZGC bitmaps have their own default-ON lever, `CRATONVM_ZGC_SIDE_TABLE_NO_THP`. Environment only (latched at the first allocator call); `libcratonvm` / `cratonvm-embed` use their host's allocator. `CRATONVM_GC_STATS` prints `[cratonvm] mimalloc-sparse-blocks: mode=<off|zeroed|all|not-installed> min_kb= big_allocs= big_zeroed_allocs= big_zeroed_kb= big_live_kb= big_live_peak_kb= mapped= mapped_live_kb= mapped_peak_kb= remaps= map_failed=`; the `big_*` counters run in every mode, so the default arm already says how much of the arena big blocks held. Report: internal records.: the mapping modes read as `off` on a mimalloc v2 backend (`mi_version < 300`): v2's `mi_is_in_heap_region` stops at 48 TiB, so the ownership-by-address rule could unmap a mimalloc segment placed above it. |
| `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_FIRST_TOUCH` | off (opt-in: `lazy`, `huge`, `populate`, combined with `,` or `+`; `=1` is `huge+populate`; arena size `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_FIRST_TOUCH_ARENA_MB`, default 1024, floor 64) | Linux launcher only. A pre-`main` constructor (`.init_array.00102`) maps mimalloc's first arena itself (2 MiB aligned, read-write, `MAP_NORESERVE`, `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE`) and registers it uncommitted with a commit callback (`mi_manage_memory`), so mimalloc reports every slice range it is about to use. `huge` advises the whole 2 MiB chunks of a commit `MADV_HUGEPAGE` (only a big singleton page or a medium-page run is that large: dense, one fault per 2 MiB); `populate` pre-faults the rest with one `MADV_POPULATE_WRITE` per commit (Linux 5.14+, latched off on `EINVAL`); `lazy` does neither (the control: it must equal `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_LAZY_ARENA_COMMIT=1`). A purge is `MADV_DONTNEED`, reported decommitted so the reuse comes back through the policy. The aim is lazy commit's RSS (-80 MB on the zr rows) at eager's fault count; the host's interleaved rows decide the default. Environment only; `libcratonvm` / `cratonvm-embed` use their host's allocator. `CRATONVM_GC_STATS` prints `[cratonvm] mimalloc-first-touch: mode= outcome=<off|registered|map-failed|refused|not-run> arena_mb= commits= commit_kb= huge_kb= populate_kb= populate_failed= populate_unsupported= purges= purge_kb=`. Report: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_SPARSE_BLOCK_MMAP=boot` (budget `CRATONVM_MIMALLOC_SPARSE_BLOCK_BOOT_COUNT`, default 64; `0` maps nothing) | **default ON** since FP-SPARSE-BOOT-FLIP (`=0` kill) | Mode `zeroed` with a budget: only the first N big zeroed blocks of the process get their own `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE` mapping (the boot's long-lived tables: shadow stacks, the class-id side tables, jimage-sized maps); later ones, the per-cycle scratch that made mode `1` cost +5 % CPU on a GC-heavy row (zr xmt), stay in mimalloc. The budget is latched at the first allocator call, a freed block does not return it, and  a mapping the kernel refuses gives it back. `[cratonvm] mimalloc-sparse-blocks:` adds `boot_budget_left=` / `boot_declined=`. Not host-measured yet: rows in internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_TIER_C2_STANDBY` (`_DEPTH`, default 4, floor 1; `_SCALE`, opt-in) | off (opt-in `1`/`on`) | Every C2 compile worker but the first is spawned parked before its first allocation (a parked thread owns no mimalloc pages; the zs s6a `TIER_C2_THREADS=1` row put three C2 threads' pages at 7-13 MB of boot RSS) and is promoted for good when an enqueue leaves `_DEPTH` requests on the C2 lane; under `_SCALE` the k-th promotion waits for `_DEPTH x` the live workers (with `_DEPTH=2`, the shape of HotSpot's `UseDynamicNumberOfCompilerThreads`). Shutdown and a restart release parked workers. Collector-independent. `[jit] c2-standby: depth= scale= started= promoted=` under `CRATONVM_DBG_RSS_BY_MAPPING`. Not host-measured yet: rows in thereport. |
| `CRATONVM_VTABLE_LAZY_DISPATCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | default ON since fp-default-flip (3000 java.base classes, Windows release: working set 141 -> 129 MB, `--nojit` megamorphic call 421 -> 413 ns, 20 race runs `bad=0`, Zt2FpLazyDispatch / Zs3Fp3Dispatch / Zs2Fp2Meta SAME as HotSpot in both modes, JIT and `--nojit`).* A declared virtual bytecode method's dispatch snapshot (the interpreter's `CachedBytecodeMethod` and padded bytecode, ~1.9 KB a java.base class by thecensus) is built on the first vtable dispatch through one of its slots instead of at link, one per method (an inherited slot shares its declaring method's), with the same contents; HotSpot builds a method's counters only once it runs. Every collector (class metadata, not heap). Read once per VM. `[vm] vtable-lazy-dispatch: on= deferred= built=` under `CRATONVM_DBG_RSS_BY_MAPPING`. Rows (RSS, `--nojit` virtual-call CPU, SAME) in thereport. |
| `CRATONVM_PADDED_CODE_MEMO_KEEP_LIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The interpreter's process-wide padded-bytecode memo (`vm/src/runtime/frame.rs`, `padded_bytecode_for_method`) no longer clears its whole table at 16 384 entries: it drops only the entries whose padded body nothing else holds and raises its limit to twice what it kept. The vtable link pads ~6 methods a class, so a class-heavy start passed the cap during the link and a live method padded again got a second `Arc`, hence a second quickened stream and liveness table. Not Java-visible; every collector. `[vm] padded-code-memo: entries= limit= evictions= evicted= clears=` under `CRATONVM_DBG_RSS_BY_MAPPING`. `=0`: clear whole (`clears=` counts it). |
| `CRATONVM_JIMAGE_SKIP_DIRECTORY_RECORDS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A `lib/modules` runtime image on the class path leaves its `/packages/<package>/<module>` and `/modules` directory records out of the resource index (`resource_to_modules`) and the module list: libjimage's package table and `jrt:/`'s directories are not resources, and `packages` / `modules` are not modules. Before, `find_resource("java.lang/java.base")` read the `/packages` record, so any VM resource door that falls back to the class path could answer a name HotSpot answers `null`. Saves ~2 300 index entries (~0.45 MB) a boot; the `jrt:` file system reads the image directly and is unaffected. Every collector. Probe `Zt2FpJimageDirRecords`. `=0`: indexed as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REFILL_GATE_COLLECTS` | **ON** (`=0` kill; opt-in until) | The same refused-refill window is where a single compiled allocating thread STALLS: on `Zr1TrCauseProbe 200` at `-Xmx48m` the default spilled `main` per object past the 75 % threshold until another thread's native-boundary pressure hook collected or the arena ran out (36-191 `Allocation Stall` collections per run against HotSpot's 4-5); with the switch every local run had 0. Rows: `t12-st-report.md` (probe `Zt12StStallRate`). On ZGC the compiled refill gate is `!needs_gc`, so it refuses exactly when a collection is due; today the collection waits for the refill consult (4 MiB of successful refills, which a refused gate never makes, or 65,536 slow-path entries) or the wedge breaker (16,384 refusals), and every object of every compiled thread takes the locked per-object path meanwhile. With the switch the refused refill takes the due collection at once (stamped occupancy door `tlab-alloc-shaped`, at most one per 1/32 of the heap allocated since the last collection) and re-probes. The window is counted on every run: `gate_refused=` on `[GC] zgc-refusal:`; engagement `gate_collects=` / `gate_collects_dropped=` / `gate_collects_joined=` (: a request that ran no pause of its own, counted apart). Rows: `t4-ch-report.md` CH-3. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_INLINE_ARM_SKIPS_REPUBLISH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | In the optimizing tier's inline ZGC allocation (`new`, `newarray`, `anewarray`), the inline start-bit arm, which makes no call, jumps straight to the site's join instead of running the post-call frame republish (two TLS stores) that follows the `zgc_note_tlab_object` helper call. Value-identical; the helper arm is unchanged. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ANNOUNCE_OWNED_WINDOW` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The LONG inline ZGC start-bit store (both tiers' inline `new` / `newarray` / `anewarray`, box prefixes) proves the bitmap word it ORs into lies wholly inside the thread's chunk by comparing the word index against the buffer's owned-word window (`Tlab::zgc_owned_words`, two u32 written by `Tlab::new`), instead of re-deriving the word's 512-byte granule from the table base and comparing it against `start` and `end`. Same inequalities, so value-identical; 11 instructions become 4 (7 fewer, one load fewer) per compiled allocation. The SHORT form (`CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_SHORT_ANNOUNCE`) keeps its own test. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ANNOUNCE_SKIP_GRID_TEST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The LONG inline ZGC start-bit store drops its `test rcx, 7; jnz` (object offset on the 8-byte grid). Every caller's object is an 8-aligned bump, and the collector publishes the announce table only for an 8-aligned bitmap base, so the branch was never taken. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ANNOUNCE_COMMIT_FROM_RAX` | **on** (`=0` kill) | In both tiers' inline ZGC allocation (start bit before the commit), the start-bit store keeps the announce table in R9, so RAX still holds the bump's end, and the inline arm commits with one store instead of re-deriving the end (5 instructions for an array, `lea` for a `new`). Both announce forms; the long form only with `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ANNOUNCE_OWNED_WINDOW` on. Value-identical; the helper arm is unchanged. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ANNOUNCE_SKIP_SPAN_TEST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With the owned-word window, the LONG inline ZGC start-bit store (both tiers) drops its `base <= obj` and `obj - base < span` tests (3 instructions, 1 load), which the window implies for every armed buffer. `words != 0` stays. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_INLINE_ARM_FAST_JOIN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | In the optimizing tier's inline ZGC `new` / `newarray` / `anewarray`, the inline start-bit arm (no call made) lands past the frame-identity store and the zero test that only the stub owes, at sites whose shadow reload is empty. Value-identical; the safepoint-id store is unchanged. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_IR_NEWARRAY_CONST_LENGTH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The optimizing tier's inline ZGC `newarray` / `anewarray` with a constant length bakes its size: no length load, screen or size arithmetic (7 instructions). Value-identical. ZGC only; Generational and G1 keep their bytes. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_INLINE_ARM_FALL_THROUGH` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The optimizing tier's inline ZGC `new` / `newarray` / `anewarray` keeps only its fast half at the site (bump, header, inline start-bit store), whose inline arm falls through to the continuation instead of ending in `JMP allocated`. The announce helper arm, the allocation stub, the identity republish, the null test and the exception exit move after the method body. One taken branch fewer per compiled allocation, and about 100 bytes less hot-loop code per site. Value-identical. ZGC only; sites with a pending shadow publication, a protected bci or an armed call-throw / catch plan keep the unsplit layout. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_PREFETCH` | `0` (off; opt-in `1`..`4`) | After the optimizing tier's inline ZGC bump passes its end test, emit that many `PREFETCHW [new cursor + distance + 64*i]`, so the lines the next allocations will write are owned before their header stores (HotSpot's `AllocatePrefetch*` shape). Value-identical: a prefetch has no architectural effect and never faults. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_PREFETCH_DISTANCE` | `256` | The first prefetched line's distance past the new cursor, in bytes (0..4096). |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_NEWARRAY_SIZE_OFF_CHAIN` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The optimizing tier's inline ZGC `newarray` / `anewarray` with a run-time length computes its whole footprint from the length alone (`LEA RDX, [RCX * 2^log2 + 23]; AND RDX, -8`, no `AND` for 8-byte elements) and bumps with the two-component `LEA RAX, [R11 + RDX]`. The old bump `LEA RAX, [R11 + RDX + 16]` has three components, the slow `LEA` on Intel Sandy Bridge to Skylake, and it sits on the TLAB cursor's loop-carried chain. Two to three instructions fewer per varlen allocation (subsumed the word-round skip `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_NEWARRAY_SKIP_WORD_ROUND`, deleted by). Value-identical. ZGC only; a constant length keeps its baked footprint. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_PREFETCH_INSTANCE_LINES` | unset (= the `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_PREFETCH` count) | The prefetch line count for an inline `new` only (`0`..`4`), as HotSpot keeps `AllocateInstancePrefetchLines` beside `AllocatePrefetchLines`: an array site keeps `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_PREFETCH`'s count. ZGC only. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_TLAB_ALIGN_TEST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The optimizing tier's inline ZGC bump (`new`, `newarray` / `anewarray`, box prefixes) tests the loaded TLAB cursor for 8-alignment (`TEST R11B, 7; JNE slow`) instead of aligning it (`ADD R11, 7; AND R11, -8`). The cursor is the one value a compiled allocation loop carries through memory from iteration to iteration; the test takes two dependent ALU operations off that chain (HotSpot's C2 bump has none). An unaligned cursor (none is known) takes the slow path before anything is written. ZGC only; Generational and G1 keep their bytes. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_FUSED_REF_STORE_GATE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The optimizing tier's gated reference `putfield` / `aastore` on ZGC's age-floor plan opens with ONE 16-bit compare of the adjacent `pre_active` / `post_active` gate bytes (`CMP word [rip+disp32], 0; JE store`, or through R11 when out of RIP reach) and stores at once when both read zero; otherwise the full gate sequence runs unchanged behind it. Both bytes zero is sufficient for every arm of that sequence that reaches the store, so the outcome is identical; the fast path drops from 9 gate instructions (`aastore`: pre gate, null test, flags byte, floor) to 2-3. ZGC only (the generational mask plan and G1 keep their bytes; the single-pass tier is unchanged). Needs `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_POST_GATE_TRUTH` to engage. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_FUSED_GATE_ALL_SITES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The fused gate above also opens the remaining compiled reference stores on ZGC: the single-pass `putfield` (a compact receiver stores at once on a zero word; legacy receivers and a set word run the unchanged sequence) and `aastore`, and the constant-`VarHandle` / `Unsafe.compareAndSetReference` gate screen of both tiers (`MOV R11, imm64; CMP word [R11], 0; JE` past the screen). Same soundness argument; needs `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_FUSED_REF_STORE_GATE`. G1 and the generational mask plan are byte-identical. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ELEMENT_CARD_BARRIER` | off (opt-in `=1`) | Only acts on a generational ZGC heap (`CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`) with an old generation, and pays off only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_ELEMENT_CARDS=1`: the single-pass `aastore`'s gated post barrier (ZGC's age-floor plan) hands `write_barrier` the ELEMENT's slot (tagged with bit 0, in place of the already-tested non-null value), so a compiled store into an old reference array of 64+ elements cards the element's 8-byte granule, as the interpreter's `aastore` does, instead of the array's object card (which the next young cycle converts with a whole-array scan). The optimizing tier's gated `aastore` already sends such stores to `jit_aastore`, which takes the element card unconditionally since the same change. Deciding row: E1 of internal records with the JIT on (`minor_remset_us`, `element_granules_scanned`). |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_FRESH_RECEIVER_STORE` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The optimizing tier's gated reference `putfield` into a FRESH receiver (an `Op::New` of the same block; only pure arithmetic and stores into that same object scheduled in between; the object not stored anywhere; the field not yet written) stores with no gate at all: its old value is null, so the SATB pre barrier logs nothing, and it is age 0, so the floor would pass. Engages only where the fused gate does (ZGC's age-floor plan). `CRATONVM_DBG_JITC=1` prints one `zgc fresh-receiver ref store` line per elided site. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_SP_AASTORE_NULL_OLD_SKIP` | off (opt-in `=1`) | While a mark runs (`pre_active` set), the single-pass `aastore` loads the old element and calls `satb_pre_write_barrier` for every store, a null old element included. Under the switch a `TEST RAX, RAX; JZ` after that load skips the call for a null old element. Value-identical: the helper returns at its first test on a null old value. ZGC only (the age-floor plan); the generational mask plan and G1 keep their bytes. Census under `CRATONVM_DBG_SP_REF_STORE_TRACE=1 CRATONVM_DBG_JIT_METHOD_STATS=1`: `sp aastore satb-pre executions: entered=E called=C` (`E - C` calls skipped; `E = C` with the switch off). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_POST_INIT_REGISTER_FIRST` | off (opt-in `=1`) | A compiled `new` (both tiers) of a class that still needs `jit_post_tlab_init` -- a finalizer or a `java.lang.ref` superclass, or a legacy-layout field that owes a typed default (a compact class's `long` / `float` / `double` fields owe nothing since gcf f2/p); in the single-pass tier only under `CRATONVM_JIT_ENABLE_INLINE_NEW=1` -- commits the TLAB cursor and only then registers the object inside that helper, so between the two the object is below the cursor, outside a frozen peer's published tail and unregistered (r21's window 1); today only `CRATONVM_ZGC_SKIP_COVERS_INFLIGHT` (default ON) covers it. Under the switch the site registers first, exactly as a post-init no-op class does (the inline start bit, else `zgc_note_tlab_object` called with the cursor uncommitted and the commit after it), then calls `jit_post_tlab_init` after the commit with bit 30 of its field count set, so the helper does not register the object a second time, nor rewrite the header the emitter already wrote (a whole-byte flag store after registration could clear a collector's `GC_FLAG_MARKED` / `GC_FLAG_OLD_GEN`). The helper's body runs where it ran before. Census: `jit_post_tlab_init_registered_first_calls=` on the `compiled-new helper entries:` line (`CRATONVM_DBG_JIT_METHOD_STATS=1`). ZGC only (needs `jit_tlab_registration_required`); Generational and G1 keep their bytes. Off whenever `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_HELPER_BEFORE_COMMIT=0` or its parent `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_START_BIT_BEFORE_COMMIT=0`; the single-pass tier also needs a cached thread slot. Deciding rows: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_ZGC_ALLOC_SPILL_SINK` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The single-pass tier's inline `new` of a post-init no-op class on ZGC sinks its blind GPR spill and its shadow push onto the allocation's slow edge, as Generational and G1 already do (`CRATONVM_JIT_NO_ALLOC_SPILL_SINK`). On ZGC that fast path runs the inline start-bit store, which makes no call; with this switch its refusals (a mark in progress, a buffer carved before the last collection, a chunk's first or last 512 bytes, an unarmed buffer) jump to an out-of-line edge that stores the withheld registers and then calls `zgc_note_tlab_object` as the unsunk site does, so the fast path has no call. it lost all four CPU bars (1.03-1.12x) because every allocation during a mark ran the whole Rust allocator (`TLAB object shapes: compact=` 15,554 -> 2,239,252 on the engagement row). Needs `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_INLINE_ANNOUNCE`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_START_BIT_BEFORE_COMMIT` and `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_HELPER_BEFORE_COMMIT` (all default ON), the allocation spill sink itself (`CRATONVM_JIT_NO_ALLOC_SPILL_SINK` unset) and a cached JIT-thread slot in the compiled method (`zgc-zt12x9-*` item 9); a site without them keeps the full spill. Engagement (`CRATONVM_DBG_JIT_METHOD_STATS=1`, no GC stats needed since t12/ca4): on the `[cratonvm] compiled-new helper entries:` line, `jit_new_object_calls=` and `jit_zgc_announce_calls=` stay within noise of the default arm, and the `CRATONVM_DBG_JIT_DISASM` listing shows the blind-spill stores after the start-bit store's refusal jumps instead of before the bump. ZGC only; Generational and G1 keep their bytes. The optimizing tier is unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_POST_GATE_TRUTH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A new ZGC heap publishes its JIT post gate as the truth it mirrors (`has_old_objects` = false, so 0) instead of armed; the promoting sweep raises it before the truth, as before. On the default non-generational collector the gate therefore reads 0 for the heap's lifetime. `=0` starts it armed (the fused gate then never answers "store" and every compiled store pays it on top of the old sequence). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_NEWARRAY_HEADROOM` | **on** (`=0` kill; opt-in before) | WARNING zt-t28-flip, (page internal records item B11).* ZGC: the compiled `newarray` helper collects on the occupancy verdict as its siblings (`jit_new_object`, `jit_anewarray_object`) do, instead of spilling every array until the TLAB wedge breaker fires. Flipped on the rows `rt2_o8_*`: `tail` SAME, the stall budget held in 5/5 `L48` runs (default arm 4/5), CPU 1.03 (`sizes`) / 0.96 (`mt`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_NEWARRAY_HEADROOM_RATE` | **on** inside the headroom arm (`=0` removes the limit) | The opt-in `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_NEWARRAY_HEADROOM=1` arm asks for at most one occupancy collection per 1/32nd of the heap allocated since the last collection. Counted as `newarray_headroom_rate_skips=` on the `[GC] zgc_stale_gc_requests:` line. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_LAZY_TLAB_STARTS` | **OFF** (`census` or `1` opt-in: both are the census) | The census half of a ZGC start registry with no per-allocation bit inside a live VM-TLAB chunk (proposal C of internal records, refuted as designed by the zr z8a census: 1-2 k header reads per oracle query). The start bit is still set at every allocation and the JIT is unchanged. On, the heap keeps a table of live VM-TLAB chunks, walks each chunk's consumed span by its headers when the buffer retires and when a pause publishes a frozen peer's tail, and counts the oracle hits (`is_object_address`, `is_heap_addr`, the pin probe) that land inside a live chunk, own thread or peer, re-checking one in 8 by a read-only walk. It registers nothing. Figures on `[GC] zgc-lazy-starts:` (`lz_unregistered_found`, `lz_q_own`, `lz_q_peer`, `lz_q_mismatch`). It stays as a diagnostic; the steps that would drop the bit were not built . |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_LAZY_STARTS_DROP_UNPUBLISHED` | **ON** (`=0` kill) | Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_LAZY_TLAB_STARTS` on. A collection forgets the lazy-starts record of every live VM-TLAB buffer that the pause neither retired nor published: the sweep may free the dead objects below that buffer's cursor, and /zb (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_SKIP_DEAD_HEADERS`) their headers survive, so the retire walk would parse the corpses as objects. Counted as `lz_chunks_unpublished_dropped` on `[GC] zgc-lazy-starts:`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_SHORT_ANNOUNCE` | **OFF** (`1` opt-in) | Every inline ZGC start-bit store (the single-pass and optimizing tiers' inline `new` / `newarray` / `anewarray`, the box prefixes, the inline `StringBuilder.toString` pair) emits the SHORT form: 25 instructions, 5 branches and 7 loads against 38 / 9 / 10. It sets the same bit of the same bitmap word under the same ownership test, so every start-registry reader answers as before. The collector publishes two more table words whatever the switch says: `short_blocked` (zero only while the table is published, not blocked, and the arena base is 512-byte aligned) and `granule_bias` (`words - (base >> 9) * 8`). On a misaligned arena base every short site takes the announce helper (correct, slower). Decided against a card / block-offset start index: internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SOFT_CLEAR_CONSERVATIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | On the last-ditch clear-all-soft collection, a condemned soft referent that only a conservatively scanned compiled-frame word names is cleared, not restored (`SoftRefBeforeOome` printed `cleared false` with the JIT on). Trade: a live compiled local holding the referent also loses its soft reference, before an `OutOfMemoryError` only. |
| `-XX:SoftMaxHeapSize=<size>` | `-Xmx` | ZGC only, as on HotSpot: the size the warm-up rule (10/20/30% of it) and the `CRATONVM_ZGC_RATE_TRIGGER` rules aim under; printed as `Soft Max Capacity:` in `-Xlog:gc+init`. Clamped to `-Xmx`. A goal, not a limit. The legacy 75% live-bytes threshold still reads `-Xmx`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_REF_CHUNK` | **4096** | Numeric, not boolean. How many of one object's strong out-edges a mark worker traces before returning to its loop head, where it re-tests the safepoint yield flag — so it is what bounds time-to-yield on a huge reference array instead of leaving it proportional to the array's length. Read once per mark pool (at construction) and once per serial-mark call (`mark::ref_chunk_budget`, over `mark::Z_MARK_REF_CHUNK`, in `gc/src/zgc/mark.rs`); it was latched once per process until. **Live on every ZGC configuration**, both through `ZgcRealHeap`'s own `ZMarkContext::visit_refs_chunked` and through the `ZHeapMarkBridge` wrapper that forwards it (both in `gc/src/zgc.rs`) — the bridge inherited the unsplit default , which made the in-crate test population exercise the unchunked path while the counter read zero. `0` and unparseable values fall back to the default; there is deliberately no "disable" value. Engagement counter: `ref_chunks=` on `[GC] zgc-markend:` (a concurrent cycle) and `[GC] zgc-mark-parallel:` (`CRATONVM_ZGC_PARMARK`), and `chunks=` on `[GC] zgc-mark-serial:` for the default serial marker, which chunks with the same budget /mk (this row named a `[GC] zgc-mark:` line, which never existed, ) — **zero means "no object on this workload needed splitting", not "broken"**. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SPLIT_ARRAYS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A reference array longer than one split unit is split on its first visit into STEALABLE chunk tasks (tagged work-queue words, halved like HotSpot's partial-array entries), so one huge `Object[]` is no longer one mark worker's serial slot loop. Every element is walked exactly once. Inert on a one-worker pool. Read once per mark pool. Engagement: `array_splits=` / `array_tasks=<pushed>/<run>` on `[GC] zgc-mark-parallel:` and, sinceon `[GC] zgc-markend:`. `=0` restores the private in-progress slot (one worker per array). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SPLIT_CHUNK` | **unset** (= the yield chunk) | The unit of a split array's chunk tasks, in elements, apart from `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_REF_CHUNK` (the yield chunk). Smaller (512-1024; HotSpot splits at `ZMarkPartialArrayMinSize`, 512 wide refs) spreads a mid-size array over more workers; larger cuts the task count on a 10^7-element array (each task is still walked in yield-chunk steps). Unset or `0` is byte for byte the behaviour before the key. Read once per mark pool; inert without `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SPLIT_ARRAYS`. Deciding rows G1-G3 of internal records. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_PREFETCH_RUNS` | **0 = off** (`1..16` = distance; `on` = 2) | Prefetches the entry `d` below the popped one, but only inside a run of at least 8 entries pushed by one scan (a reference-array chunk, a wide object) (`mark_prefetch_runs_distance`, `gc/src/zgc/mark.rs:496`). ab-s2b: within 1-2 % of the default everywhere. Opt-in. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_MEMO_WAYS` | **8** (numeric, `1..8`) | Entries in each mark worker's direct-mapped oop-map memo (`mark_memo_ways`, `gc/src/zgc/mark.rs:1431`; `0` or a non-number gives 8, larger values clamp to 8). `1` is the serial loop's one-entry memo, kept as the A/B arm: ab-s2b `MEMO_WAYS=1` on `map` +5 %. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_ROOTS_OWN_STRIPES` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | A root batch is published as one chunk per worker, each on a stripe that worker owns, instead of one chunk per stripe over all `4 x W` stripes; the objects claimed are the same (`mark_roots_own_stripes_enabled`, `gc/src/zgc/mark.rs:462`). ab-s2b: tree -1.7 %, hubs -0.6 %, inside the noise. Opt-in. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SATB_DEFER_REGISTRY` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | Concurrent mark only: the SATB barrier's per-store registry screen moves to the thread buffer's flush, beside the mark-bit filter (needs `CRATONVM_ZGC_SATB_FILTER_MARKED=2`, thread buffers and the mark bitmap; seeded per heap at `gc/src/zgc.rs:4589`). Never measured; inert while `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START` is 0 (the default). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_ARMED_REF_CAS` | **OFF** (`=1` opt-in) | With the ZGC read barrier ARMED, `Unsafe` reference CAS on an array slot is served as heal-then-CAS (`VmHeap::cas_ref_slot_barriered`) instead of falling back to the lock path (`vm/src/vm/vm_exec.rs:70627`). Nothing arms the barrier outside tests, so the switch is unreachable in production today. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_PAGE_SURVEY` | **OFF** (diagnostic, `=1`) | *; row added.* Makes `gc/src/zgc/page.rs` reachable for a per-cycle survey of the arena as logical pages; changes no allocation or collection decision (`page_survey_enabled`, `gc/src/zgc/page.rs:1567`, read once per heap). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_PAGE_CENSUS` | **OFF** (diagnostic, `=1`) | *; row added.* One extra streaming pass over both bitmaps per sweep, printing `[GC] zgc-sweep-pages: wholly_dead=` (about 16 MB read for a 512 MB arena, inside the pause). Read per cycle (`zgc_sweep_page_census_enabled`, `gc/src/zgc/sweep.rs:105`). The census that decides the page-chunk proposals. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE_BUDGET_MB` | **unset** (= `ZFWD_DEFAULT_MAX_EVACUATION_BYTES`) | *; row added.* Numeric: the per-cycle evacuation budget of the slide's selector in MiB; `0` = unlimited (a zero budget is `CRATONVM_ZGC_RELOCATE=0`); a value that does not parse keeps the default (`zgc_relocate_budget_bytes`, `gc/src/zgc.rs:22002`, latched per process). Read `relocation_budget_engagement`'s `cycles_truncated` first: at zero the budget does not bind. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_JMX_YOUNG_BELOW_FLOOR` | **on** in the mode (`=0` kill) | default ON since: with it CVM answers `young_holds_survivors=true` deterministically (t20 `gn7_JY_1j` / `_1jc`, t21 `pl10_JY_1jc`), HotSpot 25 answered `true` in 5 of its 9 runs of `Zt16Gn6JmxYoungPool` (its own answer depends on whether its tenuring promoted the held set), the kill `false`; the same rule as `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_JMX_MAJOR_YOUNG` after a major.* Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: while the last collection was a young cycle on the young-aged sweep, the JMX `ZGC Old Generation` pool leaves out the young survivors below the floor that cycle measured (they count in `ZGC Young Generation`, as after HotSpot 25's minor); the next major lowers it. `=0`: they count old (the t6 `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_YOUNG_POOL_LIVE` figure). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_JMX_MAJOR_YOUNG` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`: while the last collection was a whole-heap cycle, the JMX `ZGC Old Generation` pool leaves out the survivors that cycle's sweep left young by age below the large-object region (`ZSweepShard::gen_young_low_bytes`); they count in `ZGC Young Generation`. HotSpot 25's major runs a young collection that tenures by age, so after a `Warmup` / `Proactive` / rule major its young pool keeps them; only its `System.gc` pre-clean promotes every survivor (here too: that sweep ages them all to the promotion age, so the figure is 0). Before this, the old live figure held them and the young pool read ~0. Also applies before anything was promoted. A young cycle resets the figure (its below-floor survivors are `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_JMX_YOUNG_BELOW_FLOOR`'s). Rows `pl10_JM_*` (`Zt16Gn6JmxYoungPool 100 8 major`). `=0`: they count old, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_STALL_PRECLEAN` | **off** (`=1` opt-in) | * Under `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_GENERATION_STATISTICS`: a ZGC major that began with an allocation failure latched (`hard_alloc_failure`) prints the two young blocks of a pre-cleaning major whatever its cause, as HotSpot 25's `should_preclean_young` does when `is_alloc_stalling_for_old` (JDK 25 `zDriver.cpp`). A failure-door major is `Allocation Stall` and pre-cleans by its cause already; this reaches a major another door started while an allocation had failed. Opt-in until a probe reaches that state on both VMs. |
| `CRATONVM_EMPTY_FINALIZER_NOT_FINALIZABLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Every collector, both modes: a class whose own `finalizeV` is the single `return` bytecode (HotSpot's `_has_empty_finalizer`) is not finalizable, nor is a subclass that does not override it again -- `java.lang.Enum` and `ThreadPoolExecutor` declare such a `finalize`, as does any `protected void finalize {}`. Its instances are never registered, so a `PhantomReference` to one is enqueued by the first collection that finds it unreachable, as on HotSpot 25 (it waited for a finalization round). `=0`: an empty body counts as a finalizer, as before. Separately, the launcher now takes `--finalization=enabled|disabled` (JEP 421, `VmConfig::finalization_enabled`): disabled gives no class a finalizer. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT` | **off** (`=1` opt-in) | The concurrent ZGC profile, stage 1 of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md): one switch for every built stage-1 piece, so a run measures the profile as a whole. Today it turns on `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER` (every trigger opens a concurrent mark), `CRATONVM_ZGC_SYSTEM_GC_CONCURRENT`,  `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_SWEEP`,  `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS`,  `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_END_SERVICE`,  `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_CONTINUE` (both default ON sinceacting wherever the every-trigger mode is armed) and  `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_RATE_START`. An explicit value of a piece's own switch wins over the profile, so `=0` on a piece is its kill inside it. Read at heap construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SYSTEM_GC_CONCURRENT` | **off** (`=1` opt-in; on under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `=0` kills it there) | A ZGC `System.gc` / `jcmd GC.run` runs HotSpot 25's shape: a concurrent cycle that starts after the call (Pause Mark Start), a concurrent mark with every other thread running while the caller waits (it still takes part in safepoints; bounded at 10 s), and the requested collection as that cycle's mark end, which closes the cycle with its mark set instead of abandoning it (`CRATONVM_ZGC_FULL_REQUEST_FRESH_MARK`). Only in the every-trigger mode (the request rides its request slot), not in generational mode; JVMTI `ForceGarbageCollection` and the metadata door keep the stop-the-world collection. A peer's collection that closes the cycle first (allocation failure, the urgent reserve) falls back to the old path. Counted on `[GC] zgc-conc-trigger:` (`ct_sysgc_requests`, `ct_sysgc_opened`, `ct_sysgc_served`, `ct_sysgc_lost`, `ct_sysgc_not_opened`, `ct_sysgc_held`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER` | **OFF** (`=1` read, `=mark` read + mark) | Arms the ZGC read barrier at heap construction in **plain-store mode**: every reference read the heap serves (`get_field` compact and legacy arms, `get_array_element`, so natives, JNI, reflection, `Unsafe`, VarHandles and the JIT field/element helpers) goes through `load_barrier_slot`, the JIT read bounds are cleared and inline field loads are not emitted, the interpreter's quickened arms decline; stores still write plain pointers, and a word without bit 63 is answered as the pointer it is. `mark` also publishes each loaded reference to the concurrent marker (additive: SATB stays on). Output must not change; it costs throughput. Compiled inline `aaload` (both tiers) is not routed yet. `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=1` prints the census `[GC] zgc-load-barrier: lb_armed= ... lb_routed= lb_plain= lb_colored= lb_marked= lb_unrouted=` at exit; `=2` also makes the heap accessors skip their armed arm and count it as `lb_unrouted` (the counter's positive control). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_INLINE_LOAD_BARRIER` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only under `CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=colored`) | Under colored stores both JIT tiers read a reference-array element (`aaload`, `ArrayLoad(Ref)`) inline and decode it in place -- `TEST RAX,RAX; JNS done; AND RAX, offset mask; ADD RAX, arena base` -- instead of calling `jit_aaload`. Sound only while the good color is pinned at `Remapped` (no colored word is ever bad); the color flip must withdraw it or add the bad-mask slow path. `=0` restores the helper call. Inline sites are not counted in the census `lb_jit_slot` / `lb_routed` / `lb_colored`. volatile fields, `Reference.referent`, statics and spliced single-pass bodies keep `jit_getfield`, and `=0` sends field loads back to it too. engagement line `splice zgc-colored-inline-getfield`), under the same `=0`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_SWEEP` | **off** (`=1` opt-in; on under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `=0` kills it there) | Stage 1d of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md): the sweep leaves the pause. A whole-heap, non-generational ZGC cycle on the bitmap sweep ends its pause at the sweep (HotSpot's Pause Mark End): the pause prunes the object-start registry and the monitor table and takes the low free list; the collecting thread then sweeps after the release, with every other mutator running, installs the free spans under the arena lock, and runs every consumer of the sweep's totals there (the `allocated` gauge and re-arm floor, the pause-target budget, the metrics, the mark-bit clear, `[GC] zgc-real:` / `zgc-pause:` / `zgc-reclaim:`), before the `-Xlog:gc` line and the JFR / JMX events, which report the swept heap. Until the install the dead objects are on no free list (an allocation bumps or takes an old large-object hole; one that cannot be served finishes the sweep and retries) and `needs_gc` starts no new cycle. A deferred cycle never slides; after 4 deferred cycles in a row with the slide enabled one sweeps in the pause. The pause keeps the sweep (refused) for: no runner (a direct `collect_garbage`), generational mode, a per-object sweep switch, a monitor cleanup that wants the dead list, a frozen peer's published TLAB tail, an allocation failure or short headroom, the corpse / lazy-starts diagnostics. `System.gc` returns after the sweep. Per cycle under `--verbose:gc`: `[GC] zgc-concurrent-phases: cp_markend_pause_us= cp_sweep_pause_us= cp_refs_pause_us= cp_sweep_wait_us= cp_sweep_conc_us= cp_sweep_install_us= cp_runner= cp_mark=`; at exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`) `[GC] zgc-concurrent-sweep: csw_deferred= csw_refused= csw_last_refusal= csw_by_*= csw_alloc_retries= ...`. Read at heap construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_INIT_UNCOMMIT_DISABLED_FIRST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC's `-Xlog:gc+init` uncommit line follows HotSpot 25's `try_enable_uncommit` order: an explicit `-XX:-ZUncommit` prints `Uncommit: Disabled` even when `-Xms` equals `-Xmx`, where this VM printed `Uncommit: Implicitly Disabled (-Xms equals -Xmx)`. Rows `h3_U*` of internal records. `=0`: the implicit line first, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_SAFEPOINT_LINES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Under `-Xlog:safepoint` a ZGC collection prints HotSpot 25 ZGC's `Safepoint "<op>"` lines: the VM operations the real JFR backend writes for it (`ZMarkStartYoungAndOld`, `ZMarkEndOld`, `ZRelocateStartOld`; `ZMarkStartYoung`, `ZMarkEndYoung`, `ZRelocateStartYoung` for a minor), cut where its pause rows are cut (`CRATONVM_ZGC_PAUSE_PHASES`; a collection without a split prints the one opening operation), the first line with the pause's time since last and time to safepoint, the rest 0. A concurrent cycle's own mark-start pause prints `ZMarkStartYoungAndOld`. It printed no line for any ZGC pause (had left ZGC out of scope), so the default collector's `-Xlog:safepoint` was empty. HotSpot runs 5 operations per major and 8 per `System.gc` (the intended 3-pause difference recorded in `docs/GC.md`). Rows `h3_S*` of internal records. `=0`: no ZGC line, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_COLOR_FLIP` | **off** (`=1` opt-in; acts only under `CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=colored`) | The ZGC color flip: a concurrent cycle (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START` / `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`) makes its mark bit (`Marked0` / `Marked1`, alternating) the good color at the mark-start pause and `Remapped` again at the closing pause, so the first load of each colored slot per phase takes the barrier's slow path (marks the target while marking, self-heals the slot with one CAS). Both JIT tiers test a loaded reference element against the heap's bad-mask cell and call `jit_aaload` on a bad word instead of the pinned inline decode (: an inline compact reference field load likewise, calling `jit_getfield`;: in spliced single-pass bodies too) (`CRATONVM_ZGC_INLINE_LOAD_BARRIER=0` still sends every element load to the helper). SATB stays on and relocation stays stop-the-world, so output must not change. The census line (`CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=1`) carries `lb_color_flip= lb_flips_mark= lb_flips_remap=`; compiled engagement shows as `zgc-color-flip-aaload` lines under `CRATONVM_DBG_JITC=1`. referent` keep-alive (`Reference.get` interpreted, compiled, native or through a handle / reflection / `Unsafe` slot-0 read, JNI `GetObjectField` of the referent) marks the referent through the load barrier's mark step instead of the SATB pre-barrier; census key `lb_referent_keep_alive=`. Without the flip it is the SATB pre-barrier as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS` | **off** (`=1` opt-in; on under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `=0` kills it there) | Stage 1c of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md), its first step: the referent slot keeps its referent through the collection. On a non-generational ZGC collection the VM's pre-collection pass no longer nulls the `referent` of each active Weak / Phantom (and policy-condemned Soft) `Reference`; it hands those rows to the collection, whose markers (serial, parallel, the concurrent mark end's refresh and kept-soft roots) hide the slot, and whose pause nulls it after the mark is final and before the sweep when the referent was not marked. Java-visible answers are those of the null pass by construction; what changes is that no live referent's slot ever reads a transient `null` and no slot ever names freed storage -- the precondition for moving the VM's reference round out of the pause, which is NOT done (the page's 1c lists what blocks it). Refused per collection in generational mode (`crf_refused`). The census of the VM reference round runs with the switch off too: per collection under `--verbose:gc` `[GC] zgc-vm-refs: cycle= vr_pre_us= vr_post_us= vr_jni_us= vr_mode=skip|null vr_skip_rows= vr_stw_cleared=`, the sum as `cp_vm_refs_pause_us=` on a deferred cycle's `[GC] zgc-concurrent-phases:`, and at exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`) `[GC] zgc-concurrent-refs: crf_enabled= crf_skip_cycles= crf_skip_rows= crf_stw_cleared= crf_refused= crf_rounds= crf_pre_us= crf_post_us= crf_jni_us= crf_max_round_us=`. Read at heap construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REF_ROUND` | **off** (`=1` opt-in; on under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `=0` kills it there) | On a ZGC cycle that defers its sweep (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_SWEEP`) and hid its referent slots (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS`), the VM's reference round -- the row phases, the prune, the enqueues, the finalizer and cleaner submissions -- runs after the release, on the collecting thread, before the sweep's install, as HotSpot's `Concurrent Process Non-Strong References`. The loader / class-unload reconciliation, the JNI weak-global sweep and the root update stay in the pause. The round's locks (the class manager, then the reference processor) are taken as the last thing in the pause and held through the release, so no row joins and no class is defined between the mark and the round; the sweep's install waits for the round (an allocation that needs the space waits at most 2 s, then fails as before). Refused (kept in the pause) when a pointer moved, a `ReferenceQueue` class has no `lock` field (the direct-write enqueue), or `CRATONVM_DBG_NO_REFPROC` / `CRATONVM_WEAKREF_CLEAR=0`. Census `[GC] zgc-concurrent-ref-round: crr_*` (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_START_BINDS_REFERENCES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC concurrent mark start (every-trigger, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`, a concurrent `System.gc`) binds the `Reference`s registered at allocation (discovery at allocation: every `--jdk-only` VM and `--compatible` over a real JDK) before it hands the marker its referent skip set (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_REF_SKIP`), as the collection's own pre-collection pass does. Without it the cycle traced the referents of every `Reference` made since the last collection strongly, so a `System.gc` that owns its concurrent cycle (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`) returned with those weak referents still set (`Zt1MkSystemGcFresh`: `alive_after_gc=80000`, HotSpot 25 `0`). `=0`: they are bound at the next collection, as before. Read per mark start. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_START_ROOTS_PENDING_FINALIZERS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC concurrent mark start (every-trigger, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`, a concurrent `System.gc`) roots the objects queued for `finalize` and not yet finalized, as G1's initial mark does (`CRATONVM_G1_MARK_PENDING_FINALIZERS`). Without it a `finalize` that the delivery thread ran during the concurrent phase and that stored its object (or an object only it reached) into an already-scanned object -- a static list, a node allocated black -- left that object unmarked at the mark end, and the sweep freed it while reachable. HotSpot keeps it strongly reachable from `Finalizer`'s queue for the whole cycle. `=0`: the queue is not a mark-start root, as before. Read per mark start. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_LAST_DITCH_FRESH_MARK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | With a ZGC concurrent cycle open when the allocation ladder reaches its last-ditch soft-reference clear, that clear-all collection carries the collecting thread's full request, so the open cycle is abandoned (`CRATONVM_ZGC_FULL_REQUEST_FRESH_MARK`) and the collection marks from scratch, as HotSpot's `Allocation Stall` cycle starts after the stall. A concurrently marked close also stood the soft-precise probe down (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SOFT_CLEAR_PRECISE_FIRST`), so a soft reference whose referent a static still holds was cleared when a stale compiled-frame word named that referent (`Zr7SpStaticHolderStaleWord`: `soft_cleared true`, HotSpot `false`). `=0`: the collection closes the open cycle with its mark set, as before. Read only with a cycle open at the rung's entry. |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_RELOC_SELECT_CENSUS` | off (debug `=1`) | At each whole-heap ZGC closing pause, after the mark and before either sweep arm (zc2's deferred sweep included), the relocation set is chosen from the mark's survivors over the 2 MiB region grid (empty regions apart, regions a live object from below reaches into kept out, regions holding a pin word or a VM TLAB tail end kept out) under the slide's own policy, and compared region by region with the slide's selection on the same cycle (after its pin screen, before its cycle gates). One `[GC] zgc-rsel:` line per cycle: `rsel_regions=` (grid regions, the denominator), `rsel_set=`, `rsel_empty=`, `rsel_straddled=`, `rsel_excluded=`, `rsel_compared=` (0 when no slide selected: refused, deferred-sweep or relocation-off cycles), `rsel_agree=`, `rsel_r1_only=`, `rsel_slide_only=`, `rsel_retracted=`, `rsel_us=`, `rsel_young_skipped=`. Reads headers and mark bits only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REGION_RELOCATE` | off (opt-in `=1`) | The low slide's evacuating arm (as `CRATONVM_ZGC_SLIDE_EVACUATE=1`, which it implies, with the same gate: a cycle after an allocation failure, on a pressure tier or without tail headroom slides instead) copies its survivors through a per-region relocation cycle: one forwarding table per selected region sized from its exact survivor count, regions claimed in address order, copy-then-install into the to-space window, each region sealed. Same copies and destinations as the evacuating loop. the `PointerMap` still reads the pairs. Under `-verbose:gc` one `[GC] zgc-rreloc:` line per drain (`rreloc_regions= rreloc_objects= rreloc_bytes= rreloc_stays= rreloc_halted= rreloc_alloc_stops= rreloc_refusals= rreloc_xcheck_bad= rreloc_cycles= rreloc_fallbacks=`; `rreloc_xcheck_bad` must stay 0). Unset or `=0`: today's slide, byte for byte. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_ALWAYS_PRE_TOUCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * `-XX:+AlwaysPreTouch` (`VmConfig::always_pre_touch`, per VM; launcher and `libcratonvm`, last spelling wins) makes the ZGC heap touch every page of its `-Xms` startup commit before the first allocation, and every later commit that makes a granule resident (including one the readable give-back had reset) as it is made: `madvise(MADV_POPULATE_WRITE)` on Linux 5.14+, else one value-preserving compare-and-exchange per 4 KiB. The `gc,init` block prints `Pre-touch: Enabled`. Costs start-up time and RSS in proportion to `-Xms`, as on HotSpot; nothing without the flag. G1 and the Generational backend do not honour it (their line stays a true `Disabled`). `=0`: the option is accepted and ignored, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_END_SERVICE` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only where the every-trigger mode is armed: `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1` or `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER=1`) | default ON since(rows `zc6_*`: parity SAME in both modes, CPU 0.59x of the kill on the idle shape, <= 1.05x where polls close the cycles; the default collector never arms it, so no tick thread starts).* Stage 1f of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md): the mark end follows the fixed point. Under the every-trigger mode the closing collection of a concurrent cycle is otherwise the first allocation poll after the mark pool's fixed point, so a program that stops allocating after a burst keeps the cycle open and the SATB barrier armed on every reference store. With the switch the ZGC director tick thread (started for it) looks at an open every-trigger cycle every 1 ms (every 10 ms with none open) and, when the pool is at its fixed point on two looks in a row with no collection closing it meanwhile, runs the closing collection on a thread attached for it through the allocation-threshold door -- the cycle keeps its trigger's cause, the collection count is the one the poll would have made -- as HotSpot's driver runs Pause Mark End when marking completes. A cycle a `System.gc` request owns is left to its caller; generational and window (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START=<pct>`) modes are inert. The census of every concurrent close runs with the switch off too: per cycle under `--verbose:gc` `[GC] zgc-markend-work: cycle= mew_caught_up= mew_pushed= mew_claimed= mew_conc_marked= mew_end_marked= mew_conc_scanned= mew_end_scanned= mew_end_us=`, and at exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`) `[GC] zgc-markend-census: mec_service= mec_closed= mec_caught_up_at_entry= mec_fallbacks= mec_pushed= mec_claimed= mec_conc_marked= mec_end_marked= mec_conc_scanned= mec_end_scanned= mec_end_us= mec_end_max_us= mec_svc_ticks= mec_svc_closes= mec_svc_lost= mec_svc_stale= mec_svc_extra=`. Read at heap construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_EXIT_FINISHES_SWEEP` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only when a deferred sweep is pending, i.e. under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_SWEEP`) | md` item 28d).* The VM's exit finishes a deferred sweep still pending (an exit from another thread between a deferring pause and its install), or waits for the thread running it, before it reads the heap it reports: the `-Xlog:gc+exit` heap lines, the `[GC]` summary and the end-of-run metrics row describe the cycle's end instead of the unswept `allocated` (a thread still running may defer a later cycle after them). Counted as `csw_by_exit=` on `[GC] zgc-concurrent-sweep:`. `=0`: the exit reads the heap as it finds it, as before. Read once per exit, only with a sweep pending. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE_CONCURRENT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md`).* The weak interned-String table (`CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE`) also engages on a ZGC heap that marks concurrently (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER=1`, any `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`); generational mode stays strong only under `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_WEAK_STRING_TABLE=0` . The mark start's and the closing collection's root scans leave the pool out, every pool hit while a cycle marks is logged through ZGC's SATB pre-barrier (as HotSpot's `StringTable` peek keeps its String alive; under `CRATONVM_ZGC_COLOR_FLIP=1` through the load barrier's mark step instead,), and the closing collection's `update_all_roots` sweep drops the entries whose String the final mark left unmarked (in the pause, also on a deferred-sweep cycle). Without it every interned String was immortal on such a heap: `Zr1RfInternProbe weakclear` printed `dropped-intern-weak-cleared=false` (HotSpot 25 `true`) and `churn` ran out of memory at `-Xmx64m`. `CRATONVM_DBG_STRING_TABLE=1` appends `zgc_keep_alives=` (pool hits logged during a mark) to its `[string-table]` line. `=0`: the strong pool on a concurrently marking heap, as before. Read at VM construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REFERENT_READS_WEAK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md`).* The VM's reference round (the pre-collection null / hide pass and the bind of allocation-registered `Reference`s) reads each referent slot with ZGC's non-marking `Weak` load (`VmHeap::get_field_weak`). Only an armed read barrier (`CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=colored`) reads differently: under `CRATONVM_ZGC_COLOR_FLIP=1` the pass runs at a concurrent cycle's closing collection before the remap flip, while marking is still up, so the strong read of a slot no mutator had loaded during the phase (a `Remapped` word, bad under the flip) MARKED the referent the pass was about to judge, and those weak references were never cleared (`Zt1MkSystemGcFresh`: `alive_after_gc > 0`, HotSpot 25 `0`). `=0`: the strong read, as before. Read once per pass. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REGION_QUARANTINE` | off (opt-in `=1`; `=2` without the hand-over) | Acts only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_REGION_RELOCATE=1`. The region relocation cycle outlives the closing pause: the from-space it vacated is withheld from the free list as `CRATONVM_ZGC_EVAC_QUARANTINE=1` withholds it, the cycle answers the forwarding barrier's stale-address lookups (`forwarded_after_slide`) first, and the slide does not publish the pairs the cycle answers into its relocation table. Both are released at the next collection's mark end, before either sweep arm; there `=1` hands the cycle's pairs to the relocation table (the fallback for an address held across that pause, until the remap-in-mark piece R4), and `=2` drops them. Under `-verbose:gc` one `[GC] zgc-rquar:` line per release (`rquar_regions= rquar_pairs= rquar_table_kb= rquar_handover= rquar_handed= rquar_kept= rquar_released= rquar_unpublished= rquar_answered= rquar_stale_answered= rquar_replaced=`; `rquar_stale_answered` counts, under `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_QUARANTINE_DEAD_CHECK=1`, the stale words at release the cycle answers). Unset or `=0`: R2 as above. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REMAP_IN_MARK` | off (opt-in `=1`) | Acts with `CRATONVM_ZGC_REGION_QUARANTINE` (the kept cycle) under `CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=colored` with `CRATONVM_ZGC_COLOR_FLIP=1`, on a cycle that closed a concurrent mark. The slide no longer rewrites, in its pause, a colored compact field or reference-array element that still carries the closing mark's color and names an object the kept cycle moved: the load barrier forwards such a word through the kept cycle and heals it, the next mark's markers forward and heal every slot they visit (the referent hole of a `Reference` included, healed but not traced), and the SATB pre-barrier logs the forwarded value; the cycle is released at that mark's end as R3 releases it. HotSpot's ZGC has no rewrite pass: its next mark remaps what the load barrier did not. Legacy cells, roots, statics and the VM's side tables are still rewritten in the pause. Refused (every slot rewritten) without the flip, after a stop-the-world mark, and in generational mode. Census `[GC] zgc-remap-in-mark: rim_*` (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_REFERS_TO_NO_KEEPALIVE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | `Reference.refersTo0` and `PhantomReference.refersTo0` (the natives, the JIT direct helpers and the leaf-native arm) read the referent with ZGC's non-marking `Weak` load (`NativeHeapAccess::get_field_no_keepalive` -> `VmHeap::get_field_weak`), as HotSpot 25 loads it `AS_NO_KEEPALIVE`. Only an armed read barrier (`CRATONVM_ZGC_LOAD_BARRIER=colored`) reads differently: there the strong read of a bad colored referent word during a mark (under `CRATONVM_ZGC_COLOR_FLIP=1`, every `Remapped` word) marked the referent, so a `refersTo` alone kept it alive for that cycle and its `WeakReference` cleared one cycle later than on HotSpot. `=0`: the strong, marking read, as before. Asked only while the barrier is armed; read once per process. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TRANSPARENT_HUGE_PAGES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Linux: `-XX:+UseTransparentHugePages` (`VmConfig::use_transparent_huge_pages`, per VM; launcher and `libcratonvm`, last spelling wins, an explicit `-XX:-UseLargePages` turns it off) gets HotSpot 25 ZGC's large-page decision from the kernel's THP modes (`/sys/kernel/mm/transparent_hugepage/enabled` and `shmem_enabled`): with THP unsupported (`enabled` not `always`/`madvise`) `os::large_page_init`'s `[warning][pagesize] UseTransparentHugePages disabled, ...` line on stdout; with `shmem_enabled` `never` the measured `[warning][pagesize] Shared memory transparent huge pages are not enabled in the OS. ...` line  and the `gc,init` line `Large Page Support: Disabled (OS enforced)`; otherwise `Enabled (Transparent)` and the heap reservation is advised `MADV_HUGEPAGE` before the startup commit is touched (it overrides `CRATONVM_ZGC_ARENA_NO_THP`). Without the flag a `shmem_enabled` of `always` prints `Enabled (Transparent, OS enforced)`. Texts recalled from HotSpot's `ZLargePages`; rows `h7_T*` decide. An explicit `-XX:+UseLargePages` without THP (`VmConfig::use_large_pages`) prints the measured `[warning][pagesize] UseLargePages disabled, no large pages configured and available on the system.` line when `/proc/meminfo` reports no free hugetlbfs page ; explicit hugetlbfs pages themselves are not used. `=0`: the flag is accepted and ignored and the line is `Disabled`, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_OOM_LADDER_FRESH_MARK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * When the allocation ladder reaches its clear-soft rung with no active soft reference on a ZGC heap where a concurrent cycle is open, or where the last collection closed one with its mark set (the ladder's forced collection reused a snapshot taken before the failure), the rung runs one collection with the collecting thread's full request, so the mark starts now (an open cycle is abandoned, `CRATONVM_ZGC_FULL_REQUEST_FRESH_MARK`) before `OutOfMemoryError`, as HotSpot fails a stalled allocation only after a cycle that started after the stall. Reached only under the concurrent profile (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`); the default stop-the-world ZGC never marks concurrently. Engagement `oom_fresh_marks=` on `[GC] zgc-refusal:` (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`). `=0`: no collection there, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_OUT_OF_MEMORY` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * With `-Xlog:gc` (the exact `gc` tag set at `info`, or `-verbose:gc`), a heap `OutOfMemoryError` (`Java heap space`) raised on ZGC prints HotSpot 25 ZGC's `Out Of Memory (<thread>)` line (`ZHeap::out_of_memory`), after the stall's `Allocation Stall (<thread>)` line, as measured on 25.0.3 (`Zr2CpCapacity grow -Xmx256m`). A logging spelling. `=0`: no line, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_JMX_MANAGEABLE_HEAP_FREE_RATIOS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 30b).* Every collector, both modes, per VM. On the `com.sun.management.internal.Flag` surface the modelled `{manageable}` rows `MinHeapFreeRatio` and `MaxHeapFreeRatio` are `writeable` and `external`, as jmm's `GetVMGlobals` lists a manageable flag, and ZGC's `SoftMaxHeapSize` row is `external`, so `HotSpotDiagnosticMXBean.getDiagnosticOptions` lists them. `setVMOption("MinHeapFreeRatio", n)` / `("MaxHeapFreeRatio", n)` writes the value (origin `MANAGEMENT`, `{management}` in `jcmd VM.flags`) and hands it to the Generational / G1 sizing policy; a value outside `0 ... 100` throws `IllegalArgumentException: <name> error: must have value in range [0...100]`, one that crosses the other ratio `<name> error: value violates its flag's constraint.` (HotSpot's `WriteableFlags` texts, recalled; row `x20_mf*` decides). It answered `VM Option "MinHeapFreeRatio" is not writeable`. `=0`: every modelled row read-only and non-external, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_VM_FLAGS_ERGONOMIC_GC_ORIGIN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 30c).* Every collector, both modes. The modelled `UseZGC` / `UseG1GC` row of the collector this VM runs without that flag on the command line (the default ZGC; G1 chosen another way) reads origin `{ergonomic}`, as HotSpot's `GCConfig::select_gc_ergonomically` sets the collector it picks: `jcmd VM.flags` then names it (`-XX:+UseZGC`), PrintFlagsFinal prints `{ergonomic}` and `getVMOption("UseZGC").getOrigin` is `ERGONOMIC`. | `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_CONTINUE` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only where the every-trigger mode is armed: `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1` or `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_EVERY_TRIGGER=1`) | default ON since(rows `zc8_*`: parity SAME in both modes on every arm, the four-releases-per-cycle positive control included; CPU 1.04x / 1.01x of the kill; the default collector never arms it).* Stage 1f of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md): Concurrent Mark Continue. A ZGC collection through the allocation-threshold door -- the occupancy poll (not a latched request) or the mark-end service (`CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_END_SERVICE`) -- that closes an open every-trigger cycle first runs a Pause Mark End attempt inside its stopped world: it hands the mark pool every thread's SATB buffer, the heap ingress and the roots again, without disarming the barrier or ending the cycle. If nothing new is claimed and the pool is at its fixed point the same pause collects; otherwise the world is released without a collection (`[GC] pause: gc=- door=zgc-mark-end`, one `ZGC Major Pauses` pause, `Safepoint "ZMarkEndOld"` under `-Xlog:safepoint`), the pool keeps marking, and the next fixed point tries again -- HotSpot 25's failed Pause Mark End and Concurrent Mark Continue. The closing collection's `-Xlog:gc,phases` lines then carry `GC(n) O: Pause Mark End <ms>` and `GC(n) O: Concurrent Mark Continue <ms>` per failed attempt, before its own `O: Pause Mark End`. At most 4 failed attempts per cycle, and none at the urgent reserve: those closes drain in the pause, as before. `System.gc`, an allocation failure, the timer and generational / window modes never attempt. At exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`): `[GC] zgc-markcontinue: mcc_enabled= mcc_attempts= mcc_continues= mcc_completes= mcc_exhausted= mcc_urgent= mcc_pushed= mcc_claimed= mcc_pause_us= mcc_continue_us= mcc_max_cycle_continues= mcc_svc_continued=`. Read at heap construction. |

Under the concurrent profile the closing pause carries the root scan, the SATB replay and the VM reference round, and the loop read each long one as "walk less": the t20 `zc1_A2_cm_Z` row ran 66 cycles, 61 of them allocation-budget triggers sized down to 11-20 MB, against 11 on the stop-the-world arm (`collection_ms` 48.6 s against 31.2 s). HotSpot 25's ZGC has no pause-length trigger. Engagement `cpb_forgets=` on `[GC] zgc-markcontinue:` (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`). `=0`: the loop is fed as before. Read at heap construction. |

On an all-garbage cycle the list streamed every covered word twice to find a few survivors: 3-4 ms of a ~20 ms pause on the r21 lean row, 25 -> 2 CPU samples. Same words: the prune runs stop-the-world with no writer of the registry. `=0`: the locked form. Read once per sweep. |
