A ZGC allocation poll that finds no concurrent cycle open or requested asks for one at HotSpot's allocation-rate point (`rule_major_allocation_rate`, `ZAllocationSpikeTolerance` 2): when free space is at or below the urgent reserve plus twice the allocation expected during a mark (smoothed rate x smoothed concurrent-phase duration x 3/2), while the heap can still finish a mark (free above the reserve plus one expected allocation), after the three warm-up cycles and at least half the reserve of allocation since the last collection. Without it the cycle was requested only when a clause fired (the live-bytes threshold at 75 % of `-Xmx`, the allocation budget, headroom) and, on a churning heap, the clause fired where no mark could finish, so the collection was stop-the-world (`ct_no_headroom`; `MtChurnProbe 8 2500 384 -Xmx1g` under the profile: 35 of 53 collections stop-the-world marked, 17.4 s of 54.9 s wall in pauses). A request of this rule whose mark start is refused (the take-over cover) or that no site serves is dropped instead of becoming a stop-the-world collection, and the rule waits that grace of allocation before asking again; at the urgent reserve it collects. At exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`): `[GC] zgc-rate-start: crs_enabled= crs_requests= crs_opened= crs_dropped=`. Read at heap construction. |

HotSpot runs `finalize` on its `Finalizer` daemon and notifications on its `Notification Thread`, so a finalizer backlog never holds a notification back; here one thread does both, and a peer making finalizable garbage (`Zt13X10MissedDrain 40 fin`, every `finalize` on that thread since `CRATONVM_ZGC_FINALIZERS_OFF_CALLER`) kept the step running for as long as it churned. `=0`: one unbounded step per batch, as before. |

| `CRATONVM_ZGC_RUN_FINALIZATION_STALL_WAITS_BATCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | rows `x10_rfg_*`).* ZGC, both modes. `Runtime.runFinalization`'s hand-off waits, after a served step that did not shorten the queue, for a whole delivery batch begun after the request (as G1 / Serial since), so a step wait served between the delivery thread's finalizer slices -- while it delivers the GC notifications `--compatible`'s `System.gc` handed it -- no longer counts toward giving the queue back to the caller (`Zt31X10RunFinalizationGap`: every `finalize` ran on the caller in 10 of 10 runs before, none after; HotSpot none). `=0`: the step wait again. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_STALL_MARK_REACHES_JOINED_PAUSE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | ZGC, with `-XX:ZCollectionInterval`. The allocation-stall classification reaches the cycle that serves the stall, not only a pause the stalled thread runs itself: the stalled thread raises a heap-side pending bit before its pause request and the next cycle's end takes it, so a stall that lost the stop-the-world race and took part in another thread's pause leaves the `Timer` clock alone too (`CRATONVM_ZGC_TIMER_SKIPS_STALL_CYCLES`). `=0`: the collecting thread's mark only. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_EDGE_BOOK` | **on** (`=0` kill) | unit test `zt31x10_the_edge_book_reaches_the_generation_tables`, rows `x10_xl_*`).* ZGC `-Xlog:gc*`. The generation tables print the collection's booked edges: when its mark ran concurrently, the Mark Start column at the occupancy then and the bytes allocated since as the young table's `Allocated`; the slide's copy volume as `Compacted` (the old table's of a major, a minor's own). A stop-the-world collection keeps Mark Start = Mark End = Relocate Start (nothing is allocated or freed inside the pause; HotSpot's Relocate Start frees empty pages, this collector frees every dead object at relocate end). `=0`: the pre-pause figures and zero `Compacted`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_XLOG_LOAD_MMU` | **on** (`=0` kill) | unit test `zt31x10_load_and_mmu_lines_follow_hotspots_zstat`, rows `x10_xl_*`).* ZGC `-Xlog:gc*`. Each generation block carries HotSpot 25's `gc,load` line (`Load: a (p%) / b (q%) / c (r%)`, the one-, five- and fifteen-minute load averages and their share of the CPUs) and `gc,mmu` line (`MMU: 2ms/x%, ...`, the running minimum mutator utilisation over this VM's last 200 pauses, one pause per stop-the-world collection), before its `Metaspace:` line. `-Xlog:gc+load`, `gc+mmu`, `gc+reloc`, `gc+task`, `gc+nmethod`, `gc+stats` select without the `No tag set matches` warning (the last four print nothing: see GC.md's intended differences). `=0`: no load / MMU lines. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_VM_THREAD_CPU` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Linux: `sun.threads.cpu_time.vm` is the CPU the requesting threads spend inside stop-the-world operations (this VM has no VM thread), monotone. Windows reads 0, as HotSpot. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_DEFINE_COUNTS_PLATFORM` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * `sun.cls.defineAppClasses` / `appClassBytes` count the platform loader's defines and the class-path door's defines of platform- and tool-module image classes, which no longer count in `sysClassBytes` (HotSpot's platform / application loader defines them through `JVM_DefineClass`; the JDK's `lib/classlist`, the default CDS archive's list, holds `java.base` classes only). |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_GC_CAUSE_CONCURRENT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * `sun.gc.cause` names the cause of a cycle in progress, as HotSpot's ZGC sets it for the whole cycle: the mark-start pause that opens a concurrent mark (opt-in `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`) writes its trigger's cause, every collection's pause refresh writes `No GC` back. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_ATTACH_HOTSPOT_LOOKUP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | POSIX.* `Perf.attach0` of another VM runs HotSpot 25's `mmap_attach_shared`: `kill(pid, 0)` (`Process not found` / `IOException`), the newest non-empty file of the id in any secure `hsperfdata*` directory (root reads other users'), `/proc/<pid>/root/tmp` and the namespace pid for a container, and HotSpot's `IllegalArgumentException` / `IOException` / `OutOfMemoryError` texts; a file with a second link is a zero-capacity buffer. `=0`: this user's directory only, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_FOREIGN_VIEWS_AT_TEARDOWN` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The foreign views `Perf.attach` mapped and nobody detached are unmapped when the VM that recorded them is torn down (`Drop for SharedVm`: no Java thread of it is left, and its heap goes with it), so an embedder creating VMs one after another no longer leaks them. HotSpot has no twin (one VM per process). |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_GC_THREAD_CPU` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * G1's GC worker threads book their CPU (the evacuation pool's helpers per job, the concurrent-mark workers per step): `sun.threads.cpu_time.gc_parallel_workers` / `gc_conc_mark` (`gc_conc_refine` / `gc_service` read 0: there are no such threads here) and their sum `sun.threads.total_gc_cpu_time`, as HotSpot's G1 `CPUTimeCounters`. ZGC and Serial publish neither and read 0, as HotSpot's. |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_NATIVE_SITE_OWNER_BY_ID` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * The JIT native site cache decides native vs bytecode on the class the site resolved (by id), not on the boot class of the same name. |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_FINALIZER_DELIVERY` | off (debug `=1`) | Every collector, default finalizer policy. One stderr line per delivery-thread finalizer step that ran anything: `[fin-delivery] ran=N left=M step_us=T sliced=<bool>` (throughput = ran / step_us; `left` = the backlog after the step). Names whether a slow or never-ending finalizable churn is the delivery thread falling behind. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_SIGNAL_EXIT_BACKSTOP` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Launcher, every collector. For each caught `SIGTERM` / `SIGINT` / `SIGHUP` (Windows: the console events) a backstop thread waits 5 s for the signal's job to START on its attached Signal Dispatcher thread; a started job is HotSpot's path (hooks, then `Shutdown.exit(128 + signal)`) and is left alone. A job that never started means the VM cannot run Java (wedged in a pause, or on a lock the attach needs): the backstop ends the process with `128 + signal` (the OS default action for an explicit `SIG_DFL`, nothing for `SIG_IGN`) without the VM. A hung `--compatible` `Zt13X10MissedDrain 40 fin` ignored `SIGTERM` until `SIGKILL`; HotSpot exits 143. `=0`: no backstop. |
| `CRATONVM_DBG_LAUNCHER_SIGNAL_STALL_MS` | 0 (debug, a number of ms) | The signal job sleeps this long on its attached thread before it counts as started: a stand-in for a wedged VM, the positive control of `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_SIGNAL_EXIT_BACKSTOP` (rows `fin1_S*`). |
| `CRATONVM_JIT_STATIC_OWNER_LOADS_FIRST` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md`); every collector (not a GC switch), both modes.* A compiled `invokestatic` whose symbolic owner no loader holds yet, with a `Bridge` or `SyntheticStub` registered on the exact triple, loads that owner first by the route the interpreter's `execute_invokestatic` takes (`static_owner_load_route`), so the native-vs-bytecode decision is made on the loaded declaration in both tiers; the JIT native site cache does not cache such a site until the owner is loaded. `--compatible` `ManagementFactory.getPlatformMXBean` answered `null` from an OSR body (row `df7_nl_c`) and the bean when interpreted (`x20_mf_c`). A name whose load fails is remembered per VM and not retried. `=0`: the compiled doors decide on the absent owner (the registered native runs), as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_WEAK_STRING_TABLE` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only in ZGC's generational mode, `CRATONVM_ZGC_GENERATIONAL=1`) | md` item 13).* The weak interned-String table (`CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE`, and under a concurrent mark `CRATONVM_ZGC_WEAK_STRING_TABLE_CONCURRENT`) also engages in the generational mode, which kept the pool strong: every collection -- minor or major -- leaves the pool out of its roots and drops, in its own epilogue, the entries whose String it freed. The verdict is the start registry, and a minor sweeps only non-old bases, so an old pooled String a minor did not trace keeps its entry until a major proves it dead; a dead young one leaves at the minor that freed it (HotSpot 25 keeps it to its old mark -- not visible to Java: nothing else references it). HotSpot 25: `Zr1RfInternProbe weakclear` prints `dropped-intern-weak-cleared=true`, and `churn` completes at `-Xmx64m`. `=0`: the strong pool in the mode, as before. Rows `gn5_gst_*` . |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_MINOR_TIMER` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only in ZGC's generational mode with `-XX:ZCollectionIntervalMinor=<seconds>`) | HotSpot 25's `rule_minor_timer`: the director tick runs a young collection with cause `Timer` (`-Xlog:gc` `Minor Collection (Timer)`) whenever `ZCollectionIntervalMinor` seconds passed since the last collection of any kind ended (HotSpot's young `time_since_last`), after the major rules (`-XX:ZCollectionInterval`'s `Timer`, the idle `Proactive`) as `make_minor_gc_decision` comes after `make_major_gc_decision`. The request does not force a major; before the first promotion the cycle is whole-heap (`Major Collection (Timer)`), as every cycle of the mode is. Off the mode (the default single-generation heap has no young collection) the flag arms nothing. `=0`: the flag is parsed and ignored, as before. Rows `gn5_zim_*`. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SKIP_CLASS_FILTER` | **on** (`=0` kill) | Item 2 of [the concurrent collector program](feature-designs/concurrent-gc-maturation.md) (stage 1c's `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS` cost 1.20x CPU). Mark CPU only; every answer is the same. At each referent-skip snapshot (the parallel stop-the-world mark, a concurrent mark start, the concurrent mark end's refresh) the class id of every member `Reference` sets a bit in a 4 096-bit table, and the mark asks that table, with the class id it already decoded, before the skip set's Bloom filter: an object whose class has no bit is not a member. Without it a heap with many live `Reference`s saturated the 32 768-bit filter (200 000 members under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS` on `Zr1RfWatchCostProbe 200000 20`, or the concurrent cycles' VM rows), and every marked object took the skip set's lock and hash probe. A member that is not a registered base switches the table off for the cycle (`skc_off`); a refresh only adds bits. At exit (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`): `[GC] zgc-skip-class-filter: skc_enabled= skc_fills= skc_off= skc_members= skc_classes=`. Read at heap construction. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_SKIP_BITS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | The referent skip set of a collection (the live `Reference`s whose slot 0 the markers hide: `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_REF_SKIP`'s concurrent rows, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT_REFS`' hidden rows) is a member bitmap in the mark bits' geometry: one bit set per member at the snapshot, one bit test per visited object, every member bit cleared when the cycle ends. No `FxHashSet`, Bloom filter or member-class table is built per collection (the class table's header reads and the set's inserts were 6.5 % of the process on `Zr1RfWatchCostProbe 200000 50` under `CONCURRENT_REFS`). Census `[GC] zgc-skip-bits: skb_*` (`CRATONVM_GC_STATS=1`). `=0`: the hash set behind the class table and the Bloom filter, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MARK_START_GRACE_US` | **5000** (`0` = the global `CRATONVM_XT_FIRST_PASS_GRACE_US`) | How long a ZGC concurrent mark start's pause waits for its mutators to arrive at a safepoint poll on their own before it takes over (freezes) one still in compiled code, capped at 10 ms; an early arrival ends the wait. The take-over cover (`CRATONVM_ZGC_SATB_TAKEOVER_COVER`) refuses to open a cycle whose mark start froze anyone, and at the 200 us global grace a mutator descheduled on a box with more runnable threads than cores was frozen often (MTCHURN: 10 of 28 mark starts refused, each refusal a stop-the-world collection). A mark start is not urgent; HotSpot's is a cooperative safepoint. Census: `ct_start_refusals=` on `[GC] zgc-conc-trigger:` and the `[zgc-satb-window]` lines. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SLIDE_TAIL_ZERO_AT_ALLOCATION` | **on** (`=0` kill) | A ZGC slide no longer zeroes the tail it vacated above its new cursor inside the pause (~41 ms of a ~50 ms slide over ~390 MB on the r21 lean row); the bytes are not listed as zeroed, so the TLAB carve or `alloc_raw` that next hands them out zeroes them, which is where HotSpot pays it. Sound because every probe of a candidate object asks the object-start registry (which the slide rebuilt without the vacated bases), and a forwarding record is read only inside the last slide's stamped range, where no older record can survive (every byte below the cursor the slide found was handed out, and so zeroed, after any earlier slide). `=0`: zeroed in the pause, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_ITL_DEFER_IN_CYCLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 3).* ZGC; matters on `--compatible` (the native inherited-`ThreadLocal` table; `--jdk-only` runs the JDK's own field) and acts only in a concurrent cycle (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`). A concurrent cycle's opening scan and the scans while its mark is in flight pin the inherited values of a queued child `Thread` to the child (`metadata_pin`) instead of rooting them, as a stop-the-world ZGC collection already did; a bucket taken mid-cycle (the child started) leaves a shadow that owns no handle until the closing pause, so the closing scan files every pin owner the opening scan filed and the mark-end repair still certifies the mark. A never-started `Thread` dropped before a concurrent `System.gc` loses its values in that cycle (`UnstartedThreadItlProbe` under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1 --compatible`: `gcCalls=1` as on HotSpot, was 2). `=0`: those scans root the values and no shadow is made, as before. Read per scan and per take. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SIDE_ROW_DEFER_IN_CYCLE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 2).* ZGC; matters on `--compatible` (the native `LogManager` and `SSLContext` side rows) and acts only in a concurrent cycle (`CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT`, `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONC_START`). A concurrent cycle's opening scan and the scans while its mark is in flight pin a side-row value (a logger's handlers and filter, a context's trust managers) to its key (`metadata_pin`) instead of rooting it, as a stop-the-world ZGC collection already did; every scan of the cycle files the union of the pins the cycle has filed since its opening scan, so no pin edge is deleted before the closing pause and the mark-end repair still certifies the mark. A webapp loader whose handlers or trust managers are the only holders unloads in that cycle (`GciEp23LoaderUnloadProbe 32 both` under `CRATONVM_ZGC_CONCURRENT=1 --compatible`: `unloaded=32` as on HotSpot, was 0). `=0`: those scans root the values, as before. Read per scan. |
| `CRATONVM_XT_SKIP_TLAB_POINTER_SLOTS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 1).* Every collector's stop-the-world take-over of a peer frozen in compiled code. The conservative scan of that peer's native stack skips the three pointer words of every published TLAB (`cursor`, `end`, `start`, inside the `JvmThread` that lives on the thread's stack): `end` points at the first object of the next chunk, which belongs to whichever thread took it, so a churn thread's `end` word kept another thread's dead array (or its loader) alive. `=0`: those words are read as candidate references, as before. Read per take-over. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_INIT_LINUX_BACKING_LINES` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 5, host row `tg10_h3`).* ZGC on Linux. The `gc,init` block carries HotSpot 25's five reservation and backing lines between `Large Page Support` and `Min Capacity`: `Address Space Size: unlimited` (`RLIMIT_AS`), `Reserved Space Type: Contiguous/Unrestricted/Complete`, `Reserved Space Size:` 16 x the maximum capacity (limited by the 4T..16T address-offset maximum and half the address-space limit), `Heap Backing File: /memfd:java_heap`, `Heap Backing Filesystem: tmpfs (0x1021994)`. `=0`: the lines are left out, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_MAX_MAP_COUNT_WARNING` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 23i; host rows `tg8_s2`, `tg8_s2h`, `x15_T2`).* ZGC on Linux. A maximum heap needing more than `/proc/sys/vm/max_map_count` mappings (`(max / 2 MiB) * 3 * 1.2`) prints HotSpot 25's five `[warning][gc] ***** WARNING! INCORRECT SYSTEM CONFIGURATION DETECTED! *****` lines on stdout at heap initialisation (`-Xmx1t` on a 1048576 limit), `-version` included; under `-Xlog` through the logger, inside the ZGC block. `=0`: nothing, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_ZGC_HEAP_TOO_LARGE_REFUSED` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 23i, host rows `x15_T1`, `x15_T1c`).* ZGC (the default collector included). A maximum heap above 16 TiB ends the launch with HotSpot 25's `Error occurred during initialization of VM` / `Java heap too large (max supported heap size is 16384G)` on stdout, exit 1, `-version` included. `=0`: such a heap boots, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_LAUNCHER_BARE_JAR_XLOG_BOOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * Every collector. A bare `-jar` with an `-Xlog` selection prints `Error: -jar requires jar file specification` on stderr, then boots the VM (its `-Xlog` startup lines on stdout) and prints the full usage on stderr, exit 1, as libjli's `ARG_CHECK` then `JavaMain` do. `=0`: both texts without a VM, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_VM_IDENTITY_HOTSPOT` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md`, host row `pl8_census_J`).* Both modes, every view (`System.getProperty`, JVMTI, the perf counters `java.property.java.vm.*` and `sun.rt.internalVersion`, `RuntimeMXBean`, `jcmd VM.version`, JDWP, JFR). `java.vm.name` is `OpenJDK 64-Bit Server VM`, `java.vm.vendor` the image's `release` `IMPLEMENTOR`, `java.vm.version` its `JAVA_RUNTIME_VERSION`, `sun.management.compiler` `HotSpot 64-Bit Tiered Compilers`, as HotSpot 25 answers for the same image. `=0`: `cratonvm`, `cratonvm`, `25.0.1+8`, `CratonVM JIT`, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_JMX_MANAGEABLE_EFFECT_FLAGS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 30h).* Every collector, both modes. The modelled HotSpot flag table carries the manageable flags whose effect this VM has -- `HeapDumpOnOutOfMemoryError`, `HeapDumpPath` (the out-of-memory dump), `PrintClassHistogram` (the `SIGQUIT` class histogram, after the `Heap` section), `ShowCodeDetailsInExceptionMessages` (the helpful NPE text), and on ZGC `HeapDumpBeforeFullGC` / `HeapDumpAfterFullGC` (no effect there, as on HotSpot) -- listed writeable and external, so `getDiagnosticOptions` lists them and `setVMOption` writes them per VM (origin `MANAGEMENT`); each consumer reads the written value. `=0`: the rows are absent, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_DEFINE_THIS_CLASS_MUTF8` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 7).* Every collector, both modes. The `this_class` name a nameless `defineClass` is adopted under (`extract_this_class_name`) is decoded as Modified UTF-8: `C0 80` is NUL and a supplementary character's surrogate pair one character, where the lenient UTF-8 decode made both U+FFFD and the adoption found no class (the retry then met `LinkageError`). A malformed name keeps the lenient decode. `=0`: the lenient decode, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_PERF_COLLECTOR_TIME_NANOS` | **on** (`=0` kill) | md` item 9).* Every collector, both modes. The perf counters `sun.gc.collector.<n>.time` (`jstat` `YGCT` / `FGCT` / `CGCT`) carry the collector bean's accumulated pause time at nanosecond precision (`JmxCollector::time_ns`), as HotSpot's tick timer accumulates it; they were its whole milliseconds, so ZGC's sub-millisecond pauses read behind HotSpot's. `getCollectionTime` is unchanged (milliseconds). `=0`: whole milliseconds, as before. |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SLIDE_NO_MOVE_DECLINE` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * A ZGC low slide whose selection provably cannot move one survivor is declined before its partition pass, its slide loop, its rewrite/screen pass over the whole live set and its `compact_low_to`; the cycle takes the empty-selection arm (free tail retracted, large-object end compacted). The proof is one header-free walk of the live set over the slide's size table: the probe moves a survivor only into a window starting on a free 8-byte grid cell below it, on selected pages, after the previous selected survivor, and the window may overlap the survivor's own bytes, so a selection is declined only when no such cell exists before any selected survivor (unsizable selected survivor, no size table, an evacuating arm: never declined). Same survivors, same layout; the relocation-set books record the empty set the cycle relocates; the free list keeps the sweep's blocks (the moved-nothing `compact_low_to` dropped those overlapping the slide window). `h7_OOM1_KV`: 62 of 65 cycles slid 893 k survivors to move nothing (2.78 s). Engagement on `[GC] zgc-cpu-skips:` (`slide_nomove_declined=`, and `slide_nomove_missed=` = moved-nothing slides the walk could not prove; under `=0` every moved-nothing slide). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_TLAB_CARVE_FUTILE_LATCH` | **on** (`=0` kill) | * After a ZGC TLAB chunk carve fails, a later carve asking no more (`want` and `need` at least the failed one's; the VM thread's refill and the heap's own per-object cell keep separate slots) is answered "no chunk" without taking the arena lock, until a collection runs, a TLAB tail rejoins the free list, a carve succeeds, or 64 such answers were given (then one real carve re-tests). The answer only routes the object to the per-object path a failed carve routes it to (`alloc_raw`, the same free list and bump); the VM's refill-failure census and wedge breaker count it as the failed refill it is. Read once per heap. Engagement on `[GC] zgc-cpu-skips:` (`carve_futile_skips=` answers, `carve_futile_latches=` latches set). |
| `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_SIZE_MEMO` | **on** (`=0` kill; acts only with `CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_SIZE_HINTS`, on a non-generational heap, and on a sweep inside the pause) | And one rename, which is the other half of the same defect class:
`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_HEADER_ZERO` → **`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_HEADER_ZERO`** and
`CRATONVM_ZGC_GEN_DEAD_RUNS` → **`CRATONVM_ZGC_SWEEP_DEAD_RUNS`**, when the
optimisations stopped being scoped to young cycles. The `GEN_` names are read by
nothing; setting one is silently a no-op, and this page named them until
.

Two flags worth knowing because they decide how
often the default collector runs:

- `CRATONVM_ZGC_ALLOC_TRIGGER` — collect once this **percent of capacity** has
 been allocated since the last cycle. **Default 0, i.e. off.** It caps the span
 a pause has to walk at `budget + live` regardless of `-Xmx`, which is the fix
 for "pause work scales with the heap flag rather than with the garbage". A
 pause target used to supply an implicit 25% floor under it; **that floor was
 withdrawn , the day it shipped** — `org.h2.test.db.TestLargeBlob`
 went from 0 GC cycles and a PASS to 34 cycles and a SIGSEGV in libc, 3 runs of
 4, on one switch. The floor is a reproducer rather than the defect (without it
 that test never collects at all, so nothing exercised the
 `FileChannelImpl.implWrite` → `DirectByteBuffer` path), but a default that
 turns a passing test into a native crash does not ship while the underlying
 bug is open. Setting the percent explicitly still works and is unaffected.
- `CRATONVM_ZGC_PAUSE_TARGET_MS` — **default 200**; `0` turns the pause-target
 budget off. `-XX:MaxGCPauseMillis=<n>` no longer reaches it (`CRATONVM_ZGC_IGNORE_MAX_GC_PAUSE_MILLIS`, default on): HotSpot's ZGC ignores
 the flag, so it must not change how often this ZGC collects. With
 `CRATONVM_ZGC_IGNORE_MAX_GC_PAUSE_MILLIS=0` the flag sets the target and wins
 over the variable, as before. The feedback loop is blind to the first cycle, so that one still
 runs to the occupancy clause's 75%. **An intended difference from HotSpot** (closing
 `zgc-zt1tp-proposal-pause-target-stands-down-on-zgc.md`): HotSpot's ZGC has no pause
 target because its pauses are sub-millisecond; this collector's whole-heap stop-the-world pauses
 are not, so the 200 ms budget stays as a latency guard with no HotSpot twin. Once a pause
 overruns it, the budget's cycles print `Allocation Rate`, which a HotSpot run of the same
 program may not show. It is revisited with the director flip
 (`zgc-zs1tp-proposal-director-stw-model.md`); the rows that would retire it are in
 internal records (T7). (The generated inventory renders
 `CRATONVM_ZGC_ALLOC_TRIGGER` as `default-on` because its row carries an
 off word; the code default is off, `zgc_alloc_trigger_percent`.)

### Targeted compaction (deleted)

`CRATONVM_ZGC_TARGETED_COMPACTION` (an allocation failure named the window the
next collection should empty, and pages in it bypassed the profitability
ranking) was deleted by: on every workload tried it engaged **zero**
times, because the windows that fail are in the large-object end, which has no
logical pages; the large-object compactor (`CRATONVM_ZGC_HIGH_COMPACTION`) is
the repair that engages. Its debug log `CRATONVM_DBG_ZGC_TARGET`, and the
opt-ins `CRATONVM_ZGC_JIT_BLANKET_REFUSAL` and
`CRATONVM_ZGC_UNREWRITABLE_PEER_REFUSES`, went in the same wave. The selector
still carries the policy fields (`ZRelocationPolicy::target_pages`,
`budget_exempts_targets` in `gc/src/zgc/forwarding.rs`) and `targeted_pages=` /
`targeted_overrun=` still print, always 0 / unchanged: nothing names a target
any more.

> **Registration, — the caveat here is discharged.** All
> new flags are declared in
> `types/src/flag_groups.rs` (`INVENTORY`, among the `zgc-*` rows marked
> `since: ""` or `""`), carry
> `CRATONVM_GC=<token>` spellings (`docs/flag-tokens.md`) and appear in
> [`docs/config/flag-inventory.md`](config/flag-inventory.md) with the correct
> opt-in/default-on rendering. The gap this block used to name
> 
> is **closed**, including the residue in which seven default-OFF booleans were
> declared with `off_word: Some("0")` and therefore rendered as *default-on*;
> that was fixed in commit `9a03106ff`.

## Sizing knobs

### Heap totals (`VmConfig`)

- `max_heap_size` — `-Xmx` equivalent. Sizes the entire managed heap; the
 backend partitions it into young/old (generational) or regions (G1). The
 **library** default (`VmConfig::default`, every embedding entry point) is
 256 MiB. The **`cratonvm` launcher** without `-Xmx` sizes it like a stock
 JDK instead: a quarter of physical RAM (of the cgroup limit, if smaller),
 capped at 4 GiB, floored at 256 MiB and never above the basis
 (`ergonomic_default_max_heap` / `clamp_ergonomic_heap`,
 `vm/src/runtime/container.rs`). `CRATONVM_DEFAULT_HEAP_MAX_MB` moves the
 cap, `CRATONVM_DEFAULT_HEAP_ERGONOMICS=0` restores the fixed 256 MiB, and
 `--verbose:gc` prints the size chosen. An embedded VM inside a cgroup that
 left the default in place gets a quarter of the cgroup limit, same cap and
 floor (`suggested_default_max_heap`); an uncontained embedder keeps 256 MiB.
- `initial_heap_size` — `-Xms` equivalent. Default 16 MiB. The bytes committed
 at startup, honoured on all three backends: G1 commits the
 prefix of its reservation, ZGC the prefix of its arena, Generational the
 request across its two young semi-spaces and, , whatever
 they cannot absorb as an old-generation prefix (the old generation reserves
 and commits on demand like the rest of the heap; before that it was
 committed whole at startup). The DEFAULT is why no diagnostic is
 raised from the heap constructor — 16 MiB arrives there on every run and is
 indistinguishable from an explicit flag, so only the launcher can tell the
 two apart.
 It is NOT the whole of `-Xms`: HotSpot's `-Xms` also sets `MinHeapSize`,
 which is `VmConfig::min_heap_size` here, and only vm-cli sets both. ZGC
 uncommits down to `min_heap_size` (`CRATONVM_ZGC_UNCOMMIT_TO_MIN_HEAP`), and
 when it is `None` that floor is HotSpot's ergonomic `min(8M, initial)`. An
 embedder (`VmConfig::default`, `libcratonvm`, `cratonvm-embed`) that wants
 the old "never uncommit below the startup commit" floor sets
 `min_heap_size = Some(initial_heap_size)` as well (from
 `zgc-zr4rv4-footprint-residuals-CLOSED.md` item 2).
- **What `Runtime.totalMemory` reports.** The heap's COMMITTED size
 (`VmHeap::committed_bytes`), on every backend — so a fresh
 `-Xms64m -Xmx512m` process reports about 64m, as on HotSpot, and
 `freeMemory` is that minus occupancy. Before that date Generational and
 ZGC reported their reservation (`-Xmx`), and a cache sized from
 `totalMemory` or `freeMemory` was sized against a heap that did not
 exist. **ZGC** preserves the startup committed-byte floor. It returns
 eligible unused granules at a collection safepoint, after forwarding records
 are retired, with `-XX:+ZUncommit` (default) and
 `-XX:ZUncommitDelay=<seconds>` (default 300). `-XX:-ZUncommit` disables
 every arena give-back path; `-XX:ZUncommitDelay=0` enables immediate
 give-back while retaining `-Xms`. The readable give-back policy retains
 committed mappings while returning resident pages, and obeys the same delay
 and enable switch. See
 internal records.
 **Generational** can now fall too, but never below `-Xms`: since
 a stop-the-world old-gen collection decommits the old
 generation's free tail (`CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK`, default ON, `=0` off;
 after a concurrent cycle only with the opt-in
 `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK_AFTER_CONCURRENT`).
 `Runtime.maxMemory` is `-Xmx` everywhere.
- **Object size: the 8-byte header** (`docs/architecture/compact-object-and-field-layout.md`). Every object starts
 with one 8-byte header word (class id and a 32-bit mark word). A **compact
 instance** has nothing else: its fields are packed at their natural widths
 from offset 8, and the object is rounded to 8 bytes with a 16-byte minimum
 (`MIN_OBJECT_SIZE`), so a node with two references and an int is 24 bytes
 instead of 32, as on HotSpot. **Arrays** keep the 16-byte header
 (`HEADER_SIZE`, `ARRAY_DATA_OFFSET`), so a large-array workload sees no
 change. **Legacy instances** — classes that are compatibility stubs, or
 inherit from one — keep the 16-byte header *and* 16-byte field cells, and
 are several times larger than their HotSpot counterparts. What this means
 for sizing: an instance-heavy live set is smaller than it was before
 , so a `-Xmx` tuned before that date has more headroom now, and
 `HeapUsedDelta`-style heap numbers are not comparable across the change.
 It is not a switch: `-XX:+UseCompactObjectHeaders` is accepted and only
 echoed back in the reported JVM flags (`VmConfig::use_compact_headers`);
 the layout is the same with or without it. Two side effects an operator may
 see: the identity hash of a compact instance has 20 significant bits, and a
 hashed compact instance inflates its monitor on the first `synchronized`.
- **`-XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=<size>`** (launcher, ; not a
 `VmConfig` field — the launcher installs it on the VM's class manager). A
 class defined from raw bytes that would push the charged class metadata
 past the limit triggers one full collection (class unloading returns dead
 loaders' charges) and, if it still does not fit, fails with
 `OutOfMemoryError: Metaspace`. That collection is not a `System.gc`
 (gc-common w7-g): the defining thread holds the class-loading lock, so it
 runs no finalizer, Cleaner or GC-notification drain there; the next GC door
 delivers them. The charge is the class-file size of every
 class not defined by the bootstrap loader, a deliberate under-estimate of
 HotSpot's metaspace use: a value that works on HotSpot is never hit
 earlier here. It is NOT the non-heap `used` JMX reports (a walk of
 everything the class store holds, every class included), and the non-heap
 `max` stays `-1`. Unset = unbounded. `-XX:MetaspaceSize` is accepted and
 ignored.
- **`-XX:+UseCompressedOops`** (alias `CRATONVM_COMPRESSED_OOPS=1`; opt-in,
 default off, Generational only — G1 and ZGC run 64-bit references and say
 so). Reference fields and array elements become 4 bytes against a window
 fixed from the heap's reserved spans at init (`HeapBased`, shift 3, base
 8 GiB below the lowest span). The width is **per process**, not per VM: in
 an embedding process the first VM decides it, and since gc-common w8-f a
 later VM that cannot run at that width — its heap outside the window, no
 flag, or an unaudited collector — is refused at creation rather than handed
 the window unchecked, while a later flag after a 64-bit VM falls back to
 64-bit references with a message (`compressed_oops::admit_heap`). Measured
 at 4.7 % of peak RSS here, not HotSpot's 20-30 %
 (`gc/src/compressed_oops.rs` module doc).

### Generational (`gc/src/gen_heap.rs`)

| Constant | Value | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| young semi-space | **`-Xmx / 4`** each | What a VM-built heap actually uses (`GenerationalHeap::with_capacity`: half the heap for the from/to pair, half for the old generation). `-Xmn`, `-XX:NewSize`, `-XX:MaxNewSize` and `-XX:NewRatio` change the split (next section). Smaller young = more frequent minor GCs; larger young = longer copy latency but more time for objects to die. The constant `DEFAULT_YOUNG_SEMI_SIZE` (64 MiB) is only `GenerationalHeap::new`'s, for tests and embedders that build a heap with no budget. |
| `YOUNG_GC_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` | **50** | Minor GC fires when from-space utilisation crosses this percentage. **Not 75** — this row said 75 ; the constant (`gc/src/gen_heap.rs`) has read 50 since the moving young gen shipped, because to-space must be able to hold every survivor. Settable at runtime, unlike its neighbours in this table: `CRATONVM_GC=young-trigger-percent=<n>`, clamped to `1..=95`. Raising it collects less often and copies more survivors per cycle; which wins is a property of the workload's survival rate. **What it actually sets** is the INITIAL threshold and the CEILING of the pause-goal loop (`CRATONVM_GC_YOUNG_PAUSE_MS`, default 200), which then moves the live value; and with the goal OFF it does not reach non-moving cycles at all (those fire at the 90 % row below). `GenerationalHeap::young_trigger_census_line` (`[GC] young_trigger:`) reports the live threshold and every loop decision — see proposal #4 of [the round's design page](feature-designs/generative-gc-architecture.md) for the sweep protocol. |
| `NON_MOVING_YOUNG_GC_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` | 90 | The **second** young trigger, and this table did not mention it. The non-moving sweep reclaims dead spans in place and needs no Cheney headroom, so a cycle that is going to be non-moving is allowed to fill 90 % of the semi-space first. Not settable. A workload that diverts to the sweep every cycle (see `docs/GC.md`'s `divert_non_moving` table — a live JIT frame does this) is therefore running at the 90 % trigger, not the 50 % one, and its young occupancy curve looks nothing like this table implies. |
| `MAX_HEAP_EXPANSION_FACTOR` | 4 | Young from-space can grow up to 4× initial when minor GC reclaims < 25 % — **but only on a heap built by `with_sizes`** (explicit arena sizes, no budget: tests and embedders). The production constructor, `with_capacity` (and `-Xmn`'s `with_capacity_and_young`), passes the initial semi as the growth ceiling because the `-Xmx` budget leaves growth nothing to take, so on a VM-built heap this row is inert. **The young generation has no shrink path**; the old generation's committed size does shrink (`CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK`, see "Heap totals"). |
| old-gen triggers | concurrent cycle at an adaptive occupancy (45 % until measured, never below 20 %); stop-the-world collection at 75 % | **Concurrent-first, default ** (`OldGenPolicy::ConcurrentFirst`, `gc/src/concurrent_mark.rs`). A concurrent old-gen mark cycle opens at an initiating occupancy *below* the 75 % stop-the-world floor, predicted from the old-gen growth one cycle was measured to see (`ConcurrentStartPolicy`); `CRATONVM_GC_CONC_START_PERCENT=<n>` (`CRATONVM_GC=conc-start-percent=<n>`, clamped to 20–75) fixes it instead. While a cycle is open the stop-the-world old-gen collection inside a young pause **defers** to it, unless it was requested (`System.gc`), an old-gen allocation failed, or the generation is 90 % full. **Growth hysteresis is ON under this policy** (flipped ): above 75 %, a repeat stop-the-world collection waits until half of what the previous one left free — at least 1/16 of the generation — has been promoted since; a failed old-gen allocation always collects; `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_TRIGGER_HYSTERESIS=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-old-trigger-hysteresis`) switches it off. `CRATONVM_GC_NO_CONCURRENT_FIRST=1` (`CRATONVM_GC=-concurrent-first`) restores the pre- legacy policy: one shared 75 % trigger, no deferral, hysteresis off unless set. `GenerationalHeap::old_gen_trigger_stats` counts `would_suppress` and `low_yield`, and `[GC] major_cadence:` splits the majors by cause. |
| `PROMOTION_AGE` | 3 | Survive this many minor GCs before being promoted to old gen. Lower (1–2) sends short-lived but slightly-tenured objects to old prematurely; higher (4–6) costs more copy work but keeps the old gen denser. **Settable ** through the HotSpot tenuring flags, which also switch on HotSpot-style *adaptive* tenuring for this VM's heap: `-XX:MaxTenuringThreshold=<0..15>` (ceiling, HotSpot default 15), `-XX:InitialTenuringThreshold=<0..15>`, `-XX:TargetSurvivorRatio=<0..100>` (default 50) and `-XX:+PrintTenuringDistribution` (prints the age table after every moving young collection). Any one of the three values engages it (`TenuringConfig`, `gc/src/gen_heap_tenuring.rs`); `CRATONVM_GC_ADAPTIVE_TENURING` engages it with HotSpot's defaults. A bad value is a `Warning:` and is ignored; under G1 or ZGC the flags print one `note:` and do nothing. Unset, the fixed age 3 applies, as before. |

The remaining constants have no setter; embedders who need
non-default sizing can construct `GenerationalHeap::with_capacity` (or
`with_sizes`) directly, then build the `VmHeap` around it. The defaults
are deliberate and cover the vast majority of workloads.

#### Young generation size: `-Xmn`, `-XX:NewSize`, `-XX:MaxNewSize`, `-XX:NewRatio`

The **Generational** collector honours HotSpot's four young
sizing flags (`gc/src/gen_young_sizing.rs`, `VmConfig::young_gen_sizing`).
Before that date all four were accepted and silently dropped, and the young
generation was always half of `-Xmx`.

HotSpot's young generation is eden plus two survivors; this collector's is the
from/to semi-space **pair**, so a young size is the pair and each semi-space
gets half of it. The old generation gets the rest of `-Xmx` — the pair plus the
old generation is always exactly `-Xmx`.

| Flag | Meaning here |
|---|---|
| none of the four | Default, unchanged: young pair = `-Xmx / 2` (each semi `-Xmx / 4`). |
| `-Xmn<size>` (also `-Xmn <size>`) | Young pair = `<size>`, exactly. Wins over the other three (a `Warning:` says so). |
| `-XX:NewRatio=<n>` | Young pair = `-Xmx / (n + 1)`. `NewRatio=1` is the default split. |
| `-XX:NewSize=<size>` | A **floor** on the NewRatio-derived (or default) pair. |
| `-XX:MaxNewSize=<size>` | A **ceiling** on it. `NewSize > MaxNewSize` raises the ceiling to `NewSize`, as HotSpot does. |

Why floor/ceiling rather than HotSpot's "start at `NewSize`, grow toward
`MaxNewSize`": this collector's young pair does not resize once built (under an
`-Xmx` budget the growth path has nothing to give — see `with_capacity`), so a
single size held between the two bounds is the honest reading.

**Clamps.** The old generation keeps at least 1 MiB (half the heap on a heap
under 2 MiB) and each semi-space is at least 1 KiB. Every clamp or override
prints one `Warning:` line at startup; `--verbose:gc` also prints the resolved
split (`[cratonvm] generational young generation: 256 MiB (-Xmn; 2 x 128 MiB
semi-spaces), old generation 768 MiB, heap 1024 MiB`).

**Other collectors.** G1 sizes its young generation from region counts and ZGC
has none; on either, passing any of the four prints one
`[cratonvm] note: … honoured by the Generational collector only` line and the
flags have no effect.

**What changes when you shrink the nursery.** The young trigger
(`YOUNG_GC_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` above) and the humongous threshold (half a semi,
capped at 256 MiB) both scale with the semi-space, so `-Xmn` smaller than the
default means more frequent minor collections AND more arrays allocated
straight into the old generation. The historical measurement against a smaller
initial semi on JIT-heavy workloads (a net regression, see `with_capacity`'s
doc) still applies: this is an operator's knob, not a recommended default.

#### Opt-in allocation experiments (default OFF)

| Variable | Effect |
|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_TLAB_FILLER_SKIP_ZERO=1` (`CRATONVM_GC=tlab-filler-skip-zero`) | Every backend: a Java thread's TLAB retire writes the tail filler's header but not the memset of its data area, which is still zero from the refill. A tail whose first word is not zero is zeroed as before. |
| `CRATONVM_TLAB_GATE_BUMP_FLOOR=1` (`CRATONVM_GC=tlab-gate-bump-floor`) | Generational only in effect: the compiled-code TLAB refill gate accepts a young bump tail smaller than the full request (down to the object being allocated) instead of spilling that allocation to the old generation. |
| `CRATONVM_TLAB_SHARE_SIZER=1` (`CRATONVM_GC=tlab-share-sizer`) | Default ON on the Generational heap since gcg g6/jn (`=0` kills it there); `=1` arms it on G1 and ZGC. HotSpot-shaped TLAB sizing: each thread asks for a fiftieth of its exponentially averaged bytes allocated per young pause (`TLABWasteTargetPercent` 1 %, `TLABAllocationWeight` 35 %), clamped to 8 KiB – 1 MiB, and a compiled allocation that misses with a tail bigger than `desired / 64` keeps the buffer and allocates outside it (the refill-waste limit). Replaces the ladder sizer when on (the ladder's two retired-buffer switches and the Generational pristine-chunk skip were removed in gce e2/o); read back on `[GC] tlab-sizer:`. Its protocol-A win on Generational (W1 610 → 63 minors) made it the Generational default for a moment in gen r5w4, **reverted** before merging: with the tail sink it hands out live memory . That page is fixed, and gcg g6/jn flipped the Generational default back on vg5 (3 interleaved pairs: park 82-88 -> 37 minors, correctness rows 5/5). |

All but the share sizer (Generational) are off until their A/B runs are taken; the protocols are in
internal records (the first two),
internal records (the next three)
and protocol A of internal records
(which measured the share sizer and the tail sink on the Generational heap;
the tail sink is in the next table now, the share sizer is the Generational default since gcg g6/jn). Every default lives in one place,
`cratonvm_types::flags::alloc_policy_defaults`: flipping an arm is that
constant (plus its row's `off_word` in `types/src/flag_groups.rs`, for the
generated flag docs).

#### Allocation kill switches (default ON)

One row below (`CRATONVM_SOFTREF_HOTSPOT_LRU`) is ON by default on the **Generational heap** (`-XX:+UseGenerationalGC`, gen r5w4, )
and, /rf, on **ZGC** (`SOFTREF_HOTSPOT_LRU_ZGC`): their default is per backend
(`alloc_policy_defaults::BackendDefault`) because its evidence was taken on
Generational alone (the share sizer used the same mechanism and was reverted). On G1 it stays off unless set to `1`; on every
backend an explicit `=0` / `=1` wins. Each constant in `types/src/flags.rs`
carries its evidence.

| Variable | Effect of `=0` |
|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GEN_TLAB_TAIL_SINK=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-gen-tlab-tail-sink`) | Generational (default ON since gen r5w2): buries a retiring TLAB's unused tail under an `int[]` filler again. ON, the tail goes back to the young from-space (the bump cursor retracts, or the tail joins the young free list) instead of being counted as occupied by the young trigger. Read back as the `young_tlab_tail_*` counters of `HeapStatsSnapshot`. |
| `CRATONVM_SOFTREF_HOTSPOT_LRU=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-softref-hotspot-lru`) | **Generational and ZGC default ON** (gen r5w4; ZGC /rf, `SOFTREF_HOTSPOT_LRU_ZGC`; G1 default OFF): the SoftReference LRU policy goes back to the wall clock now and the free space measured before the collection. ON, it takes HotSpot's `LRUMaxHeapPolicy` inputs — the soft clock and the free heap as of the end of the previous collection — so a soft reference read since the last collection is never cleared by the policy. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_XMS_USABLE_FIRST=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-gen-xms-usable-first`) | Generational (default ON since gen r5w4): splits the `-Xms` startup commit evenly across the two semi-spaces again, so `Runtime.totalMemory` starts at about `-Xms / 2`. ON, it is split the way HotSpot Serial sizes its initial heap (one survivor of copy reserve in the to-space, the rest usable): a fresh `-Xms64m -Xmx512m` heap reports about 64m. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_ZERO_ONCE=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-gen-zero-once`) | Restores the memset of every young hand-out. ON, a TLAB chunk or slow-path object carved from the young bump tail is not zeroed again: the young wipe (or the OS, for a freshly committed page) is its one zeroing point. Free-list reuse is always zeroed. Read back as `gen_zero_skipped_bytes=` / `gen_zeroed_bytes=` on `[GC] gen-alloc:`; `gen_zero_repairs=` non-zero is a finding. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_HUMONGOUS_ZERO_UNLOCKED=0` (`CRATONVM_GC=-gen-humongous-zero-unlocked`) | Zeroes a humongous array under the old-generation lock again. ON, a primitive humongous array (64 KiB or more) has its body zeroed after the lock is released; reference arrays always keep the locked memset. Read back as `gen_humongous_unlocked_zeroes=`. |

#### Old-generation switches

The `types/src/flag_groups.rs` rows carry the full reasoning; these are the
ones that change what the old generation does with memory.

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK` (`old-shrink`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Decommit the old generation's free tail after a stop-the-world old-gen collection (HotSpot's `MaxHeapFreeRatio` shape), never below its `-Xms` share. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_OOM_COMPACT` (`old-oom-compact`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Compact a fragmented old generation for the one collection after an allocation was refused for want of a contiguous block, instead of throwing a false `OutOfMemoryError`. Moving path only; a cycle with conservative roots downgrades to the in-place sweep. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_WALK_GAP_RECOVERY` (`old-walk-gap-recovery`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | An in-place sweep that meets an unwalkable gap scans it conservatively and carries on, instead of failing closed for the whole generation. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OVERHEAD_PROGRESS` (`overhead-progress`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | The mutator-progress half of the GC-overhead limit, and the full collection before its error (see "GC overhead limit" below). |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_FRAG_COMPACT` (`old-frag-compact`) | off | Proactive fragmentation-triggered compaction, before any allocation fails. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_PINNED_COMPACT` (`old-pinned-compact`) | off | Let the non-moving path answer a fragmentation request by compacting around the objects conservative roots pin. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_FRAG_PINNED_COMPACT` (`old-frag-pinned-compact`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | On a fragmented non-moving major (a pending fragmentation or humongous refusal, or `fragmentation_repair_due`), try the pinned compactor at most once every four old-gen collections; decline when pins reach 10% of marked live bytes. After the mark it slides only the cheapest pin-free window of survivors whose gaps free `FRAGMENTATION_FIT_FACTOR` times the request (the request itself when the free bytes cannot), and nothing when the in-place sweep already frees a run that holds it; free blocks that held no moved object are not zeroed. `[GC] oldgen_j3og3: ... oldsz_frag_window_compactions= oldsz_frag_window_not_needed=` under `CRATONVM_GC_STATS`. This is how a JIT-warm Generational run's old generation compacts without `System.gc` (HotSpot Serial's full collections slide; its dense prefix keeps up to `MarkSweepDeadRatio` of dead space in place). |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_GIVE_BACK` (`old-give-back`) | off | Also return large free granules after an in-place sweep, not only the tail. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK_AFTER_CONCURRENT` (`old-shrink-after-concurrent`) | off | Run the committed-size shrink after a concurrent cycle's sweep too. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_CONC_SERVICE_THREAD` (`gen-conc-service-thread`) | off | Run the concurrent old-gen cycle on a service thread instead of on the mutator whose young pause found it due. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_COMPACT_FORWARD_INDEX` (`old-compact-forward-index`) | **ON** (`=0` off; WARNING flip gcj j3 close : pinned compaction 0.80x at -Xmx1g, 0.89x at 64m, CPU 0.95-0.98x, every row SAME on j3a) | The old-gen compactors (the full-GC slide and the pinned compaction) resolve each reference slot through a sorted, block-indexed survivor table instead of a hash map with one insert per survivor and one random probe per edge. Same heap after the compaction (unit-tested); `[gcphase] old-gen compact-forward-index: count=` under `CRATONVM_DBG_GCPHASE`. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_FREE_RATIO_GROWTH` (`old-free-ratio-growth`) | on (`=0` kills) | After an old-gen collection (a stop-the-world one, or a concurrent cycle's sweep at the next young pause with no cycle open), grow the old generation so `MinHeapFreeRatio` (default 40 %) of it is free, up to HotSpot Serial's old maximum (`2/3 · -Xmx`), as HotSpot's `TenuredGeneration::compute_new_size` does; the young trigger cedes the budget. Needs `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_BORROW_YOUNG` (default ON). Without it the generation grows only after a refused allocation. Inert on the default heap, whose old generation is built at HotSpot's `Xmx - Y` already (`CRATONVM_GEN_SERIAL_YOUNG_GEOMETRY`); it acts on the semi-space pair split (`CRATONVM_GEN_SERIAL_YOUNG_GEOMETRY=0`), where `Gcj1OgFreeRatioGrowthProbe 96 400 8 4` at `-Xmx256m` takes far fewer old collections with it. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_MARK_LOADER_SNAPSHOT` (`old-mark-loader-snapshot`) | off (opt-in `=1`; gcj j2: back to opt-in, no measured gain on j1c -- `orch-notes.md`) | The old-gen mark answers each marked object's instance -> defining-loader edge from one `loader_pin` snapshot per collection instead of taking the registry's read lock and probing it once per marked object. Same answers (the rows naming this heap cannot change inside the pause). Engagement: `[gcphase] old-gen mark-loader-snapshot: count=1` per mark (`CRATONVM_DBG_GCPHASE=1`), and `OldGenSizingStats::loader_snapshot_marks` / `loader_snapshot_lookups` (0 unless `=1`). |

#### Old generation and full GC switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GC_CONC_BITMAP_GROWTH_SPAN` (`conc-bitmap-growth-span`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | A Generational concurrent cycle's mark bitmap is built over the old generation's growth limit, not its capacity at the driver's read. A growth between that read and the initial-mark pause (another thread's major) used to make the remark refuse the whole traced cycle; `[GC] conc_cycles: conccyc_bitmap_cover_refusals=` counts the refusals. The bitmap is zero-allocated, so the extra span is address space until marked. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_SHRINK_FACTOR` (`old-shrink-factor`) | off | The committed-size shrink after a stop-the-world old-gen collection releases 0, 10, 40, then 100 % of the excess over the `MaxHeapFreeRatio` target on consecutive shrinking collections (HotSpot's `ShrinkHeapInSteps` `_shrink_factor`), from the top of the committed span; a collection without excess resets it. Off: the whole excess is released unless the committed end advanced since the previous resize. Same `oldsz_shrinks` / `oldsz_shrunk_bytes` counters. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_MARK_PREFETCH` (`old-mark-prefetch`) | off | The stop-the-world old-gen mark (`old_gen_gc_inner`) moves entries off its LIFO worklist into an 8-entry ring, prefetching each header as it enters, and scans the oldest ring entry. Only the scan order changes; the same objects are marked. `[gcphase] old-gen mark-prefetched: count=` under `CRATONVM_DBG_GCPHASE`. Proposal `gcj-j1og-proposal-old-mark-stack-prefetch`. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_PAR_ADJUST` (`old-par-adjust`) | off | The full-GC sliding compaction's Phase 2 (every survivor's reference slots rewritten through the forwarding table) runs on scoped worker threads, one contiguous run of survivors each, joined before the slide. `1` / `on`: `min(cpus, 4)` workers; `n >= 2`: `n` (at most 16). Serial below 65 536 survivors. Same heap after the compaction (unit-tested over 2, 3 and 7 workers). `[gcphase] old-gen compact-par-adjust: count=1` under `CRATONVM_DBG_GCPHASE`. Proposal `gcj-j1og-proposal-parallel-full-gc-adjust-and-mark`, step 1. |

<!---->
#### Old generation compaction and major switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_PINNED_COMPACT_REUSE_CLOSE` (`old-pinned-compact-reuse-close`) | off | The pinned old-gen compaction (every requested major without a conservative root, `CRATONVM_GC_REQUESTED_MAJOR_COMPACTS`) takes its Phase 0b answer from the mark's last in-mark closure (the true-root seed's), exactly as the in-place sweep already reuses it, instead of closing the live set again. Phase 0b still runs when the compactor itself marked a pin. `[gcphase] old-gen compact-phase0-skipped: count=1` under `CRATONVM_DBG_GCPHASE`. Proposal `gcj-j1og-proposal-full-gc-drops-the-redundant-phase-0-closure`, the cheap half. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_COMPACT_TRUST_MARK` (`old-compact-trust-mark`) | off | The full-GC slide skips Phase 0 when the old mark recorded no unfollowed reference (a rejected in-range candidate, an in-extent address outside the readable span, a scan that bailed on a bad header) and nothing was marked without a push (no finalizer round, no in-mark closure). The proposal's full design; decide it with `..._VERIFY=1` first. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_COMPACT_TRUST_MARK_VERIFY` (`old-compact-trust-mark-verify`) | off | *(debug)* A compaction handed a "closed, no escape" verdict runs Phase 0 anyway, uses its answer, and counts each disagreement (`[gcphase] old-gen compact-trust-mark-disagreement: count=`, an error log line). Must never fire. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_TRIGGER_SERIAL` (`old-trigger-serial`) | off | An unrequested major runs when the old generation's free space no longer holds the padded average promotion of a young cycle (HotSpot Serial's `promotion_attempt_is_safe`, `AdaptiveSizePolicyWeight=10`, `PromotedPadding=3`), or after a refused old-gen allocation, instead of at 75 % occupancy on every young cycle. The 75 % floor stays as a ceiling for a generation that cannot grow and whose free list is fragmented for the recent requests. Proposal `gcj-j1og-proposal-serial-shaped-major-trigger`. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_CAPACITY_SHRINK` (`old-capacity-shrink`) | on (`=0` kills) | After a stop-the-world collection, an old-gen capacity that grew into the reservation's headroom (`CRATONVM_GC_OLD_BORROW_YOUNG`, `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_FREE_RATIO_GROWTH`) comes back down to where the committed resize left the generation (never below the construction capacity or `MinHeapFreeRatio`; not within one collection of a growth), and the young trigger gets back the budget the growth ceded. Blocker B2 of `gcj-j2og2-free-ratio-growth-flip-blockers`. With `CRATONVM_GC_OLD_FREE_RATIO_GROWTH=1` at `-Xmx256m` a peak-then-fall churn takes 33 young collections instead of 46-47 (the baseline's 33; HotSpot 30). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_TENURED_POOL_MAX_FIXED` (`tenured-pool-max-fixed`) | on (`=0` kills) | `Runtime.maxMemory()` and the pool maxima are HotSpot Serial's fixed figures (`-Xmx` less one aligned copy-reserve survivor; `Tenured Gen` the matching fixed tenured maximum), byte-for-byte HotSpot 25's at `-Xms16m -Xmx256m`, and they no longer move with a growth; current capacities stay lower bounds. `=0`: the old dynamic maxima (the tenured max is the current capacity). Blocker B3 of the same page. |

#### Young collection and TLAB switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_XLOG_GEN_TLAB_TOTALS_ALONE` (`xlog-gen-tlab-totals-alone`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational: a `-Xlog:gc+tlab=debug` rule alone (no `gc` rule at info) arms the collection's events, so HotSpot's `GC(n) TLAB totals:` line prints at every pause. `=0`: the line prints only when another collection tag is also logged, as before. G1 and ZGC are unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_TAKES_UNPROVEN_FRAMES` (`gen-pinned-copy-takes-unproven-frames`) | on (`=0` kills) | A JIT-warm young cycle whose coverage proof failed only for frames the young pin ledger holds (an unregistered or unpublished compiled frame, a peer the ledger accounts for, a pinned take-over window) runs the pinned in-place copy when the ledger read is complete, instead of the in-place sweep; a peer whose proof failed only for those reasons deposits its words into the ledger. Engagement: `[GC] young_pinned_unproven:` and `[GC] young_pinned_unproven_reads:` (why a read refused). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_YOUNG_COPY_TAKEOVER` (`gen-pinned-young-copy-takeover`) | on (`=0` kills) | A JIT-warm young cycle whose only divert is a take-over pause's helper windows (blocked peers with compiled frames, every window pinned from a whole band) runs the pinned in-place copy from the young pin ledger instead of the in-place sweep. Engagement: `[GC] young_pinned_takeover:` and `[GC] young_pinned_takeover_declines:`. |
| `CRATONVM_XT_FIRST_PASS_GRACE_US` (`xt-first-pass-grace-us`) | 200, **10000 on a Generational heap** (; capped at 10 ms; `0` = no grace) | How long a take-over pause waits for its mutators to arrive at a safepoint poll on their own before it freezes one still in compiled code. A frozen peer forces a Generational young cycle to sweep in place (`ptkod_frozen=`); on a box with more runnable threads than cores 4-thread javac froze a merely descheduled peer on 4 of 26 pauses at 200 us and on none at 10 ms. A typed value applies to every backend. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_ROOT_SLICE_NAMES` (`gen-pinned-copy-root-slice-names`) | on (`=0` kills) | Under the named rule, a pinned in-place young cycle seeds only the objects named by pin words the pause's root slice also holds (plus card walk-gap and blocked-peer words); a word only the young pin ledger holds still pins its page but roots nothing, as in every other collection. 4-thread javac: promotion 3.6-7.0 -> 2.2-2.7 MB a cycle. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_MINOR_PROMISE` (`gen-minor-promise`) | on (`=0` kills) | A Generational pause that cannot be proven minor only because its own promotions might make the occupancy trigger due promises to stay minor: that major is deferred to the next pause (whose decision finds it due). Lets the reference passes treat the pause as minor (below). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_PAUSE_SETS_ASIDE_OLD_ROWS` (`gen-young-pause-sets-aside-old-rows`) | on (`=0` kills) | On a minor (certain or promised) Generational pause, the weak / phantom reference rows whose `Reference` and referent are both old skip both reference passes and are put back in place afterwards; javac holds 10-17 K of them. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_PAR_CARD_SCAN` (`gc-par-card-scan`) | on (`=0` kills) | Generational moving young pause: the dirty-card walk and its slot reads run on the evacuation workers, split by contiguous groups of dirty ranges whose edges concatenate to the serial scan's, in its order. A group that meets a header it cannot size, or a refused block-offset table, sends the whole scan back to the serial walk. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_PAR_EVAC_CARD_SEED` (`par-evac-card-seed`) | on (`=0` kills) | The parallel young copy forwards the dirty-card roots on its workers, each taking an equal slice, instead of on the driver before the pool opens. Ignored under `CRATONVM_GC_FULL_RSET_SCAN`. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_SURVIVOR_RESERVE` (`gen-pinned-copy-survivor-reserve`) | off | A JIT-warm young cycle (predicted non-moving) fires early enough to leave a survivor space free in from-space -- HotSpot Serial's survivor, or 5/4 of the last pinned cycle's survivors, at most half the semi -- because the pinned in-place copy copies only into from-space's free spans and promotes the rest. Cuts premature promotion (evac: overflow promotions 11.26M -> 0.31M) at the cost of more young cycles. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_TAKES_PROMOTION_RISK` (`gen-pinned-copy-takes-promotion-risk`) | off | A JIT-warm young cycle (unrewritable conservative JIT roots) that the promotion-OOM guard alone (old and young both at least 90 % full) sent to the in-place sweep is offered to the pinned in-place copy instead, which keeps a survivor nothing can take rather than aborting. Engagement on the shutdown line `[GC] young_term4_offer:` (`t4o_promotion_risk_admitted=`). |

#### Young generation allocation and TLAB switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_KNOWN_ZERO_CARRY` (`gen-young-known-zero-carry`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational: the non-moving young sweep keeps claiming as known-zero the bytes of the previous claim that are still on the free list (merged with the spans it zeroes itself), and the TLAB tail sink returns a tail without dropping the claim. A TLAB refill or slow allocation served wholly inside the claim skips its memset. Engagement on the shutdown line `[GC] young_premium:` (`yp_kz_carried_bytes=`, `yp_kz_carry_sweeps=`); effect on `[GC] gen-alloc:` (`gen_zeroed_reuse_bytes=` falls, `gen_zero_skipped_bytes=` rises). `=0`: the claim is the last sweep's spans only, and a tail return drops it. |
| `CRATONVM_TLAB_HOTSPOT_WASTE_LIMIT` (`tlab-hotspot-waste-limit`) | off | Generational, share sizer on: HotSpot's refill-waste limit -- re-armed to `desired / TLABRefillWasteFraction` at every refill, raised by `-XX:TLABWasteIncrement` words (default 4) per kept miss -- instead of this VM's `desired / TLABRefillWasteFraction` raise. G1 and ZGC unchanged. Ignored under `CRATONVM_TLAB_HOTSPOT_FLAGS=0`. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_SERIAL_EDEN_NO_PAUSE_GOAL` (`gen-serial-eden-no-pause-goal`) | **ON** (=0 off) | A Generational heap the Serial-shaped young trigger (`CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_TRIGGER_SERIAL_EDEN`) armed runs no young pause goal (HotSpot Serial has none), unless `CRATONVM_GC_YOUNG_PAUSE_MS` is set explicitly. `[GC] young_trigger:` then reads `young_pause_goal_ms=0` and `adapt_halvings=0`. |

#### Young generation promotion and mirror switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_MIRROR_ROOT_WHEN_PROMOTABLE` (`gen-young-mirror-root-when-promotable`) | off (opt-in `=1`; back to opt-in : it keeps dead loaders from unloading on the conc-unload rows) | Generational: a root scan that publishes its precise-table counts (`CRATONVM_GEN_PRECISE_ROOT_PROMOTE`, default ON) roots a YOUNG user-loader class mirror from the class-mirror table instead of deferring it under `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_MIRROR_DEFER`, so the scan no longer forces the in-place young sweep (term 3) to keep the deferral's promise: a JIT-warm young cycle can reach the pinned copy, and the mirror is promoted by the copy or by precise-root promotion in a sweep. `[GC] conc_y2o:` `young_mirrors_deferred=` stays 0 on such scans. `=0`: the young mirror is deferred and the cycle swept in place, as before. A dead young loader whose young mirror is its only keeper unloads after the mirror's promotion (HotSpot's young collection also roots every loader's mirrors). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_MIRROR_DEFER_MOVING_FOLLOWS` (`gen-young-mirror-defer-moving-follows`) | off | Generational, with `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_MIRROR_DEFER` on (the default): a root scan that deferred young user-loader class mirrors to their loaders no longer forces the in-place young sweep (term 3) to keep that promise; the moving young collection (the copy and the pinned in-place copy) follows the loaders' `mirror_pin` rows itself -- an old loader's always, a young loader's once the collection keeps it -- as the opt-in moving `System.gc` does. A dead young loader's young mirror is still reached by nothing and is reclaimed. JIT-warm young cycles can then reach the pinned copy (`[GC] decision histogram:` `moving-pinned-pages=`); `young_mirrors_deferred=` on `[GC] conc_y2o:` still counts the deferrals. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_TYPED_MIN_TLAB_FLOOR` (`gen-typed-min-tlab-floor`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational: the smallest-whole TLAB refill's floor and the TLAB tail sink's smallest listed tail are the VM's `-XX:MinTLABSize` (as the refill request already is), not the 8 KiB constant. Nothing typed: 8 KiB, unchanged. `=0`: the constant. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_TLAB_RAISE_BY_GRANTED_BYTES` (`gen-tlab-raise-by-granted-bytes`) | off | Generational, share sizer on: a refill window doubles a thread's desired TLAB size when its GRANTED bytes reach `2 x target_refills x desired` (the same threshold in bytes), not after `2 x target_refills` refills, so a window of short grants (whole blocks below the request, fragmentation mini-TLABs) no longer raises toward 1 MiB. `[GC] tlab-sizer:` `sizer_raises=` falls on such runs. G1 and ZGC unchanged. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_CHENEY_OVERFLOW_PROMOTES` (`gen-cheney-overflow-promotes`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational: the serial (Cheney) moving young copy tenures a survivor that young to-space cannot take, as the parallel copy already does and as HotSpot's young collection does on a survivor overflow, instead of aborting the process. Unreachable while to-space can hold all of from-space (every configuration today); `cheney_overflow_promotions=` on `[GC] par_evac:` counts it. `=0`: the abort. |

#### Pinning and conservative roots

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_SEEDS_NEVER_DROPPED` (`gen-pinned-seeds-never-dropped`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | A pinned in-place young cycle whose plan names its seeds (the default watched / finalizable filters, or `_EXACT_ROOTS`) never reclaims a seed whose forward was refused before the pinned arm (a suspect header); it stays in place. `=0` reclaims it, as before. Count: `psk_kept_sum=` on `[GC] pinned_exact_roots:` -- seeds kept by this switch alone (unvisited, not refused); 0 under `=0` and on a healthy run. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_PIN_SHORT_SWEEP_INCOMPLETE` (`gen-young-pin-short-sweep-incomplete`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | A young-pin deposit that licenses moves but cannot sweep to its stack top (top unknown: a foreign stack pointer; or no band at all) marks the pause's ledger incomplete, so the cycle declines to the sweep (`nonmoving-pin-ledger-incomplete`) instead of moving under unswept words. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_NAMED_ROOTS` (`gen-pinned-copy-named-roots`) | **ON** (=0 off) | The pinned in-place young cycle roots only the objects a pin word names (the containing object, an array ending at a start word, arrays within the 256-byte slack), not every object on the pinned page; the rest stay in place only if reached. Narrower than `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_EXACT_ROOTS`, and wins over it. Engagement: `[GC] pinned_exact_roots:`. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_CONSERVATIVE_LOCALS_SKIP_PRIMITIVE_KINDS` (`gen-conservative-locals-skip-primitive-kinds`) | on (`=0` kills) | Generational only: the conservative interpreter-locals probe stops treating a `long` / `double` local whose value equals an object address as a reference (a minted JNI handle still roots). Less retention, fewer promotion pins; HotSpot never retains through a primitive.: the operand-stack probe follows the same rule (`long` / `double` kind slots). |

#### Advanced pinning and root scans

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_REFUSED_FORWARD_KEPT` (`gen-pinned-refused-forward-kept`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | A pinned in-place young cycle whose plan names its seeds keeps a pinned object whose forward was refused (a suspect header, a parallel worker's refusal): something reached it, so it is not reclaimed. `=0` reclaims it as in gcj j1. Count: `prk_kept_sum=` on `[GC] pinned_exact_roots:` (0 on a healthy run). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_WATCHED_REGISTER_WORDS_ROOTED` (`gen-pinned-watched-register-words-rooted`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational only, inside `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_WATCHED_START_NEEDS_ROOT`: a young pin-ledger word from a register image (a deposit's callee-saved registers, a peer's captured register file) counts as rooted when it names a watched object's start, so the pinned in-place cycle never drops a `Reference`, queue or referent a live register names. Stack words keep the i35 rule. `=0` tests register words like every other ledger word. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_WATCHED_START_LAYOUT_FREE_ROOTED` (`gen-pinned-watched-start-layout-free-rooted`) | off | Generational only, inside `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_WATCHED_START_NEEDS_ROOT`: the wider form of the row above -- every young pin-ledger word that did not come from a compiled frame's band alone (a layout-free band such as a Rust frame, a register image, a peer register word, a blocked-peer band) counts as rooted when it names a watched object's start. Opt-in: it roots stale Rust-frame words too and keeps `WeakClearYoungProbe`'s `inline` referent alive (4/4 runs; HotSpot clears it). Off, only the register words (previous row) are rooted. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_PINNED_COPY_OBJECT_WINDOWS` (`gen-pinned-copy-object-windows`) | off | The pinned in-place young cycle keeps in place only the objects overlapping a pin word's window (64 bytes below, 256 above), not every object on its 4 KiB page; the page's other objects are copied when reached and freed otherwise, and its free bytes outside the windows take copies. The 1/8 page bound and `pycopy_pages_*` still count pages. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_BAND_DEAD_SPILL_BOUNDED` (`gen-band-dead-spill-bounded`) | **ON** (=0 off) | Generational only: the moving-young coverage verifier's "dead operand spill above the live cursor" excuse covers only operand-spill words, not the typed ranges, the locals tail or offsets past the spill area. More words are checked for shadow publication, so more JIT-warm cycles can be refused a moving copy. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_CLAIMANT_SKIPS_UNMOUNTED` (`gen-claimant-skips-unmounted`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational only: a parked (unmounted) virtual thread no longer claims its last carrier's OS thread in the blocked-peer stack-capture fold, so a blocked carrier adopts its own captured words and its wake rewrites them after a moving young cycle. `=0` restores the gen r5w1 rule (the newest publish wins). |
| `CRATONVM_BLOCKED_NATIVE_FIXUP_WAKE_FLOOR` (`blocked-native-fixup-wake-floor`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Every collector: the blocked-peer native-stack write-back at a thread's wake refuses a captured word that lies below the wake path's own entry (a frame the wake pushed, which did not exist when the word was captured); the word's value check alone used to protect it. Refusals count as `below_wake=` on `[GC] blocked_peer_stack_remap:`. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_PIN_FROM_SPACE_SCREEN` (`gen-young-pin-from-space-screen`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational only: the young pin ledger's words are screened to from-space as allocated (`[base - 256, top]`) before option B decides "empty" and before the pinned copy charges its census and 1/8 bound; to-space words and words above the bump top name nothing a cycle moves. Dropped words: `cjdiv_ledger_screened_out_sum=`. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_YOUNG_PIN_SKIPS_COLLECTOR_FRAMES` (`gen-young-pin-skips-collector-frames`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Generational only: the collecting thread's young pin deposit does not sweep the pause door's own frames (`run_collection_pause` and below; they hold the arenas' bases, ends and tops) and reads the callee-saved registers captured at the door instead of its own. Engagement: `cjdiv_ledger_collector_cuts=`. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_SAFEPOINT_PUBLISH_SKIPS_BAND_SETS` (`gc-safepoint-publish-skips-band-sets`) | **ON** (`=0` off) | Every collector: the safepoint root publish no longer inserts band words into the thread-local movable / unrewritable sets, which only the thread's own collection reads, after clearing them. No root, pin or decision changes. |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_INTERP_LOCALS_TYPE_MAP` (`gen-interp-locals-type-map`) | on (`=0` kills) | Generational only: an interpreter frame's reference-typed locals at its pc (the bytecode type map HotSpot's interpreter uses) decide the conservative locals probe (a local typed `int` / `float` / conflict is not probed; a minted JNI handle still roots) and the remap of a local that lost its tag (not rewritten unless typed a reference). The precise tag scan is unchanged. |

#### JNI switches

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_JNI_FOREIGN_ALLOC_LADDER` (`jni-foreign-alloc-ladder`) | **ON** (`=0` kills) | Every collector. A refused allocation on a counted foreign-attached JNI thread runs the native reclaim ladder. Indirect locals are remapped; raw entries in every open local frame are published as conservative pins. Attachments always have a base local frame, including with foreign transitions disabled. Generational majors reclaim in place while raw attachments are alive. Idle blocked threads cannot initiate collections. Acceptance: `tools/probes/run-jni-allocation-retirement.py`. |
| `CRATONVM_GC_DEDUPE_INITIATOR_SNAPSHOT` (`dedupe-initiator-snapshot`) | on (Generational; `=0` kills; gcj j2 flip), off elsewhere | The collection door's registry gather leaves out the initiator thread's own root snapshot, which the door already scanned (; `docs/GC.md`). |
| `CRATONVM_STW_UNDISCOVERED_REFS_STRONG` (`stw-undiscovered-refs-strong`) | on (Generational: gcj j2 flip; ZGC: zgc wr flip; `=0` kills), off on G1 | A stop-the-world pause keeps the referent of a `Reference` only a dead finalizable reaches, as HotSpot Serial and ZGC do (; `docs/GC.md`). |
| `CRATONVM_GEN_TLAB_REFILL_SMALLEST_WHOLE_BLOCK` (`gen-tlab-refill-smallest-whole-block`) | on (`=0` kills; gcj j2 flip) | A young TLAB refill takes the smallest whole free block that makes a useful TLAB before the default carve . hashmap-pair 10x faster on j1; alloc-ring costs 1.14x (lane yg2). |
| `CRATONVM_JNI_ARRAY_VM_LIMIT` (`jni-array-vm-limit`) | **ON** (`=0` kill) | Every collector. A JNI `NewObjectArray` / `New<Type>Array` longer than HotSpot's array limit (`Integer.MAX_VALUE - 2`, every element type) leaves `OutOfMemoryError: Requested array size exceeds VM limit` pending at once, as HotSpot's `check_array_allocation_length` does, instead of running the native reclaim ladder (a forced young collection and two majors) and reporting `Java heap space`. The interpreter, the JIT helpers and the VM's own native array doors already refused such a length first. Rows: `Gcj1JnArrayLimitProbe`; oversized JNI String backing arrays use the same refusal before decoding or collection. |

`CRATONVM_JNI_IN_NATIVE_BAND_NARROW` and `CRATONVM_JNI_IN_NATIVE_PROVEN_DEPTH_CREDIT` are read once per VM
instead of once per JNI call. The narrowing is default ON since the gck flip ( `=0` kills); the
credit is still opt-in.

#### JNI transitions and native frames

<!---->
| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CRATONVM_JNI_SWITCHES_ONCE_PER_CALL` (`jni-switches-once-per-call`) | off (opt-in) | Every backend; cost only, no behaviour change. A JNI native dispatch resolves the VM's JNI switches once and passes them to the receiver / argument local recording, the band check and the in-native entry; the entry and the leave read the VM they hold. Off, each of those sites resolves them again through the thread's JNI context (about seven TLS borrows and struct copies per call). Deciding rows: block C2 of internal records (interleaved CPU A/B). |
| `CRATONVM_JNI_IN_NATIVE_KEEP_TLAB` (`jni-in-native-keep-tlab`) | off (opt-in) | Generational only, with the JNI in-native package (default ON); not with `CRATONVM_JNI_IN_NATIVE_LOCKFREE_LEAVE`. A JNI call going into native keeps its TLAB instead of retiring it (and re-carving one on its next allocation); a collecting pause retires every kept TLAB on its in-native owner's behalf before it collects, as HotSpot retires every TLAB at the safepoint. Engagement: `tlab_kept`, `tlab_kept_reused`, `tlab_kept_retired_by_pause` on the `CRATONVM_DBG_JNI_PHASE=1` line. Deciding rows: block T of the same report. |
| `CRATONVM_JNI_VIRTUAL_UPCALL_SELECTS` (`jni-virtual-upcall-selects`) | on (Generational; `=0` kills; gcj j3 flip) | Generational only (the round's gate; the fix is collector-independent). A JNI `Call<Type>Method*` / `Call<Type>MethodA/V` up-call selects its target against the method ID, as HotSpot's `jni_invoke_nonstatic` does: a private method ID runs as resolved, and a private or cross-package package-private method of the receiver's class does not hide the method ID's override. Off, the first same-signature declaration on the receiver's class chain runs (a private subclass method hides the method ID). Rows: `Gcj2Jn2UpcallSelectProbe`, block U of the same report. |
| `CRATONVM_JNI_NEW_STRING_HOTSPOT_EDGES` (`jni-new-string-hotspot-edges`) | **ON** (`=0` kill) | Every collector. JNI `NewString(p, 0)` answers `""` whatever `p` (HotSpot reads no unit, so `NewString(NULL, 0)` is the empty String), and `NewString(p, len)` with `len < 0` leaves `NegativeArraySizeException: <len>` pending, as HotSpot's byte-array allocation does. `=0`: `NULL` with nothing pending for both, as before. Rows: `Gcj2Jn2NewStringEdgeProbe`, block S of the same report. |

The NB refusal counters (; `dep_band_narrow_no_chain`, `..._truncated`, `..._jit_above`,
`..._spill_outside` on the same line) change no behaviour: they say why `CRATONVM_JNI_IN_NATIVE_BAND_NARROW`
keeps a band whole.

#### Generational observability and launcher

<!---- begin -->
| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
